Evidence: line 633 asserts "`role="radiogroup"` is implicit here"; HTML-AAM maps `fieldset` → `group`, and `aria-invalid`/`aria-required` are supported on `radiogroup`, not on `group`. The inner `<div className="fsh-radios">` (line 648) carries no role either. `kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:578-583` sets the same two attributes on MUI's `RadioGroup`, which does render `role="radiogroup"`. Failure scenario: a required radio group fails submit → sighted users see red text, a screen-reader user is told nothing about the invalid or required state, and the same document announces correctly under kit-mui. This kit is the wrong one.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:700` (and `:661`) — an unanswered group is indistinguishable from an answer of `''`, so any option or `RadioItem` whose value is the empty string renders pre-checked. Evidence: `group.selected` is `jsonToString(value)` (line 646) which returns `''` for `undefined`; `RadioItemNode`'s `value` is `str(props['value'])` = `''` for the shipped default (`definitions.ts:499` `defaultProps: { label: 'Option', value: '' }`); `checked={group ? group.selected === value : false}` is then `'' === ''` → `true`. Failure scenario: drop a `RadioItem` into a `RadioGroup` and don't set its value — it renders selected while `FormData` holds nothing, so the user submits and gets "This field is required" on a field that visibly has a selection. Two such items both render `checked` under one `name`, and the DOM silently keeps only the last.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:795-801` — `maxSize` filters the *reported names* but never the input, and reports nothing to the user, so an oversized file is "refused" only inside `FormData`. Evidence: the handler filters `e.target.files` into a name array and calls `onChange`, but never clears `e.target.value` and never sets an error; `filesHeld` (line 760) is derived from `value`, so it shows nothing. Failure scenario: `maxSize: 100`, `multiple: false`, user picks a 5 MB file → the browser's own control displays `bigfile.pdf`, `onChange(null)` is called, no message appears anywhere, and the host reading `input.files` on submit still uploads the 5 MB file while the document says no file was chosen. The comment at 787-794 ("A refusal, not a truncation") and the panel hint (`definitions.ts:542`, "Files larger than this are refused as they are chosen") both describe behaviour the code does not have. Present in `kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:722-743` too, so it is a shared bug rather than a divergence.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:89, 102, 405-411, 423-427, 463, 499-502` — every layout/display prop is written as an inline `style` attribute, which unconditionally beats the per-component stylesheet, so the Style tab silently cannot override those properties in this kit while it can in kit-mui. Evidence: `renderer/src/index.tsx:620` emits authored CSS as one `<style>` element and `core/src/css.ts:177-180` builds selectors of the form `[data-fs-scope="…"][data-fs-key="…"]` — a stylesheet rule, which loses to an inline declaration; kit-mui expresses the same values through `sx` (`kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:373-383, 394-398, 472`), which compiles to a class and therefore loses to that (0,2,0) selector. Failure scenario: an author sets `background: #eee` on a `Box` in the Style tab → the rule is in the emitted sheet, the Box keeps its authored `background` prop, nothing reports a conflict; the same document under kit-mui applies the Style-tab colour. Either kit-html must move these to CSS custom properties, or the contract must state that inline style wins.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:803` — `<p className="fsh-field__hint">` names a class that has no rule anywhere in `kit-html.css` (grep over the file returns only this line), so the "Holding …" line renders with the UA's default `<p>` margin and 16px body text inside a `gap: 4px` flex column, unlike every other message in the kit. It is also not referenced by any `aria-describedby`, so what the form is about to submit is announced to nobody.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:908` — `BreadcrumbsNode` reads `props['label']` for the landmark name, but `Breadcrumbs` declares no `label` `PropSchema` and no such `defaultProps` entry (`definitions.ts:630-645`), and `docs/spec/03-components.md:176` lists only `items`. Evidence: the diff of the two kits' `definitions.ts` shows no divergence past `Repeater`, so both declare `items` alone. Failure scenario: the comment at 905-907 claims the name was made translatable, but the properties panel is built from `PropSchema[]` and never offers it, so `str(props['label'], 'Breadcrumb')` always returns the English fallback unless someone hand-edits the node in the JSON dialog. kit-mui hardcodes `aria-label="Breadcrumb"` (`kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:817`), so the two kits also disagree — the contract does not say who is right.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:216-218` — `Field` renders `<label htmlFor={id}>{label}</label>` with `str(props['label'])`, whose fallback is `''`, so clearing the Label property produces an empty label element and a control with no accessible name. Evidence: `str` (line 44) is documented as existing precisely because "an emptied field *is* a string", and it is given a real fallback for `Button` (line 394), `Link` (883), `Progress` (849) and `Breadcrumbs` (908) — but not for the one place WCAG 3.3.2 actually requires text. `docs/spec/02-kit-contract.md:130` notes no test checks label association, so nothing catches it. Same in kit-mui.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:702` — `RadioItemNode` hands its value straight to `onSelect` as a string, bypassing `optionValue`, while the `options` path at line 663 routes through `optionValue(options, v)` to preserve the declared type. Failure scenario: a `RadioGroup` with `options: [{label:'A', value: 1}]` plus a `RadioItem` with `value: '1'` submits the number `1` from one control and the string `'1'` from the other for the same `dataKey`, and both render `checked` at once because `jsonToString(1) === '1'`.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:570, 637` — `SwitchNode` and `RadioGroupNode` never add the `is-invalid` class that `Field` (line 215) and `CheckboxNode` (line 304) add, so their only visual error signal is the red message text; neither has an `.fsh-input` for `.fsh-field.is-invalid .fsh-input` (`kit-html.css:148`) to colour anyway.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:141, 545-546` — `role="group"` on the `Repeater`'s inner wrapper and on `ButtonGroup` with no accessible name; an unnamed group adds a boundary a screen reader announces as nothing useful. Same in kit-mui.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:109-118` — the comment block explaining what a `Step` is sits directly above `function Repeater` (line 119); `function Step` at line 159 has none. Copy-paste drift shared with `kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:140-150`.

## packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts:53, 66, 247, 269` — `gap` means CSS pixels in this kit and MUI spacing multiples in the other, so the cross-kit round trip the file header promises produces an 8× layout blowout. Evidence: this kit ships `gap: 16` for `Screen`/`Stack`/`Step`/`Repeater` and renders it as a px value (`renderers.tsx:89, 102, 143, 164`); kit-mui ships `gap: 2` for the same four and passes it to MUI `Stack gap` (`kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:126, 134, 154, 183`), with its own comment at `kit-mui/src/definitions.ts:244` recording "16 here would be 128px". Failure scenario: author a form in the playground under the HTML kit, switch kits at runtime — which `docs/spec/02-kit-contract.md:142-143` says is supported on the same document — and every gap becomes 128px. Note the unit is not even consistent inside kit-mui: `Grid.gap` is `px(gap)` there with a default of `16`. The contract is under-specified — it never says what a length prop's unit is — and the fix belongs there, not in one kit.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts:86` — `Heading` defaults to `level: 'h2'` while kit-mui defaults to `level: 'h5'`, and the two renderers use different fallbacks for an absent level (`'h2'` at `renderers.tsx:186`, `'h5'` at `kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:190`). A document dropped in one kit and rendered in the other gets a different heading rank, which changes the document outline a screen reader reads, not just the size.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts:630-645` — `Breadcrumbs` declares only `items`, so the localizable landmark name `renderers.tsx:908` reads is unreachable from the properties panel. Either add the `PropSchema` or drop the read.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts:542` — the `maxSize` hint states "Files larger than this are refused as they are chosen", which the renderer does not do; see the FileUpload finding above. A hint that promises enforcement is worse than the earlier one the comment says it replaced, which at least admitted the control did nothing.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts:499` — `RadioItem`'s `defaultProps: { value: '' }` is what makes a freshly dropped item render pre-checked (see `renderers.tsx:700`). Any non-empty default — or a `checked` comparison that distinguishes `undefined` from `''` — removes the case.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts:383` — the `Image.src` hint says "http, https, or a path. Anything else is refused", but the sink is `safeHref` (`core/src/json.ts:101-145`), which also admits `mailto:` and `tel:`. Harmless in an `<img src>`, but the hint claims a narrower allowlist than the code enforces, on the one property whose comment says the wording was unified with `Link.href` precisely so the rule reads as a property of addresses.

## packages/kit-html/src/index.ts

No issues found.

## packages/kit-html/src/kit-html.css

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/kit-html.css:457-464` — the progress track is invisible in the light palette: `.fsh-progress { background: var(--fsh-field) }` where `--fsh-field: #ffffff` (line 18) is byte-identical to `--fsh-surface: #ffffff` (line 17), which is what `.fsh-screen` paints (line 48), and the element has no border. Evidence: `test/contrast.test.ts:212-250` asserts nine token pairs and never `--fsh-field` against `--fsh-surface`, so nothing catches it; `.fsh-input` survives the same collision only because it has `border: 1px solid var(--fsh-border)` (line 139). Failure scenario: a determinate `Progress` at 30% renders as a floating 30%-wide accent bar with no visible extent to measure it against, and an indeterminate one sweeps a bar across nothing — failing WCAG 1.4.11 for the component's boundary. Dark mode is fine (`#1e2127` vs `#16181d`).
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/kit-html.css:376, 388, 490-502` — the `prefers-reduced-motion` block covers only `.fsh-progress__fill--sweeping`; the switch's `transition: background 120ms` and the knob's `transition: translate 120ms` are untouched. `docs/accessibility.md:117-118` states "The HTML kit's stylesheet has a `prefers-reduced-motion` block for its own transitions", which is true of one of the three transitions in the file. Doc-vs-code drift on a file whose stated purpose is that the accessibility claims are verifiable.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/kit-html/src/kit-html.css:361-397` — the switch has no `forced-colors` fallback and its entire state signal is a background colour. Evidence: the real checkbox is `opacity: 0` (line 366), so there is no native indicator to fall back on, and checked-vs-unchecked is `background: var(--fsh-accent)` (line 392) vs `var(--fsh-border)` (line 375) on `.fsh-switch__track`; Windows high-contrast/forced-colors overrides author `background-color` with system colours, collapsing both states to the same paint. The file has no `@media (forced-colors: active)` block anywhere, and the comment at lines 216-221 shows the author is already aware Windows high contrast renders things differently. Failure scenario: a high-contrast user cannot tell an on switch from an off one. `docs/accessibility.md` does not list this among its known gaps.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/kit-html.css:504-508` — `.fsh-progress--circular` only rounds the box; `.fsh-progress__fill` (466-472) is still sized on the inline axis, so a determinate circular progress draws a partial-width ellipse rather than an arc, and the indeterminate one translates a 40%-wide bar through a 28px circle. kit-mui renders a real `CircularProgress` ring (`kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:777`), so the same `variant: 'circular'` document looks like two different components.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/kit-html.css:62-74` and `:77-89` — the eleven dark tokens are duplicated verbatim in two blocks with no shared source. A token added to one and not the other diverges silently for exactly one of the two ways a viewer can be in dark mode (`prefers-color-scheme` vs explicit `data-fsh-theme="dark"`), and `test/contrast.test.ts:189-201` reads both selectors rather than asserting they agree. The header comment at lines 23-30 records this class of mistake already having happened once with `--fsh-success`/`--fsh-warning`.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/kit-html/src/kit-html.css:430` — `.fsh-alert`'s `border-inline-start: 4px solid var(--fsh-muted)` is dead: `AlertNode` narrows `severity` through `oneOf` (`renderers.tsx:810-814`) and always emits one of the four `.fsh-alert--*` classes, each of which overrides it.
- **[NOTE]** **[E3]** `packages/kit-html/src/kit-html.css:163-165` — `.fsh-field__message:empty { display: none }` collapses the message line, so the form shifts vertically the moment an error appears. kit-mui deliberately does the opposite, reserving the line with a non-breaking space (`kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:107-116`, "it reserves the line so nothing shifts when an error appears"). Two kits, two opposite decisions about the same contract obligation, neither recorded as a choice.

## Area 6 — designer: the shell, the store and the left pane

## packages/designer/src/Designer.tsx

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/Designer.tsx:219-232` — The shell's focus-steal guard exempts `input, textarea, select, button` unconditionally, but on the canvas those elements are deliberately prevented from taking focus, so selecting a field component leaves the shell unfocused and every shell shortcut dead. Evidence: the guard returns early when `target.closest('input, textarea, select, button, a[href], [tabindex]:not([tabindex="-1"])')` matches, on the stated rationale that "anything that takes focus itself keeps it"; `Canvas.tsx:321-325` (`onMouseDownCapture`) calls `event.preventDefault()` on every non-chrome mousedown precisely so the kit's control does *not* take focus, and `onPointerDownCapture` (`Canvas.tsx:327-343`) does not stop propagation, so the shell handler does run and does return early. The keydown listener is attached to the shell element itself (`Designer.tsx:609-614`), not to `window`/`document`. Failure scenario: fresh mount (`document.activeElement === document.body`) → click the email `<input>` of a Text field on the canvas → `onClickCapture` selects it and the frame is drawn → pointerdown target is the `<input>`, so the shell does not focus, and mousedown's `preventDefault` stops the input focusing → press `Delete`, `Backspace`, `Ctrl+Z` or `Escape`: the event path is `body → html → document`, which does not contain `.fs-shell`, so `onKeyDown` never fires and nothing happens. Clicking canvas *background* first (target matches nothing in the selector) focuses the shell and masks the bug, which is why the e2e keyboard suite — which only ever does `designer.row('…').focus()` (`e2e/keyboard.spec.ts:19,39,96,153`) — never sees it.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/Designer.tsx:579-586` — `Backspace` deletes the selected component whenever focus is on any chrome button, which is an ordinary tab stop and the state left behind by clicking a toolbar control. Evidence: the `typing` guard (`:546-549`) only excludes `INPUT`/`TEXTAREA`/`SELECT`/`contenteditable`, and the dialog guard (`:564`) only excludes `dialog[open]`; a `<button>` passes both. Failure scenario: select a component, click Undo (the Undo button now holds focus), press `Backspace` expecting nothing / browser-back → `removeComponent(selectedKey)` fires and the component is deleted with only the live region saying so.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/Designer.tsx:99-101` — `onChange` fires once at mount with the unmodified `initialDocument`, which `docs/spec/12-api.md` describes as firing "on every change". Evidence: `useEffect(() => { emit.current?.(api.document) }, [api.document])` runs on the mount pass, when `api.document === initialDocument`. Failure scenario: a host that marks the form dirty in `onChange` shows unsaved changes on a form nobody has touched.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/Designer.tsx:545` — `event.target as HTMLElement | null` is an unsound assertion (a keydown target can be any `EventTarget`); it is harmless today only because `tagName`/`isContentEditable` are read with optional chaining, so the cast buys nothing the guard does not already do.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/Designer.tsx:434` — `<h1>` inside an embeddable component: a host that already has a page `<h1>` gets two, and the designer cannot know its own heading level.

## packages/designer/src/store.ts

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:223-235` — `writeNode` compares the edited node with `JSON.stringify` on both sides, and a `ComponentNode` carries its `children`, so editing a *container* serialises its entire subtree twice on every keystroke — the opposite of what the comment above it claims. Evidence: `ComponentNode.children?: ComponentNode[]` (`packages/core/src/types.ts:158-162`) is part of the object `updateNode` hands the updater, and the comment at `:218-221` asserts "Compared as JSON, on one node… a node is small, so this costs a few microseconds on the edit path". Failure scenario: select the root `Screen` in the tree, open the Style tab, type a CSS declaration — every character runs `setNodeCss` → `writeNode` → two full-document `JSON.stringify` passes over a 200-node form, per keystroke.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:297-305` — The "checked once" document validation re-runs `validateDocument` over the whole tree and re-emits the `console.warn` on every host render when `initialDocument` is not referentially stable. Evidence: the dependency array is `[initial]` (identity), while the trailing comment states "Once, for the document this hook was mounted with"; `Designer` passes the prop straight through (`Designer.tsx:80`). Failure scenario: a host writes `<Designer initialDocument={{...doc}} …/>` (a fresh literal, which is what the prop's own "read once at mount" documentation encourages a host to feel safe doing) and every parent re-render walks the document and prints the same warning again.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:437-493` — `renameKey` repoints only the renamed node's own `renderWhen`, so every *other* reference to the old name in the document silently breaks. Evidence: the only repoint is `repointBinding(node.renderWhen, key, trimmed)` on the node being renamed; nothing scans the rest of the tree, and `dataKeyOf` (`packages/core/src/tree.ts:435-437`) falls back to `node.key`, so a node with no `dataKey` override changes the name its answer is stored under as a side effect of the rename. Failure scenario: field `email` (no `dataKey`), plus a Paragraph whose `renderWhen` is `{kind:'ref', …'email'} == 'x'`; rename `email` → `emailAddress`; the rename is accepted, the condition still names `email`, and the Paragraph is now permanently hidden with nothing reporting why — the exact failure mode the comment at `:469-476` says the fix was for, solved only from the node's own side.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:316-338` — `addComponent` decides against `latest.current` but commits against `h`, so it writes a document built on a base that is not necessarily `h.present` and can silently discard a concurrent edit. Evidence: `const doc = latest.current` at `:323`, then `setHistory((h) => commit(h, { ...doc, root }))` at `:336` — `h.present` is never consulted, unlike every other writer, which goes through `apply` (`:312-314`) and derives from `h.present`. `latest.current` is only refreshed in an effect (`:308-310`), so it is one committed render behind by construction. Failure scenario: two `addComponent` calls land in one batch (a drop commit plus a palette click, or any host calling the exported `useDesigner` API twice in a handler) → the second commits `{...staleDoc, root}` and the first component is gone from the document while its key may still be the selection.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:533-536` — `duplicateComponent` allocates a new document object unconditionally, defeating the identity check every other writer in this file guards. Evidence: `apply((doc) => ({ ...doc, root: insertNode(...) }))` with no `root === doc.root` test, while `moveComponent` (`:503`), `removeComponent` (`:512-513`) and `addComponent` (`:334`) all have one, each with a comment explaining why. `insertNode` returns the tree unchanged when `parentKey` is not found (`packages/core/src/tree.ts:353` via `updateNode`'s no-match path). Failure scenario: the parent read from `history.present` is no longer in `h.present` → `insertNode` matches nothing → a fresh-but-identical document takes a slot in a capped 100-entry stack, pushing a real edit off the far end.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:231-233` — `JSON.stringify` inside a state updater throws on a document that is not plain JSON, taking the designer and its whole undo history down — the same failure the `setTranslation` comment at `:388-395` was written to prevent. Evidence: `writeNode`'s comparison assumes "A `ComponentNode` is JSON by construction", but `useDesigner` is exported (`index.ts:17`) and a JavaScript host can hand in a node holding a `BigInt` or a cycle; the throw happens inside the `setHistory` updater. Failure scenario: a host-built document with a cyclic or non-JSON value in `props` → the first property edit throws out of the React updater and the designer unmounts with no recovery path.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:605-608` — `replaceDocument` applies an arbitrary document with no `validateDocument` gate, and it is public API through the exported `useDesigner`. Evidence: the comment at `:288-291` states "The only in-repo write path is gated by `validateDocument`", which is true of `DocumentDialog` but not of this method as an API surface. Failure scenario: a host restoring from storage calls `api.replaceDocument(parsed)`; duplicate keys then make every tree operation edit the first match, silently — the precondition the mount-time warning exists to announce, arriving by a route that says nothing.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:540` — `duplicateComponent`'s dependency array names the whole `history` object rather than `history.present.root`, so the callback's identity churns on every undo, redo and unrelated edit, which in turn churns `LeftPane`'s `onDuplicate` prop. Evidence: only `history.present.root` is read inside the body (`:522`, `:525`).
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:74` — The cap keeps 101 documents, not 100: `past.length > MAX_HISTORY` only trims once `past` has reached 101, leaving 100 in `past` plus `present`. `docs/spec/12-api.md` says "The store keeps the last hundred documents".
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:253` — `const document = history.present` shadows the global `document` for the whole body of `useDesigner`; any future DOM access in this scope would silently receive a `FormDocument` instead of failing.
- **[NOTE]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/store.ts:269-270,610-613` — `findNode` walks the tree twice per render for the same key (once to derive `selectedKey`, once in the `selectedNode` memo); the derived value could feed the memo instead.

## packages/designer/src/index.ts

- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/index.ts:17` — `useDesigner` is exported as public API but is absent from the "Embedding the designer" contract in `docs/spec/12-api.md`, which names `Designer` as the designer package's only surface; exporting the hook also publishes `replaceDocument` (ungated) and `select` (a bare `setState` setter, so a function argument is silently treated as an updater) as things a consumer may depend on.

## packages/designer/src/keyboard.ts

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/keyboard.ts:113-118` — `moveTarget` decides "can this hold children?" from kit metadata while `moveNode` decides it from the node, so a keyboard move can be reported as legal and then refused by core with the tree unchanged. Evidence: the predicate is `isContainer(previous.node.type)`, supplied by `Tree.tsx:161-163` as `defs.get(type)?.container === true`; `moveNode` refuses on `!Array.isArray(parent.children)` (`packages/core/src/tree.ts:419`), and `store.ts:193-205` documents this exact rule ("Whether a node can hold children is asked of the node… so no kit is needed"). Failure scenario: import a document containing `{ "key": "stack", "type": "Stack", "props": {} }` with no `children` array (a container by kit metadata, a leaf by node shape) → focus the row below it, press `Alt+ArrowRight` → `moveTarget` returns `{parentKey:'stack', index:0}`, `moveNode` returns the root unchanged so `commit`'s identity check drops it, and `Tree.tsx:169` still announces "moved into the component above it" to a screen reader. The move silently did not happen.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/keyboard.ts:100-101` — Siblings are matched by parent *key string* rather than by the actual parent, and the position is recomputed instead of read from `FlatNode.index`, which is already the answer. Evidence: `rows.filter((r) => r.parentKey === row.parentKey)`; `FlatNode.index` is documented as "Position among its parent's children" (`packages/core/src/tree.ts:86-88`), and this file's own header comment at `:26-35` accepts that duplicate keys are reachable because "a host can render a document it never validated". Failure scenario: an imported document with two containers both keyed `stack` → `siblings` merges both containers' children, `position` and `siblings.length` are computed over the merged list, and `Alt+ArrowUp`/`Alt+ArrowDown` compute an index that lands the node at the wrong place among its real siblings.

## packages/designer/src/Icon.tsx

- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/Icon.tsx:253-261` — The comment states "the cast is replaced by asking the type for its own keys", but the cast is still on the line it describes: `(Object.keys(GLYPHS) as (keyof typeof GLYPHS)[])`. `keyof typeof GLYPHS` *is* `IconName`, so this is exactly the `string[] → IconName[]` assertion the comment claims was removed — a reader trusting the comment would believe the array is type-derived when it is asserted.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/Icon.tsx:261` — `.slice()` on the array `Object.keys` just allocated is a no-op copy.

## packages/designer/src/panels/Tabs.tsx

- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tabs.tsx:66-73` — `event.preventDefault()` runs before the single-tab early return, so on a one-tab strip the four navigation keys are swallowed and do nothing at all rather than falling through to the browser. Evidence: `preventDefault()` at `:68`, `if (tabs.length < 2) return` at `:73`. Failure scenario: a caller renders `Tabs` with one tab (the generic signature permits it) → `Home`/`End` are cancelled on the tab button and neither move the tab strip nor scroll the panel.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tabs.tsx:87-102` — Focus is moved to `buttons[next]` unconditionally on the assumption that the parent honoured `onSelect`; a parent that ignores or remaps the selection leaves focus on a tab rendered with `tabIndex={-1}` and `aria-selected={false}`, breaking the roving tabindex until the next click. All in-repo callers honour it, so this is a contract the component does not state rather than a live bug.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tabs.tsx:64-105,125` — The `useCallback` around `onKeyDown` saves nothing: each button is given a fresh inline `(event) => onKeyDown(event, index)` on every render anyway, so the memoised identity never reaches the DOM.

## packages/designer/src/panels/Inspector.tsx

No issues found.

## packages/designer/src/panels/LeftPane.tsx

- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/LeftPane.tsx:63` — The tablist is named `"Left pane"`, a positional label; the enclosing `<aside>` is already labelled "Components and structure" (`Designer.tsx:266`), so a screen reader hears the region's purpose and then the tablist's screen position instead of its purpose.

## packages/designer/src/panels/Palette.tsx

- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Palette.tsx:102` — `GROUP_LABELS[group.group]` renders an empty `<h3>` for a group name outside the vocabulary, which is exactly the third-party-kit case `Icon.tsx:223-232` defends against by name in the same package. Evidence: `groupKit` copies `component.group` through verbatim without checking it against `PaletteGroup` (`packages/core/src/kit.ts:175-178`), and `PaletteGroup` is a compile-time union with no runtime guard; a kit authored in JavaScript, or one built against a newer core, can supply anything. Failure scenario: a kit declares `group: 'widgets'` → the palette draws a group heading that is an empty element above the components, and the entries look orphaned rather than mis-grouped.

## Area 7 — designer: drag, drop geometry, canvas, selection, tree

## packages/designer/src/drag.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/drag.ts:239-243, 216` — the `abandoned` flag raised by the window-blur/visibility path has no matching `pointerup`, so it survives to the *next* unrelated gesture and eats one click. Evidence: `lost()` sets `abandoned.current = moved.current` and calls `cancel()`, which clears `pending`/`moved`/`state` but not `abandoned`; `abandoned` is only lowered in `begin()` (line 144) and in `onUp` (line 217). `onUp` is registered unconditionally on `window` and computes `swallowClick.current = dragged || abandoned.current` with no guard on `press`. Failure scenario: drag a component, alt-tab away mid-drag (window `blur` → `abandoned = true`, drag torn down), release the button outside the window so no `pointerup` ever reaches the page. Return and click a tree row: `pointerdown` runs `lowerSwallow` (which clears `swallowClick`, not `abandoned`), `pointerup` then sets `swallowClick = false || true`, and the row's `onClick` → `consumeClick()` returns `true`, so the click silently does not select. `drag.test.ts:409-430` covers this path but only asserts `state` is cleared, never `consumeClick()`.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/drag.ts:202-219` — a drop resolver that throws leaves the controller permanently stuck in the dragging state. Evidence: the zone loop at 204-207 is not wrapped, and everything that ends the drag — `swallowClick`, `abandoned = false`, `cancel()` — is below it at 216-218. Failure scenario: any exception inside `resolveRelease` (canvas: `indexKit`/`measureTree`/`querySelectorAll`; tree: `Math.max(0, ...rows)` spread) propagates out of the window listener, so `pending.current` and `state` stay set: the drag label stays glued to the cursor, every subsequent `pointerup` re-enters the same throw, and only a window blur recovers it.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/drag.ts:270` — `pointercancel` is wired to bare `cancel`, unlike `lost`, so it does not record the abandonment. Evidence: `lost` at 239-243 sets `abandoned.current = moved.current` before cancelling; the `pointercancel` listener passes `cancel` directly, which never touches `abandoned` or `swallowClick`. Failure scenario: a touch drag on the canvas that the browser reclaims as a `pan-y` scroll (`designer.css:350` sets `touch-action: pan-y`) fires `pointercancel` mid-drag; if the platform still dispatches a `click`, it selects whatever is under the finger — the exact case the Escape path exists to prevent.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/drag.ts:221-228` — Escape only abandons while `pending.current` is set, i.e. while the button is still down; there is no way to abandon a drag whose pointer has been lost, and no `keydown` handler cares about `moved` alone. Cosmetic today because `lost` covers the same window, but the two exits do different bookkeeping (see above).

## packages/designer/src/dnd.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/dnd.ts:138-186` — inside a repeater with more than one drawn row, `resolveDrop` scans a list that interleaves children from every row, so the computed insertion index is wrong. Evidence: `children` is filtered only by `c.parentKey === parent.key` and sorted by `c.index`; `measureTree` (`geometry.ts:177-189`) emits one candidate *per drawn element*, all sharing `key`, `parentKey` and `index`, so a repeater of N rows yields N candidates per authored child, ordered `[a_r0, a_r1, b_r0, b_r1, c_r0, c_r1]` — a sequence whose `rect.top` values are not monotonic. The midpoint loop at 175-186 breaks on the first midpoint greater than the pointer, which is now `a_r1`'s, not `c_r0`'s. Failure scenario: repeater `rep` with children `a,b,c`, two rows drawn at y 0-30/30-60/60-90 and 90-120/120-150/150-180. Drag from the palette and release at y=88 (past `c_r0`'s midpoint of 75, so the correct index is 3): i=0 `a_r0` mid 15 fails, i=1 `a_r1` mid 105 > 88 → `slot=1` → `indexInParent` returns `children[0].index + 1 = 1`. The component lands after `a` instead of after `c`, and `barFor` draws the bar at y=60 — pointing at `c`'s top edge while committing index 1. Reachable in normal authoring: `data` survives the preview toggle (`Canvas.tsx:485-489` clears only `preview`/`validated`/`hoverKey`), and `revealHidden && rows.length === 0` in the renderer draws one row only while the data is empty, so "preview → add three rows → back to editing → rearrange the row's fields" is the trigger.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/dnd.ts:222-223` — the within-parent branch of `documentIndex` returns the raw drawn-list `slot`, which the file's own comment three lines above explains is wrong whenever the drawn list is shorter or longer than the real children list. Evidence: the `!draggedKey` branch at 220 was fixed to go through `indexInParent` precisely because "the drawn list is shorter than the real one"; `draggedIsSibling ? slot : ...` skips that translation. Failure scenario (short): a container `[x, y, z]` where `y`'s kit renderer returns null; drag `z` above `x` → the drawn filtered list is `[x]`, `slot` is 0, correct, but drag `z` below `x` → `slot = 1` while the real filtered list is `[x, y]` and the intended index is 2, so `z` lands between `x` and `y`. Failure scenario (long): reordering a field inside a 2-row repeater, `slot` can reach 4 for a 3-child container; `insertNode` clamps with `Math.min(at, children.length)` (`core/tree.ts:356`), so it silently appends instead of throwing.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/dnd.ts:332-333` — the `anchor === -1` fallback is unreachable. Evidence: `row` is taken from `rows` and has already been rejected at line 310 if `forbidden.has(row.key)`, and `forbidden` always contains `draggedKey` (`closure` line 87); `siblings` filters only `parentKey === row.parentKey && key !== draggedKey`, so `row` is always present and `findIndex` never returns -1. The `row.index` branch is dead and reads as if it handled a real case.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/dnd.ts:314-317` — every drop on the root row of the tree returns `index: 0`, so dropping onto the root always prepends rather than appending. Evidence: the literal `index: 0` with no reference to `offset`. Defensible as a design choice, but neither `05-canvas.md` nor the comment says which end it picks.

## packages/designer/src/geometry.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/geometry.ts:30-41, 177-189` — `Candidate` carries no row identity even though `measureTree` deliberately emits one candidate per drawn element, so every consumer of the candidate list conflates elements from different repeater rows. Evidence: the interface has `key`/`parentKey`/`index`/`container`/`depth`/`rect` and nothing else; the module docblock at lines 10-15 states that "the two attributes together identify an element, and the key alone identifies the node", and `measureTree`'s own comment at 168-176 states it pushes one per element — yet `data-fs-row`, which `rowFromEventTarget` already reads, is dropped on the floor here. This is the enabling cause of the `resolveDrop` index bug above: `resolveDrop` has no way to restrict `children` to the row the pointer is actually in.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/geometry.ts:101-110` — `measureIn` mixes transform-scaled and unscaled units. Evidence: `r.top - s.top` comes from `getBoundingClientRect`, which is post-transform, while `surface.scrollTop`/`clientTop` are CSS pixels. Failure scenario: a host applying any `transform: scale()` above `.fs-shell` gets selection frames that are correct at `scrollTop === 0` and drift proportionally to the scroll offset. `Canvas.tsx:70-76` acknowledges the same class of problem for the device frame, so the constraint is known but unstated here.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/geometry.ts:191` — `measureTree` indexes children with `forEach((child, i) => ...)` over `node.children`, so `Candidate.index` is the document index; correct, but it means two candidates for the same authored node in different rows are indistinguishable by every field the drop arithmetic reads. Recorded here only because the docblock claims the module is where the key/row distinction is honoured.

## packages/designer/src/panels/Canvas.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Canvas.tsx:154, 377, 582` — `selectedRow` is never cleared when the selection changes anywhere but a canvas click, contradicting the prop's own documentation. Evidence: `setSelectedRow` has exactly two call sites — the `useState` initialiser and `onClickCapture`. Selecting from the tree goes `Tree → onSelect → api.select` (`LeftPane.tsx:77`), which the canvas never observes. `SelectionLayerProps.selectedRow` (`SelectionLayer.tsx:59-66`) says it is "absent for a selection made from the tree, which names a node and no row". Failure scenario: click a field in row 2 of repeater A (`selectedRow = '2'`), then select a field of repeater B from the tree; `nodeElement` resolves `[data-fs-key=B][data-fs-row=…2]` and frames row 2 of B rather than row 0, with the toolbar attached to the wrong copy.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Canvas.tsx:356-367` — the "a keystroke is not typing" guard exempts every `ctrlKey`/`metaKey` combination, which lets Ctrl/Cmd+V paste into a kit field while editing. Evidence: `navigational` is true when `event.ctrlKey || event.metaKey`, and the handler returns before `preventDefault()`/`stopPropagation()`; `Tab` is also exempted, and nothing else prevents focus reaching a kit input. Failure scenario: Tab into a text field on the canvas (allowed), press Ctrl+V; the browser's paste runs, the kit's `onChange` fires, `Canvas.onChange` writes into `data` — the exact outcome the comment at 348-352 says the handler exists to prevent ("the preview shows answers nobody entered"). Worse, a paste into a repeater's field can create rows, which then triggers the `resolveDrop` index bug above.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Canvas.tsx:439` + `Tree.tsx:287` — drop-zone precedence is not "registration order" in any stable sense; it flips on the first document edit. Evidence: `registerZone` (`drag.ts:155-160`) does `Map.set`, and its cleanup does `Map.delete`, so a dependency change deletes and re-inserts, moving that zone to the end of the iteration order. Both zones' `resolveRelease` depend on `measureNow`, which depends on `api.document.root`, so **both** re-register on every edit. Failure scenario: the Tree tab is mounted after the Canvas (`LeftPane.tsx:72` mounts it only when active), so the initial order is `canvas, tree`; after the first edit, effect order (aside before main, `Designer.tsx:265/284`) makes it `tree, canvas`. The canvas resolver has no bounds check of its own, so it is asked first for a window during which a release over the tree is offered to the canvas. Currently harmless only because the left pane sits at negative surface-local x and `contains` rejects it — the guard is geometry, not code.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Canvas.tsx:206, 541` — the `snapshot` state, which holds a measured `Candidate` for every drawn element in the form, is never released when a drag ends. Evidence: `setSnapshot` is called only from `takeSnapshot`; nothing sets it back to `undefined`. Staleness is handled by the `dragId` comparison, but the array is retained for the life of the Canvas. Failure scenario: a 500-node form with a 20-row repeater keeps ~10,000 candidate objects alive after every drag until the next one replaces them.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Canvas.tsx:405-411` vs `417-425` — two adjacent comments contradict each other about a bounds check that does not exist. The outer block says "No bounds check of its own… Adding that check broke exactly that drop, which is how it earned this comment instead of the code"; the inner block, sitting inside the function body with no code under it, says "A release outside the surface is not this zone's release… Asking whether the point is on the surface at all is the question the zone is for." A reader cannot tell which is current.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Canvas.tsx:91-98` — the `Canvas` component's docblock is orphaned: a second `/** … */` for `isOwnChrome` follows immediately at 99-107, so the component doc attaches to nothing and `isOwnChrome` carries a description of the canvas.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Canvas.tsx:255` — `shown[address] = all[address] as string` asserts away the `string | undefined` that `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` produces. Sound in practice (`address` came from `Object.keys(all)`), but it is an assertion that lies about the index signature rather than a narrowing.

## packages/designer/src/panels/SelectionLayer.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/SelectionLayer.tsx:114` — the hover frame is measured without a row, so hovering any node inside repeater row N > 0 outlines the copy in row 0. Evidence: `measure(surface, hovered === key ? undefined : hovered)` passes no third argument, so `nodeElement` falls through to `surface.querySelector(selector)` — the first match. The upstream producer discards the row too: `Canvas.tsx:543-544` calls only `keyFromEventTarget`, never `rowFromEventTarget`, which it does use for selection at line 377. The module doc at 65-76 in `geometry.ts` names exactly this failure ("every caller here took the first match") as fixed; the hover path is still on the old behaviour. Failure scenario: repeater with three drawn rows, hover the email field in row 2 → the dashed outline appears around row 0's email field.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/panels/SelectionLayer.tsx:160-183` — the scroll-into-view effect keys on `selectedKey`/`selectedRow` with no notion of where the selection came from, so it also fires for selections made by clicking the canvas, and it aligns to the surface's *border box*, clipping the selection toolbar. Evidence: the comment says "Selecting in the tree should bring the component into view", but `selectedKey` changes identically for `Canvas.onClickCapture → select(...)`; the arithmetic uses `surface.getBoundingClientRect()` and never accounts for the surface's `padding: 40px 24px 24px` (`designer.css:363`), which exists precisely because "the selection toolbar sits above the frame… and the overflow clipped it". Failure scenario: click the visible top edge of a component whose box extends below the fold → `box.bottom > view.bottom` → the canvas scrolls under the cursor. Or select from the tree a component above the current scroll position → `box.top` is aligned to `view.top`, putting the toolbar (~31px above the frame) outside the visible area with no scroll room above it.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/SelectionLayer.tsx:86, 91` — the prop `document: FormDocument` shadows the global `document` for the whole component body. Nothing currently reads the global inside, but any future `document.querySelector`/`document.activeElement` here would silently get a `FormDocument`.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/SelectionLayer.tsx:135-147` — the `ResizeObserver` watches the surface and `surface.querySelector('[data-fs-key]')` (the first element, i.e. the form root). A kit component that grows without changing the root's box — an absolutely positioned popover, a control that reflows within a fixed-height container — moves under a stale frame until a scroll or resize happens.

## packages/designer/src/panels/Tree.tsx

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tree.tsx:117-123` — `onRowPointerDown` arms a drag for any pointer button, so a right-press on a tree row plus four pixels of movement reparents the component. Evidence: the two other `begin` call sites both guard it and both document this exact bug — `Canvas.tsx:330-336` ("It armed a drag for any of them, so a right-press and a small movement relocated the component under its own context menu") and `Palette.tsx:113-120` ("Any button armed a drag"). `Tree.onRowPointerDown` has no `event.button` check, and `drag.ts`'s `window.addEventListener('pointerup', onUp)` (line 269) does not filter by button either, so the secondary-button release runs the full commit path. Failure scenario: right-click a tree row to look for a context menu, drift 4px while the menu opens, release → `onUp` resolves against the tree, `commit.current` fires, and the component is moved to wherever the cursor happened to be, with an undo entry the user never asked for.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tree.tsx:294-295, 343-440` — every pointer move of every drag re-renders the entire tree and re-walks the document. Evidence: `useDrag`'s `state` lives in `Designer` and changes on each `pointermove` (`drag.ts:186`), `Tree` is not memoised, `TreeItem` is not memoised, and `tabStop` calls `flatten(root).some(...)` in the render body at line 295; the `target` memo additionally spreads `snapshot.rows` into `Math.max` at line 258. The Canvas solved the identical problem with the `form` `useMemo` (`Canvas.tsx:461-478`) and documented why; the Tree has no equivalent boundary. Failure scenario: dragging over a 400-component form re-renders 400 `TreeItem`s and walks the tree twice per pointer event.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tree.tsx:271-285` — `resolveRelease` omits the `width === 0` guard its own indicator memo carries, so a zero-width tree can still claim a drop. Evidence: line 259 is `if (width === 0 || x < 0 || x > width) return undefined`; line 279 is `if (x < 0 || x > width) return undefined`. Failure scenario: the left pane is collapsed (`Designer.tsx:264` sets `hidden` on the `<aside>`, which does not unmount the Tree), so every `getBoundingClientRect` is zero and `origin` is `{0,0}`; a release at exactly `clientX === 0, clientY === 0` gives `x = 0` (passes) and `y = 0`, which `resolveTreeDrop`'s `y >= top && y <= top + height` matches against the zero-rect root row → the invisible tree claims the drop and returns `{parentKey: root.key, index: 0}`. Narrow, but the guard asymmetry is the whole reason it is reachable at all.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tree.tsx:171, 207, 232` — three `requestAnimationFrame` callbacks that call `focusRow` are never cancelled. Evidence: no handle is kept and there is no cleanup effect. Failure scenario: press Alt+Down and immediately switch to the Components tab; `LeftPane` unmounts the `Tree` (`LeftPane.tsx:72`), the frame still fires, `listRef.current` is null so the focus call no-ops, but `onSelect(key)` still runs and moves the shell's selection to a component the user has navigated away from.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tree.tsx:146, 226` — `const rows = flatten(root)` is computed at the top of `onRowKeyDown` and then shadowed by a second, identical `const rows = flatten(root)` inside the Delete branch. The inner one reads as though it were measuring something different ("The row is measured before the removal"); it is the same call, so it is a redundant full-tree walk and a shadow that misleads.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tree.tsx:273` — `measureNow(0)` in `resolveRelease`, where the Canvas passes `-1` with an explicit comment that "passing `0` claimed to be drag zero's" (`Canvas.tsx:427-428`). Harmless today because the snapshot is local and never stored, but it is the fixed mistake left in place in the sibling copy.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Tree.tsx:81-101` — `visit` records `parentKey` as the previous node's key even when that node produced no row element, unlike `measureTree`, which returns early and skips the subtree (`geometry.ts:178`). Unreachable today because the tree renders a row for every node, but the two walks disagree about the same invariant while the file's docblock presents them as parallel.

## Area 8 — designer: the properties, condition and rule editors

## packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:661` — The `select` editor hands `optionValue` a list of *unwrapped* option values, so its declared-value pass can never match and it always returns the raw submitted string — the exact bug the comment above it claims to have fixed. Evidence: `optionValue(options, submitted)` (`core/src/json.ts:33-58`) reads a declared value only via `declaredOf(option) = option['value']` for object entries; the call passes `schema.options?.map((o) => o.value)`, so every entry is already the primitive and `declaredOf` returns `undefined` for all of them, the position pass compares `String(i)` against the spelling (not the index the panel rendered), and control falls to `return submitted`. Failure scenario: a kit declares `{ label: 'Two', value: 2 }`; the option renders as `value="2"`, choosing it stores `{ kind: 'static', value: "2" }` (a string), the row still displays as selected because `jsonToString` spells both the same, and the kit receives `"2"` where it declared `2`. Passing `schema.options` itself (a `PropOption[]`, which is assignable to `readonly JsonValue[]`) is what the function was written for.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:344` — Choosing "Always" (or "Never") after authoring a condition destroys the condition with no undo path, because both writes carry the store's single `renderWhen:<key>` coalescing label and therefore *replace* the entry that holds the authored condition instead of stacking on it. Evidence: `store.ts:556` labels every `setRenderWhen` `'renderWhen:' + key`, and `commit` (`store.ts:68-70`) replaces `present` and does not extend `past` when the label matches; the whole condition-authoring run and the subsequent delete share one label. Failure scenario: pick "A condition", build it over several edits (past=[D0], present=D3), pick "Always" → present=D4 (no `renderWhen`), Ctrl+Z → D0, which is visually identical to D4 so the undo appears to do nothing, and D3 is unreachable by undo *or* redo.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:72` — The Data key field accepts any text and the store never screens it, so the panel can write a data key that `validateDocument` rejects and that reads as arithmetic in a computed expression. Evidence: `DataKeyField.commit` (160-172) only trims, `store.setDataKey` (`store.ts:585-603`) only trims, while `renameKey` runs `keyProblem` on the node key (`store.ts:446`) and `tree.ts:789-801` reports `invalid-data-key` for any `dataKey` failing `keyProblem`; nothing on the path from this input to the document runs it. Failure scenario: type `first-name` (or `__proto__`) into Data key — the hint changes to "Submitted as first-name rather than emailField.", the document now fails its own validation on re-import through the JSON dialog, the exported JSON Schema property is outside the key grammar, and per `core/src/keys.ts:13-16` `first-name` in a `computed` binding is a subtraction.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:56-60` — A successful rename changes `node.key`, which is also the React `key` on `<KeyField>`, so the field the user is typing in is unmounted mid-commit and focus is lost to `document.body`. Evidence: the element is keyed `key={node.key}` and its `value` is the same string, so a committed rename necessarily changes the key and forces unmount/remount rather than an update; nothing restores focus. Failure scenario: type `email` and press Enter — the rename lands, the input is replaced, focus falls to `body`, and because the shortcut listener is bound to the shell element and not `window` (`Designer.tsx:596-600`), Ctrl+Z, Delete and Escape are dead until the user clicks back into the designer.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:640-643` — The number editor stores whatever `Number()` returns with no finiteness guard, so a non-JSON value can enter the document. Evidence: `set(Number(e.target.value))` with no `Number.isFinite` check, unlike `Rules.tsx:278` which has exactly that guard; `<input type="number">` treats `1e999` as a valid floating-point number and returns it from `.value`. Failure scenario: type `1e999` into a numeric property → `{ kind: 'static', value: Infinity }`, which is outside `JsonValue`, serialises to `null` on save, and (because `writeNode` compares nodes with `JSON.stringify`, `store.ts:232`) is indistinguishable from a stored `null` for change detection.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:845` — Choosing "(none)" in the icon picker stores an empty string instead of clearing the property, unlike every other editor in the row. Evidence: `onChange={(e) => onChange(e.target.value)}` unconditionally, where the `number` editor (642) and `select` editor (659-660) both call `onChange(undefined)` for `''`. Failure scenario: set an icon then choose "(none)" → the node keeps `icon: { kind: 'static', value: '' }`, the row still shows the modified dot and "(modified)" to a screen reader, and the kit default no longer applies.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:36,50` — `indexKit(kit)` rebuilds a `Map` over every kit component and `keyScopesFor` walks the whole document tree on every render of the panel, which is every keystroke in any property. Evidence: both are plain calls in the component body with no `useMemo`; `indexKit` (`core/src/kit.ts:151`) constructs a new `Map` per call and `keyScopesFor` (`core/src/rows.ts:167`) does two full recursive walks. Failure scenario: typing a 40-character label on a large document runs 40 full tree walks and 40 kit indexings, and the freshly-allocated `dataKeys`/`rowDataKeys` arrays change identity every keystroke so nothing downstream can ever memoise.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:38-39` — A `PropSchema.section` outside the three-value union is dropped from every tab rather than falling back to `main`. Evidence: `main` keeps `(prop.section ?? 'main') === 'main'` and `advanced` keeps `=== 'advanced'`; `style` is claimed by `StyleTab`, so a fourth value matches nothing. Failure scenario: a third-party kit (JavaScript, so the union does not bind it) declares `section: 'layout'` and the property silently has no editor anywhere — the same class of invisible gap the docblock at 617-622 records for `multiline`.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Properties.tsx:683,674,678,626` — The text, colour, code and multiline editors render `''` for any stored value that is not a string, so the first keystroke overwrites a value the box never showed. Evidence: `value={typeof raw === 'string' ? raw : ''}` in four places, with no equivalent of the `JsonEditor`'s "show what the document holds" treatment. Failure scenario: a hand-edited or kit-defaulted `label: 3` draws an empty box that reads as "unset"; typing one character replaces the number with a one-character string.

## packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx:482,531` — The membership options box spells its lines with `jsonToString`, which returns `''` for objects, arrays and `null`, so any non-primitive option renders as a blank line, is dropped by the blank-line filter on the next commit, and the box silently disagrees with the document in the meantime. Evidence: `jsonToString` (`core/src/json.ts:13-18`) returns `''` for anything that is not a string, number or boolean — which is precisely why `LiteralField` was given `spell()` (459-462) — while `OptionsField` still uses `jsonToString` on both the read (482) and the write-back (531), and `commit` filters `line !== ''` (526). Failure scenario: `coerce` (421-428) deliberately parses a leading `{`/`[` as JSON, so typing `{"a":1}` as an option commits `options: [{a:1}]` and then immediately blanks the textarea; the author sees an empty list while the document holds one option, and their next edit to that box writes the object out of existence. The same happens on load for a document holding `null` or a nested list, and for a stored string containing a newline (rendered as two lines, committed as two options).
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx:666-678` — The "Remove condition" button writes through the same coalescing label as the typing that preceded it, so it has no undo entry of its own and one Ctrl+Z discards the whole authoring run instead of restoring the removed row. Evidence: every write here reaches `api.setRenderWhen`/`api.setProp`, and `store.ts:556` labels the former `'renderWhen:' + key` while `commit` (`store.ts:68-70`) replaces the top entry rather than pushing when the label repeats; the docblock on `morph` (82-87) already records this mechanism biting once. Failure scenario: build an `or` group with two rows, then click × on the second → undo returns to before the group existed, and redo only returns to the post-removal state, so the removed row is unreachable in both directions.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx:326,690` — Both fallbacks mint a `ref` whose `dataKey` is the empty string when the offered list is empty, producing a condition that is false forever and cannot be corrected within the row. Evidence: `switchKind` returns `{ kind: 'ref', dataKey: dataKeys[0] ?? '' }` and the Add-condition button calls `emptyCondition(dataKeys[0] ?? '')`; the `BindingMode` picker (`Binding.tsx:145`) and `VisibilityRow` (`Properties.tsx:366`) both disable the condition option when there is nothing to test, and these two paths carry no such guard. Failure scenario: a condition authored on component B naming field A, then A is deleted — `withoutOwn(scopes.form, node)` is now empty; flipping an operand from "Value" to "Field" writes `{ kind: 'ref', dataKey: '' }`, `valueOf(data, '')` reads nothing, the component is hidden and `validateForm` skips everything beneath it, which is the silent failure the file's own comments (316-321) say the design exists to prevent.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx:124` — `morph`'s final branch is an unguarded fallthrough that turns any operator it did not recognise into `not`, rather than refusing it. Evidence: the three preceding branches test `COMPARISONS`, `UNARY`, `MEMBERSHIP` and `GROUPS` with `includes`, and the return at 124 is reached by everything else including a string that is not an operator at all; the repo has `unreadableTag` for exactly this shape and uses it in `Binding.tsx:134`. Failure scenario: a future operator added to `Condition['op']` but not to `ORDER`/the four groups is silently rewritten to `not` the first time anything calls `morph`, with no compile error.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx:573-576` — `asCondition` validates the operator but never reconciles the operand shape with it, so a document whose condition is missing the keys its operator implies draws a row with nothing editable in it. Evidence: `asCondition` (135-145) returns `value` unchanged once `op` is in `ORDER`, and every sub-editor is gated on `'left' in value` / `'value' in value` / `'conditions' in value` / `'condition' in value`. Failure scenario: `{ op: 'and' }` with no `conditions` key renders only the operator select — no nested block and no "Add condition" button — so the group cannot be given children without first switching the operator away and back; `Properties.test.tsx:909-924` covers `{ op: 'and', conditions: 'not-a-list' }` (which does recover, via `asList`) but not the missing key.
- **[LOW]** **[E5]** `packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx:562,578` — `depth` and the `data-fs-depth` attribute it renders are dead, and the comment describing them ("Nesting level, for indentation only") misnames what actually indents. Evidence: `grep -rn "fs-depth"` across `packages/`, `apps/` and `docs/` matches only this line and its compiled `dist` copy; the indentation comes from `.fs-condition__nested`'s `margin-inline-start`/`border-inline-start` (`designer.css:1333-1340`), which is depth-independent.
- **[NOTE]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx:565` — The editor draws unlimited nesting while the engine stops evaluating at 200 levels, so a deep condition can be authored that the engine will not read. Evidence: `evaluate` returns `undefined` past `MAX_DEPTH = 200` (`core/src/condition.ts:59,193`); this component recurses with `depth + 1` and never tests it against anything.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/ConditionEditor.tsx:541-548` — `OptionsField` has no Escape-to-revert, unlike `LiteralField` (392) and every other draft-and-commit editor in this slice, so an in-progress edit can only be abandoned by retyping the old text.

## packages/designer/src/panels/Rules.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Rules.tsx:120-121,189,212` — Every control on the tab — data type, "Validate while typing", each rule's parameter and message, adding a rule, removing a rule — writes through one `write()` and therefore one coalescing label, so structural edits fold into the preceding typing run and a single undo reverts all of them. Evidence: `store.setFieldSchema` labels the write `'schema:' + key` (`store.ts:372`) and `commit` replaces the top entry without extending `past` when the label repeats (`store.ts:68-70`); `06-inspector.md:257-259` states that a run breaks on a structural edit, and nothing here breaks it. Failure scenario: add `required`, then add `minLength`, then set its value — one Ctrl+Z removes both rules and every edit in between; `Rules.test.tsx:420` passes only because it performs exactly one edit after mount.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Rules.tsx:329-338,41-57` — The editor performs no cross-rule consistency check, so a contradictory rule set that no value can ever satisfy is authorable with no warning. Evidence: `RuleRow` narrows on `rule.key` and edits each rule in isolation; nothing in the file compares one rule against another or against `schema.type`. Failure scenario: set `minLength: 10` and `maxLength: 5` on a string field — every non-empty answer fails with "Too short." or "Too long." and the panel shows both rules as ordinary; likewise `min: 100` with `max: 10`, or `minLength` left on a field whose type was later changed to `boolean` (the data-type select at 145 rewrites `type` and leaves `validations` untouched).
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Rules.tsx:349-354` — The pattern field accepts any text without ever compiling or screening it, while the engine treats two whole classes of expression as an automatic failure, so an author can make a field unfillable and the panel says nothing. Evidence: `matchesPattern` (`core/src/validation.ts:226-235`) returns `false` — the rule fails — when `nestedUnbounded(pattern)` is true or when `new RegExp(pattern)` throws; `nestedUnbounded` (163-204) rejects any unbounded quantifier applied to a group containing another, which includes ordinary patterns like `^(\w+\s*)+$`. Failure scenario: type `^(\w+\s*)+$` — a pattern a form author would reasonably write — and every non-empty answer fails with the default "Invalid format.", with no indication in the panel that the expression was refused rather than unmatched.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Rules.tsx:276-284` — `NumberField.commit` updates `committed` but not `draft`, so after a normalising commit the box goes on showing text the document does not hold. Evidence: `setCommitted(String(next))` with no matching `setDraft`, and the re-sync at 271-274 then finds `text === committed` and does nothing; both `OptionsField` and `LiteralField` in `ConditionEditor.tsx` explicitly write the stored spelling back into the draft for this reason. Failure scenario: type `007` (or `5.0`) into a `minLength` and blur — the rule stores `7`, the box keeps showing `007`, and no later render corrects it.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Rules.tsx:78` — Adding a bounds rule immediately applies `value: 0`, a real constraint the author never chose. Evidence: `makeRule` returns `{ key, value: 0 }` for `min`, `max`, `minLength` and `maxLength`, and the add-select writes it straight into `validations` (212). Failure scenario: pick "Maximum" from the add menu on a number field and stop to think — the document now holds `max: 0`, so every positive answer fails validation on the canvas and on the server until a value is typed.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Rules.tsx:119-121` — The guard that tolerates a non-array `validations` does not repair it, so an edit to any other field re-writes the broken schema. Evidence: `rules` falls back to `[]` for display, but `write` spreads `{ ...schema, ...next }`, and a change to `type` or `autoValidate` passes no `validations` key. Failure scenario: a pasted `"validations": "oops"` shows "No rules yet"; changing the data type stores the same invalid `validations` back into the document.
- **[NOTE]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Rules.tsx:183` — `RuleRow` is keyed by rule key and index but not by component, the same shape `06-inspector.md:105-110` records as a real bug for the JSON editor, and `Rules` is not remounted per selection (`Inspector.tsx:49` renders it unkeyed). Evidence: the only local state below this key is `NumberField`'s draft, which re-syncs during render (271-274), so the instance sharing is currently harmless — but the safety rests on that re-sync rather than on the key, and any future stateful control in `RuleRow` inherits the original bug.

## Area 9 — designer: the remaining panels and the stylesheet

## packages/designer/src/panels/Binding.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Binding.tsx:251-268` — Committing a translation-key rename copies the current key's text onto the target key with no collision check, silently overwriting a bundle entry another component is using. Evidence: `commit()` builds `carried` from `bundleFor(translations, tag)[value]`, then `onRename` fires `onTranslate(language, next, text)` per language; that is `api.setTranslation`, whose updater does `bundle[key] = text` (`store.ts:400`) with no read of the existing entry. Failure scenario: components `email` and `contact` both bind `label` to `shared_label` ("Email address"); on `email` you rename `email_label` → `shared_label` while `email_label` holds "E-mail"; `shared_label` becomes "E-mail" and `contact`'s label changes with nothing said.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Binding.tsx:88,138-148` — The mode picker renders with no selection whenever the stored `PropValue.kind` is not among `modesFor(schema)`, so the row shows a blank picker over a working editor. Evidence: `current = value?.kind ?? 'static'` is taken from the document, `modes` from the schema, and nothing reconciles them; `<option>` is emitted only for `modes`. Failure scenario: a document imported through the JSON dialog carries `{"kind":"condition", …}` on a prop whose schema has no `conditional: true` — `validateDocument` permits it (`tree.ts:868`, `BINDING_KINDS` includes `condition`), `PropRow` renders the condition branch unconditionally (`Properties.tsx:561`), and the picker beside it is empty; picking any option then discards the condition. This is the "a working control that reads as an empty box" case `docs/spec/06-inspector.md` says is worse than an absent one.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Binding.tsx:317-320` — A key rename is not one undo step: `onChange` commits under label `prop:…` and each `onTranslate` under `translation:<lang>:<key>`, and `commit()` in `store.ts:64-78` only coalesces against an identical top label. Failure scenario: rename a key on a two-language document → three history entries; one Ctrl+Z leaves the binding on the new key with only one language's text carried over.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Binding.tsx:227-229,258-267` — The comment says "the commit has to move the translations too", but the commit only copies: nothing deletes the old key's bundle entries, so every rename leaves an orphaned entry per language. Defensible (a shared key would break), but the comment and the code disagree.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/Binding.tsx:334` — `<li key={tag}>` duplicates when `document.languages` holds one tag twice, dropping a row. `FormSettings.tsx:123` guards exactly this with `key={String(at) + ':' + tag}` and a comment explaining why; this list does not.

## packages/designer/src/panels/StyleTab.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/StyleTab.tsx:117-126` — The structured controls bypass the `safeDeclarations` refusal that `RawCss` enforces, so a brace, `@` or `<` typed into Padding/Background/etc. is written straight into `node.css`. Evidence: `onCommit` calls `api.setNodeCss(node.key, device, setDeclaration(text, control.name, next))`; `setDeclaration` (`core/src/css.ts:336`) only re-prints declarations and never consults `safeDeclarations`, and `setNodeCss` (`store.ts:562`) stores the string verbatim. Failure scenario: type `red } [data-fs-key="ssn"] { display:none` into Background and blur → the document now holds it; `styleSheetFor` runs `safeDeclarations` over the whole block (`css.ts:265`) and drops **every** declaration for that device, so the padding and border the author set earlier stop applying with no explanation, and `onChange` hands the host the unsanitised string — the exact outcome the `RawCss` comment at `StyleTab.tsx:244-254` says was closed.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/StyleTab.tsx:219-225,243-258` — A refused raw-CSS draft is silently discarded the moment the document changes underneath, contradicting "The draft is kept so the stray brace can be taken out rather than retyped." Evidence: on blur, `if (refused) return` leaves `committed` at the old document text; the re-sync guard `if (value !== committed) { setCommitted(value); setDraft(value) }` then fires on the next document change and replaces `draft`. Failure scenario: type `padding: 8px; } evil` in the CSS box, see the refusal, click the Padding field and type `4px`, tab out → the Padding commit changes `text`, the raw box resyncs, and the refused text is gone.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/StyleTab.tsx:99,181,184,231,240` — Every id is a hard-coded literal (`fs-style-device`, `fs-style-<control>`, `fs-style-raw`, `fs-style-raw-hint`). Two `<Designer>` instances on one page produce duplicate ids, so `htmlFor` and `aria-describedby` resolve to the first match in the document — the other designer's control. `Tabs.tsx:53` uses `useId()` for exactly this reason.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/StyleTab.tsx:75` — `node.css?.[device] ?? ''` assumes a string; a non-string throws out of render. Evidence: `readDeclaration(42, …)` → `parseDeclarations` → `splitTop` → `parts.push(text.slice(start))` on a number is a TypeError. The JSON dialog's gate refuses this (`tree.ts:988-1002`), but `store.ts:297-305` only `console.warn`s about a host-supplied `initialDocument`, so a document from a host's storage crashes the panel meant to repair it.
- **[NOTE]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/panels/StyleTab.tsx:261-265` — `role="alert"` is added to a `<p>` that is already mounted, in the same commit that changes its text. A live region created and populated together is unreliably announced; the repo's own persistent status region (`Designer.tsx:319`) is the pattern that works.

## packages/designer/src/panels/FormSettings.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/FormSettings.tsx:145,154-162` — The default radio and the right-to-left checkbox match languages **by tag** while `remove` deliberately matches **by index**, so the duplicate-tag case the file documents at lines 87-94 is only half handled. Evidence: `onChange={() => api.setLanguages(doc.languages, tag)}` and `doc.languages.map((l) => languageTag(l) === tag ? {...l, bidi} : l)`. Failure scenario: a hand-edited document holding `en` twice — ticking "Right to left" on the first entry flips both, and both radios render `checked`.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/FormSettings.tsx:130,170` — `language.name.trim()` throws on a language with no `name`, taking the whole designer down. Evidence: `Language.name` is required in the type but `store.ts:297-305` only warns when a host's `initialDocument` fails `validateDocument` (which does require it, `tree.ts:1148`). Failure scenario: a host restores `{"code":"fr","bidi":"ltr"}` from its own storage → the shell renders, the user opens the Settings tab to fix it → `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'trim')` during render, and React 19 unmounts the tree.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/FormSettings.tsx:104,144,186,199,204,217` — Hard-coded ids and, worse, a hard-coded radio group `name="fs-default-language"` on inputs that are not inside a `<form>`, so the group is document-scoped. Failure scenario: two `<Designer>` instances on one page share one radio group — choosing a default language in one visually unchecks the other's, and every `htmlFor`/`aria-describedby` here targets the first designer's fields.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/FormSettings.tsx:198-200` — `error` is never cleared when the code field is edited, only on the next successful `add()`. Failure scenario: type `en US`, press Add, get the refusal, correct it to `en-US` → the field keeps `aria-invalid="true"` and `aria-describedby` still points at the stale message until Add is pressed again.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/FormSettings.tsx:184-215` — The add block is not a `<form>` and no `onKeyDown` handles Enter, so pressing Enter in the Code field does nothing. Every other committing field in this slice (`StyleTab.tsx:190`, `Binding.tsx:280`) handles Enter explicitly.

## packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:72-74` — `documentJson`, `schemaJson` and `lossy` are computed in the render body of `DialogBody`, which re-renders on every keystroke in the textarea. Evidence: `setDraft` (line 179) is `DialogBody` state; nothing is memoised. Failure scenario: editing a 200-node document in the JSON view runs two full `JSON.stringify` passes plus a `toJsonSchema` tree walk and three `unexportableRules` walks per character typed — including `schemaJson`, which the document view never renders.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:101-107` — Copy silently does nothing when `navigator.clipboard` is absent, despite the comment "Say so rather than appearing to work." Evidence: optional chaining short-circuits the whole chain, so `navigator.clipboard?.writeText(text).then(ok, fail)` evaluates to `undefined` and neither callback runs. Failure scenario: the designer served over plain HTTP (or in any non-secure context) — `navigator.clipboard` is `undefined`, Copy produces no note, no error, and no clipboard write.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:82-95` — The `try`/`catch` wraps only `JSON.parse`; `documentProblems(parsed)` — and through it `validateDocument` — is called outside it. `validateDocument` is total today (bounded at `MAX_TREE_DEPTH`, and `rowScopeProblems` guards every access, `rows.ts:243-268`), so this is a latent gap rather than a live one: the one function whose totality the dialog depends on has no net under it, and a throw here unmounts the designer instead of showing an error row.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:270-274` — The note is a `role="status"` element created only when `note` is non-empty, and identical consecutive notes produce no DOM change at all. `docs/accessibility.md:79-80` records that the shell's own live region appends a zero-width space for exactly this reason; the dialog reuses neither the persistent region nor the trick. Failure scenario: press Copy twice — the second press announces nothing.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:127-139` — No size or type check before `file.text()` and `setDraft(text)`. `accept="application/json,.json"` is a picker filter, not a constraint. Failure scenario: a 300 MB file (or a mis-picked binary) is read fully into a string and pushed into a controlled `<textarea>`, freezing the tab; the `.catch` only covers a read failure.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:172,175,241,244` — `fs-document-json` and `fs-schema-json` are hard-coded ids on the labelled textareas; two designer instances break the `htmlFor` association. Same issue as `StyleTab`/`FormSettings`.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:183` — `role="alert"` on the `<ul>` replaces its list semantics, so a screen reader announces the error text without "list of 4 items". A wrapping `<div role="alert">` around a plain `<ul>` keeps both.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/panels/DocumentDialog.tsx:270` — The note renders inside `<Tabs>` children, below whichever view is active, so "The schema copied to the clipboard." stays on screen after switching to the Document tab.

## packages/designer/src/designer.css
