- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:185-221` — `.fs-panel button` and `.fs-panel input, .fs-panel select, .fs-panel textarea` are specificity (0,1,1) and beat every single-class component rule in the panel (0,1,0), regardless of source order, so a set of declarations that read as deliberate never apply. Concretely: `.fs-code__area`'s `font-family`/`font-size`/`line-height` (1268-1277) and `.fs-binding__expression, .fs-binding__key`'s (570-576) lose to `font: inherit`, so the raw-CSS box, the expression textarea and the translation-key field are **not** monospace — while `.fs-dialog__editor` (486), which sits outside `.fs-panel`, is; `.fs-color__swatch`'s `padding: 0`/`background: none`/`border-radius: 4px` (1235-1243) lose to `padding: 5px 8px`/`background: var(--fs-bg-app)`, leaving about 12px of colour inside a 28px box and defeating the rule's own stated purpose ("below about 28px a colour well is a coloured line"); `.fs-tabs__tab`'s `background: none`/`border: none`/`padding`/`color` (410-418) and `.fs-rule__remove`/`.fs-tree__remove`/`.fs-prop-row__clear`'s borderless boxes (297-302, 716-725, 1202-1205) all lose to the panel button box.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:496,519-528,651-659` — Four control rules use `--fs-border` where `--fs-control-border` exists precisely for control boundaries, and `test/contrast.test.ts` cannot see it because it measures token pairs, not which rule wins for an element. Evidence: `--fs-border` on `--fs-bg-panel` is 1.39:1 light and 1.41:1 dark — the exact figure the token comment at lines 15-23 records as failing. Affected: `.fs-dialog__editor` (a textarea whose `--fs-bg-well` fill is 1.14:1 against the dialog, so the border carries the whole message), `.fs-dialog__actions button` and `.fs-dialog__file` (background identical to the dialog's, so the border is the only thing identifying a button), and `.fs-languages__add button`, which is inside `.fs-panel` but wins over `.fs-panel button` on equal specificity by coming later at line 651. WCAG 2.2 AA 1.4.11 requires 3:1.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:855-860` — UNCERTAIN: `font-family: revert; font-size: revert; color: revert` on `.fs-canvas__frame` is a no-op, so the inheritance breach the comment at 842-854 says it fixed is still open. Evidence: `revert` in the author origin computes as if no author declaration existed **on this element**; there is no user or UA declaration of `color`, `font-family` or `font-size` on a `div`, so all three inherited properties fall through to the parent's computed value — which is `.fs-shell`'s `var(--fs-text)`, `var(--fs-font)` and `var(--fs-font-size)`. `initial` (or explicit values) is what rolls back to the UA default. Both in-repo kits mask this by setting `color`/`font-family` on their own root (`kit-html.css:45-47`), so the visible failure needs a third-party kit that does not — which is precisely the case the kit contract exists for. The one check that settles it: `getComputedStyle(document.querySelector('.fs-canvas__frame')).color` in a browser with the designer in dark mode.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:283-295` — `.fs-tree__type { opacity: 0.75 }` on a selected row falls below AA for 12px text, and the contrast suite cannot see it because it reads tokens, not composited alpha. Evidence: `.fs-tree__row.is-selected` sets `color: var(--fs-primary-text)` on `background: var(--fs-primary)`; compositing the label at 0.75 gives 3.82:1 in light (`#ffffff` at 75% over `#0b6fa4`) and 4.40:1 in dark (`#0b1116` at 75% over `#2f9fd8`), against a 4.5:1 requirement. Failure scenario: select any component — its type name in the structure tree is the failing text.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:436-440` — `.fs-tabs__panel:focus-visible` can never match. `Tabs.tsx:144-158` deliberately renders the tabpanel with no `tabIndex` and explains why, so the element is never focusable. The rule also papers over the real gap: the panel is `overflow: auto` (429-434) with no keyboard path to scroll it, and `docs/accessibility.md:104-109` records `scrollable-region-focusable` as disabled in the axe run.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:462-467,1207-1211` — Dead rules. `.fs-dialog__head` and `.fs-prop-binding` appear in no `.tsx` or `.ts` source in `packages/` or `apps/` (grep over sources only, excluding built bundles). `.fs-dialog__head` is a leftover from the hand-rolled dialog header the `Tabs` rewrite replaced (`DocumentDialog.tsx:145-157`).
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:1174-1180` — `.fs-prop-row__caption` is rendered by `Properties.tsx:566` for every condition-bound row but has no rule, so it renders at 14px in `--fs-text` while the `<label>` it stands in for renders at 13px in `--fs-text-muted` via `.fs-prop-row label`. Two adjacent rows in the same panel look like different designs.
- **[NOTE]** **[E5]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:270-277,803-806` — `.fs-tree__row` is declared in two blocks 530 lines apart. That is the exact hazard the comment at 245-251 says was eliminated ("Declared here rather than in a second block five hundred lines down") and that `declarationsFor` in `designer.css.test.ts` was rewritten to survive. Harmless today because `.fs-tree__row` is not in `ALLOWED_CANVAS_REACH`, but the comment now describes a rule the file no longer follows.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:490,574,1271` — The monospace stack `ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular, Menlo, Consolas, monospace` is written out three times with no token, while `docs/spec/11-theming.md` lists `--fs-font`/`--fs-font-size` as the type tokens. Similarly, `border-radius` is hard-coded at 2px, 3px (x2), 4px (x2) and 8px (758-1104, 1235-1266) rather than derived from `--fs-radius`, so a host that changes the radius token gets an inconsistent result.
- **[NOTE]** **[E3]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css:1036-1040` — `.fs-selection--hover` composites a `--fs-primary` outline at 0.7 opacity, giving roughly 2.93:1 against `--fs-bg-app` in light, marginally under the 3:1 that `test/contrast.test.ts:145-151` asserts for the same colour at full opacity. It is a transient pointer-only hover indicator, so the practical exposure is small, but the measured value the suite guarantees is not the value that ships.

## Area 10 — the apps, the build, the lint config and CI

## apps/demo-server/src/index.ts
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:207-216` — the lost-update the comment at lines 92-98 claims to have fixed is still live: the read-modify-write straddles an `await` and only the *write* half is serialized. Evidence: `writing` (104) serializes `writeStore` calls only; `const store = await readStore()` (207, and 221 for DELETE) happens outside that chain, and `next` is built from whatever base that read returned. Failure scenario: `PUT /api/forms/a` and `PUT /api/forms/b` arrive together; both `readStore()` resolve on the same base `{}`; A queues `{a}`, B queues `{b}`; the file ends as `{b}`; A received `{"ok":true}` and its document is gone with no log line. Same for a PUT racing a DELETE.
- **[HIGH]** **[E3]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:159-167` — `MAX_BODY` is not a request-body limit: `await c.req.text()` buffers the entire stream into memory *before* `text.length` is compared. Evidence: line 160 precedes the check on 161; no `hono/body-limit` middleware is mounted and `@hono/node-server`'s `serve()` (355) imposes no size cap. Failure scenario: `PUT /api/forms/x` with a 4 GB body — the process allocates the whole string, then answers 400. Secondary: `.length` counts UTF-16 code units, so the effective byte ceiling is up to ~3 MB for multi-byte JSON while the client-facing message (192, 328) says "under 1 MB".
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:344` — for a multi-step document the echoed payload silently drops every step but the first. Evidence: `validateAcross(doc.root, data, scopes)` (332) validates all `scopes`, but `submittedData(doc.root, data, scopes[0])` runs one projection under step one's context; `submittedData`'s `collect` opens with `if (!isVisible(node, data, scope)) return` (`packages/core/src/validation.ts:623`), and a step container is visible only when `context.step` equals its own name. Failure scenario: a two-step wizard submits answers for both steps; validation passes; the response is `{ok:true, received:{…step one only…}}`. There is no `submittedAcross` in core's exports, so the union has to be built here — and the docblock at 335-343 advertises this line as "the implementation a reader copies".
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:260` — the submit route indexes the store directly instead of calling `held()`, so it still has the prototype-chain bug that lines 117-126 document as fixed. Evidence: `const doc = (await readStore())[c.req.param('id')]` versus `held(...)` on 186 and 230. Failure scenario: `POST /api/forms/constructor/submit` → `doc` is `Object.prototype.constructor` (truthy, so the 404 guard at 261 passes) → `doc.root` is `undefined` → `contextValues`/`missingContext`/`evaluatorKeys` are all total and return empty → `validateAcross` → `validateForm` → `visit(undefined)` → `isVisible(undefined)` reads `node.renderWhen` (`packages/core/src/visibility.ts:48`) → TypeError → Hono 500. Reachable for `constructor`, `toString`, `valueOf`, `hasOwnProperty`, `isPrototypeOf`, `__defineGetter__`, etc.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:355` — no lifecycle handling at all: the value `serve()` returns is discarded, so there is no `close()`, no `SIGINT`/`SIGTERM` handler, and no `process.on('unhandledRejection')`/`uncaughtException`. Failure scenario: `pnpm dev:server` runs `node --watch`; a file save kills the process between `writeFile` (109) and `rename` (110), leaving an untracked `apps/demo-server/forms.json.tmp` (not gitignored — see the `.gitignore` finding) and dropping any queued write in `writing`.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:108-110` — the temp path is a fixed `../forms.json.tmp`, which is only safe under the "this process is the only writer" assumption stated at 97-98; `node --watch` briefly overlaps an old and a new process, and on Windows `rename` over a `forms.json` that a concurrent `readFile` still holds open can fail `EPERM`, producing a 500 and a stranded `.tmp`.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:161-166` — `bodyOf` collapses "too large" and "malformed JSON" into a single `undefined`, so a 1 MB overflow is reported as a 400 whose message talks about JSON (192) and a client cannot tell the two apart; a 413 is the accurate answer for one of them.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:178-180` — the listing guard admits arrays (`typeof [] === 'object'`) and `String(doc.id ?? '')` renders a non-string id as `[object Object]`. Failure scenario: a hand-edited `forms.json` containing `{"x": []}` or `{"x": {"id": {}}}` yields a row that `GET /api/forms/:id` can never fetch, which is the "one missing row" outcome the comment at 172-177 says it wanted to avoid producing a broken row instead.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:170, 186, 207, 221, 260` — every route re-reads and re-parses the whole store from disk; there is no cache and no ceiling on the file size that `readFile`/`JSON.parse` will accept, so the read side has the unbounded-input property the write side was capped for.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:194` — `const doc = body as FormDocument` is an unsound cast applied to arbitrary parsed JSON before anything has checked it; it is immediately guarded by `validateDocument` (195), which is documented as total, so this is style rather than a live bug.

## apps/demo-server/package.json
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/package.json:8-9` — both scripts pass `node --experimental-strip-types`, which first exists in Node 22.6.0, while the only declared floor is the root `engines.node: ">=22.0.0"`; this package declares no `engines` of its own. Failure scenario: a contributor on Node 22.5 runs `pnpm dev:server` and gets `bad option: --experimental-strip-types` with nothing pointing at the version requirement.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/package.json:7-10` — no `test` script and no test file exists anywhere under `apps/`, even though `vitest.config.ts:24-25` deliberately widened `include` to `apps/*/src/**/*.test.ts` for exactly this package; the five routes here are covered by no automated check.

## apps/demo-server/tsconfig.json
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `apps/demo-server/tsconfig.json:1-9` — no command ever uses this file, so its deliberate `lib: ["ES2022"]` (no DOM, the setting that mirrors invariant 1 for the server) is never in force. Evidence: the root `tsconfig.json` references only `packages/*`, so `tsc --build` never sees it; `pnpm typecheck`'s second pass is `tsc -p tsconfig.eslint.json`, which picks the file up via `apps/*/src/**/*` under the base `lib: ["ES2022","DOM","DOM.Iterable"]`. Failure scenario: `document.querySelector(...)` added to `src/index.ts` typechecks clean in CI and crashes at runtime in Node.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `apps/demo-server/tsconfig.json:6` — `allowImportingTsExtensions` has nothing to allow: `src/index.ts` has no relative imports at all.

## apps/playground/src/App.tsx
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `apps/playground/src/App.tsx:83-90` — `persist` is the designer's `onChange`, and it does a full `JSON.stringify` of the document plus a synchronous `localStorage.setItem` on every change, i.e. per keystroke, with no debounce. Failure scenario: a large document makes typing in the properties panel visibly janky, and the cost is paid on the main thread inside the input handler.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `apps/playground/src/App.tsx:27, 101-109` — the unreachable throw exists only because `KITS` is typed `Record<string, …>`; typing the map's keys as `'mui' | 'html'` (or `as const satisfies …`) removes both the `??` fallback and the throw without a non-null assertion. The comment defends the throw but not the type that forces it.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `apps/playground/src/App.tsx:37` — `JSON.parse(raw) as FormDocument` is an unsound cast on untrusted `localStorage` content; it is immediately guarded by `validateDocument` at 45, and the surrounding `catch` (52) covers a throw, so no live bug.

## apps/playground/tsconfig.json

The root `tsconfig.json` does not reference it, and `pnpm typecheck`'s second pass uses `tsconfig.eslint.json` (which includes `apps/*/src/**/*` and `apps/*/vite.config.ts` under its own options), so the `types: ["vite/client"]` narrowing here affects the editor only.

## eslint.config.js
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `eslint.config.js:37-237` — `pnpm lint` (`eslint .`) never lints `aliases.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`, `playwright.config.ts`, or `apps/playground/vite.config.ts`, because no config object's `files` pattern matches a root-level or app-root `.ts` file. Evidence: the patterns present are `packages/*/src/**`, `apps/*/src/**`, `test/**/*.ts`, `e2e/**/*.ts`, `**/*.test.ts(x)`, `**/*.js`, `**/*.mjs`, `*.config.js` — none matches `./aliases.ts`; ESLint's directory walk returns `matchesPattern && config !== void 0` (`node_modules/eslint/lib/eslint/eslint-helpers.js:357`) and `@eslint/config-array` returns `undefined` when no non-universal `files` matched (`dist/cjs/index.cjs:1376`). Failure scenario: a `console.log`, a missing return type, an unused `eslint-disable`, or a de-anchored alias regex in `aliases.ts` — the file whose anchoring `CLAUDE.md` calls load-bearing — passes `pnpm lint` clean.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `eslint.config.js:125-147, 159-173` — the invariant guardrails do not see dynamic `import()`. Evidence: `no-restricted-imports` registers visitors for `ImportDeclaration`, `ExportNamedDeclaration`, `ExportAllDeclaration` and `TSImportEqualsDeclaration` only (`node_modules/eslint/lib/rules/no-restricted-imports.js:817-825`); there is no `ImportExpression` visitor. Failure scenario: `const { Box } = await import('@mui/material')` in `packages/designer/src` — a plausible lazy-load — breaks invariant 2 and lints clean. (The static forms *are* enforced: the gitignore-style `group` patterns were checked against the `ignore` package ESLint uses — `@mui/*` does match `@mui/material/Button`, `antd` does match `antd/es/button`, and `@formsmith/kit-*` does match `@formsmith/kit-mui/styles.css`.)
- **[LOW]** **[E5]** `eslint.config.js:135-139` — the group `['react', 'react/*']` also matches `@emotion/react` and `@chakra-ui/react/button` under gitignore semantics (verified directly against `ignore@5.3.2`). Both are already banned by `COMPONENT_LIBRARIES`, so nothing slips through, but whichever rule entry reports first decides the message, and an `@emotion/react` import in core can be reported as "Invariant 1: @formsmith/core runs in Node… React-free", which points the reader at the wrong invariant.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `eslint.config.js:29, 32` — the `message: ''` fields are dead: `noComponentLibrary(why)` (35) overwrites `message` on every entry, and `COMPONENT_LIBRARIES` has no other consumer.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `eslint.config.js:232` — `files: ['*.config.js', …]` matches only root-level `*.config.js`, so today it applies to `eslint.config.js` alone; it reads as though it covers `apps/*/vite.config.*`, which are `.ts` and unlinted anyway (see the first finding).

## aliases.ts
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `aliases.ts:16-30` — nothing in CI exercises the two string CSS aliases, so the build failure the docblock describes would not be caught. Evidence: `@formsmith/designer/styles.css` and `@formsmith/kit-html/styles.css` are imported only from `apps/playground/src/App.tsx:12-13`, and `.github/workflows/ci.yml` never builds the playground. Failure scenario: someone "simplifies" line 29 to a plain string `find`, `@formsmith/designer/styles.css` resolves to `…/src/index.ts/styles.css`, and `pnpm test`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm build` and `pnpm e2e` all stay green.

## vitest.config.ts
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `vitest.config.ts:24-25` vs `test/count-reporter.ts:62-67` — this file deliberately collects `apps/*/src/**/*.test.ts(x)`, but the reporter's on-disk census walks only `packages/*/src/` and `test/`; see the count-reporter finding for the consequence.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `vitest.config.ts:42-52` — `coverage` declares reporters but no `thresholds`, so `pnpm test:coverage` can never fail on a regression, and `include: ['packages/*/src/**']` excludes `apps/`, so the demo server's coverage is invisible even though its tests are now collected.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `vitest.config.ts:29` — `exclude` replaces vitest's defaults rather than extending them (`...configDefaults.exclude`). Harmless today because `include` is explicit and narrow, but a future broader `include` would inherit no default exclusions.

## playwright.config.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `playwright.config.ts:42` — the e2e suite runs the Vite **dev** server, which resolves `@formsmith/*` to `src` through `aliases.ts`, so `pnpm e2e` never loads a package's `dist` despite running after `pnpm build` in CI. Combined with the build check being `test -f` only, nothing in the repository ever executes the published entry points. Failure scenario: a broken `exports` map or an emitted import that does not resolve under Node ships with every gate green.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `playwright.config.ts:27` — `retries: 1` on CI. This suite exists specifically for positional and geometry checks that jsdom cannot make; a drop-target or focus-order test that fails once and passes on the retry is reported green, which is the failure mode the header comment (1-13) argues against.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `playwright.config.ts:20-56` — no `timeout` or `expect.timeout` is set, so per-test 30 s / per-assertion 5 s defaults apply; `webServer.timeout: 60_000` covers only server startup.

## test/setup.ts
- **[LOW]** **[E5]** `test/setup.ts:25` — `observerCount` is exported and read by nothing. Grep across `packages/` and `test/` returns only its own definition and the docblock that says "`observerCount()` below is what a test asks" (line 37). The `made`/`observing`/`disconnects` bookkeeping the comment justifies therefore proves nothing today, and if a test were written against it the counters would still be wrong: they live on `globalThis` (20-22), are never reset between files, and vitest reuses a worker across test files, so any absolute-count assertion would depend on file order within the pool.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `test/setup.ts:40` — the stub is installed only `if (!('ResizeObserver' in globalThis))`. Should a future jsdom version ship a real `ResizeObserver`, the counters silently stay at zero forever — the same always-reads-zero silent pass the docblock at 5-13 says the `globalThis` placement was chosen to prevent.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `test/setup.ts:65-66` — `Element.prototype.scrollIntoView` is patched globally and never restored; every subsequent test file in the worker sees the stub whether or not it opted in.

## test/count-reporter.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `test/count-reporter.ts:47-70` — the guard disables itself the first time a test is added under `apps/`. Evidence: `testFilesOnDisk()` walks `packages/*/src/` (62-66) and `test/` (67) only, while `vitest.config.ts:24-25` also collects `apps/*/src/**/*.test.ts(x)`; `collected !== onDisk` at line 101 then holds permanently. Failure scenario: someone adds `apps/demo-server/src/index.test.ts`; from that commit on, every full run prints "suite-size check skipped: N+1 of N test files ran, so this was a filtered run" and the documented totals in README.md, CLAUDE.md and ROADMAP.md drift unchecked — the exact drift the file exists to catch, reported as a routine skip message.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `test/count-reporter.ts:36-43` — `totals()` collects `\d{2,4}` and keeps only values `>= 100`, so the check silently covers nothing once the suite passes 9 999 tests: a document quoting `10500 tests` matches no capture, `counts` is empty, and the file lands in `silent` rather than `wrong`.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `test/count-reporter.ts:133` — failure is signalled by `process.exitCode = 1` inside `onFinished`, so the default reporter still prints a green summary above it; the only evidence is the `[docs]` block on stderr plus a non-zero exit. (Vitest never resets `exitCode` to 0 — every assignment in `node_modules/vitest/dist/chunks/` sets 1, or 0 only under `passWithNoTests`, which is not enabled — so the gate does fire.)
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `test/count-reporter.ts:94-95` — `implements Reporter` with `onFinished` is the vitest 2 API; vitest 3 renamed it to `onTestRunEnd` and leaves `onFinished` unimplemented, so a major bump makes this class a silent no-op with no type error to warn anyone (`Reporter`'s members are all optional). The root pin `vitest: ^2.1.8` means it will not happen automatically.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `test/count-reporter.ts:97` — `if (ran === 0) return` means the reporter is inert on a run that executed nothing, so it cannot itself catch "the suite passed having proven nothing"; a `-t` filter that matches nothing reaches this early return.

## test/contrast.ts
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `test/contrast.ts:76-87` — `tokensFrom` collects from *every* rule whose trimmed selector equals `selector`, including rules nested inside at-rules, and later declarations overwrite earlier ones with no conflict reported. Evidence: the loop has no `break`, no at-rule awareness, and `tokens[trimmed] = value.trim()` unconditionally overwrites; the `found` flag only guards the zero-match case. Failure scenario: writing `designer.css`'s dark block as a bare `.fs-shell` inside `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` instead of the current `.fs-shell:where(:not([data-fs-theme='light']))` (`packages/designer/src/designer.css:47-49`) makes `tokensFrom(css, '.fs-shell')` return the dark palette, and every light-theme contrast assertion then checks dark twice — precisely the failure the docblock at 61-69 says exact-selector matching prevents.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `test/contrast.ts:81` — `declaration.split(':')` takes only the segment after the first colon as the value, so `--x: url(https://…)` yields `url(https`. `parseHex` then throws "Not a hex colour", which is loud rather than wrong, but the message points at a colour parse failure rather than at the parser truncating the declaration.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `test/contrast.ts:19-36` — `parseHex` accepts only `#rgb`/`#rrggbb`, so `#rrggbbaa`, `rgb()`, `hsl()`, `oklch()` and `color-mix()` all throw. That is the safe direction, but it means the contrast audit constrains what colour syntax the two stylesheets may ever use, and nothing in the file says so.

## scripts/copy-assets.mjs
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `scripts/copy-assets.mjs:4-7` — the asset list is hand-maintained and tied to nothing: `packages/kit-html/dist/kit-html.css` is produced only by line 6 and verified by nothing. Evidence: `packages/kit-html/package.json:14` maps `"./styles.css": "./dist/kit-html.css"`, but `.github/workflows/ci.yml:77` checks only `packages/designer/dist/designer.css`. Failure scenario: line 6 is dropped or its path drifts; `pnpm build`, `pnpm test` and CI all pass, and `@formsmith/kit-html` publishes with an `exports` entry pointing at a file that is not in the tarball.

## .github/workflows/ci.yml
- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:73` — the "every package emitted `dist`" guarantee omits `kit-html`: the loop is `for pkg in core renderer designer kit-mui`. `CLAUDE.md:66` states CI checks "that every package emitted `dist/index.js` and `dist/index.d.ts`". **(Verified: the loop list is exactly those four, and line 77 checks only `packages/designer/dist/designer.css`.)** Evidence that kit-html is in scope: `packages/kit-html/package.json` declares `publishConfig.access: public`, `main`/`types`/`exports` pointing at `./dist/index.js` and `./dist/index.d.ts`, and it is referenced from the root `tsconfig.json:13-15`. Failure scenario: the kit-html project reference is dropped from the root tsconfig, or its build emits nothing; `tsc --build` succeeds over the remaining four, the check passes, and the package whose entire purpose is proving a kit is a package publishes empty.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:71-78` — nothing in CI ever *loads* a `dist`. `pnpm test` resolves `@formsmith/*` to `src` via `vitest.config.ts:7` → `aliases.ts`, `pnpm e2e` runs the Vite dev server which does the same (`apps/playground/vite.config.ts:8`), and this step is `test -f` only. Failure scenario: a malformed `exports` condition, a wrong `types` path, or an emitted relative import missing its `.js` extension ships with every gate green, because no gate has ever imported the built artefact.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:77` — only `designer.css` is checked, while `scripts/copy-assets.mjs` also emits `packages/kit-html/dist/kit-html.css`, which `packages/kit-html/package.json:14` exposes as an `exports` subpath. Same failure shape as above, one package over.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E2]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:25, 27, 29, 62` — every action floats on a mutable major tag rather than a commit SHA, including the third-party `pnpm/action-setup@v4`. Mitigations are real and worth noting: the trigger is `pull_request` (not `pull_request_target`), `permissions: contents: read` (18-19), and no `secrets.*` is referenced anywhere in the file — so a compromised tag cannot exfiltrate a token, but it does execute arbitrary code with the repository checked out and the pnpm store writable.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:22-24` — the job declares no `timeout-minutes`. Failure scenario: `webServer` never answers on 5180 and Playwright's 60 s wait is not the binding constraint (a hung `playwright install --with-deps`, a stuck browser), so the run consumes the 360-minute default before anyone sees a result.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:14-16` — the `concurrency` group keys on `github.ref` with `cancel-in-progress: true`, which applies to `push: branches: [main]` as well as pull requests. Failure scenario: two merges land a minute apart; the first commit's run is cancelled, so main carries a commit that was never fully verified.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:36-58` — the actual order is Format, Lint, Typecheck, Test, Build, e2e; `CLAUDE.md` says CI runs `pnpm test`, `lint`, `format:check`, `typecheck`, `build`, `e2e` "in that order". No gate is missing and none can be masked (there is no `continue-on-error`, no `|| true`, and the shell check at 72-78 uses `|| { …; exit 1; }` correctly), so this is documentation drift rather than a hole.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:21-78` — neither app is built or started: `apps/playground`'s `vite build` is never run and `apps/demo-server` is never launched, so both are covered only by the typecheck and lint passes.

## package.json
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `package.json:8` — `build` is `tsc --build && node scripts/copy-assets.mjs` over a root `tsconfig.json` that references only `packages/*`, so "build" never touches `apps/`, and `apps/playground`'s own `build` script is unreachable from the root. The name implies a repo-wide build.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `package.json:17` — `clean` passes `"**/*.tsbuildinfo"` to rimraf with no ignore pattern; rimraf's glob does not exclude `node_modules` by default, so `pnpm clean` walks the entire dependency tree and will delete `.tsbuildinfo` files belonging to dependencies as well as this repo's.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `package.json:49` — `vitest: ^2.1.8` pins the major that `test/count-reporter.ts` depends on (`onFinished`); a bump to 3 silently turns that reporter into a no-op. Worth a comment or a `vitest` version assertion next to the reporter.

## pnpm-workspace.yaml
No issues found.

## tsconfig.json
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `tsconfig.json:3-19` — `references` lists the five packages and neither app, so `tsc --build` and `tsc --build --force` never see `apps/demo-server/tsconfig.json` or `apps/playground/tsconfig.json`. Those two files are consequently inert configuration; the apps are typechecked only incidentally, by `tsconfig.eslint.json`'s `apps/*/src/**/*` include and under *its* compiler options.

## tsconfig.base.json
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `tsconfig.base.json:20-21` — `declarationMap: true` and `sourceMap: true` are on, but every package's `files` array is `["dist","README.md"]` (e.g. `packages/core/package.json:15-18`), so the published tarballs contain `.js.map` and `.d.ts.map` files whose `sources` point outside the package. Observed in `packages/core/dist/condition.js.map`: `{"sourceRoot":"","sources":["../src/condition.ts"]}` with no `sourcesContent`. Failure scenario: a consumer steps into `@formsmith/core` in a debugger, or a bundler follows the map, and resolves `node_modules/@formsmith/core/src/condition.ts`, which does not exist — every published map is dead weight plus a broken reference. Either add `src` to `files`, or set `sourcesContent`/`inlineSources`, or turn the maps off for the published build.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `tsconfig.base.json:2-22` — `noUnusedLocals`/`noUnusedParameters` are off, which is fine where eslint's `no-unused-vars` applies; it does not apply to `aliases.ts`, `vitest.config.ts`, `playwright.config.ts` or `apps/*/vite.config.ts` (see `eslint.config.js`), so those four files have no unused-binding check from either tool. `strict`, `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` and `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` are all present as `CLAUDE.md` claims, and no config in this slice relaxes them.

## tsconfig.eslint.json
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `tsconfig.eslint.json:13-31` — this is the only config that typechecks `apps/*`, and it applies the base `lib` (`ES2022`, `DOM`, `DOM.Iterable`), overriding each app's own narrower `lib`. Consequence: `apps/demo-server`'s `lib: ["ES2022"]` never takes effect in CI. `packages/core` is unaffected because pass 1 (`tsc --build --force`) checks it under `packages/core/tsconfig.json`'s `lib: ["ES2022"]`.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `tsconfig.eslint.json:24-30` — `paths` maps the five bare `@formsmith/*` specifiers but not `@formsmith/designer/styles.css` or `@formsmith/kit-html/styles.css`, which `aliases.ts` handles for the bundler. No test imports those specifiers today; a future component test that imports a kit's stylesheet would need an entry here as well as in `aliases.ts`, and the two lists have no shared source.

## packages/core/package.json
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/package.json:9-14` — `exports` declares only `"."` with `types`/`default`, so `require('@formsmith/core')` from a CommonJS host resolves the ESM file (`ERR_REQUIRE_ESM` on Node before 22.12) and there is no `"./package.json"` subpath, which several tools read. ESM-only is a legitimate choice for this project; it is undocumented in the manifest.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/package.json:3` — `version: "0.0.0"` alongside `publishConfig.access: "public"`; publishing as-is puts `0.0.0` on the registry for all five packages.

## .npmrc
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E2]** `.npmrc:1` — `strict-peer-dependencies=false` disables the only check that would surface a peer-range violation, in a repository whose entire packaging story is peer dependencies (`react`, `react-dom` on four packages, `@mui/material` on kit-mui). Failure scenario: a package declares a peer range the workspace does not satisfy — or a consumer-facing range that has drifted from what the repo actually develops against — and `pnpm install` reports nothing, so the mismatch is discovered by a consumer.

## .gitignore
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `.gitignore:27` — `apps/demo-server/forms.json` is ignored but `apps/demo-server/forms.json.tmp` is not. **(Verified: `git check-ignore -v` on both paths matches only `forms.json`.)** The temp file is a real artefact: `apps/demo-server/src/index.ts:108-110` writes it and then renames, and the package's `dev` script is `node --watch`, which kills the process on every file save. Failure scenario: a save lands between `writeFile` and `rename`; `forms.json.tmp` survives holding the developer's form documents; it shows as untracked in a public MIT repo and one `git add -A` commits it — the same hazard the `forms.json` line was added for, and the comment above it ("Running the demo must not dirty the tree") no longer holds.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `.gitignore:1-9` — `coverage/` is ignored for Git and (via Prettier 3's default `.gitignore` handling) for `pnpm format:check`, but ESLint flat config does **not** read `.gitignore` and `eslint.config.js:39` does not list it. Failure scenario: after a local `pnpm test:coverage`, `pnpm lint` walks `coverage/**/*.js` (the HTML reporter emits `prettify.js`, `sorter.js`, `block-navigation.js`), which are not in `tsconfig.eslint.json`, and the typed-lint parser errors on files nobody wrote.

The word list is 40 lines of maintained configuration that never executes, and reads as an active gate to anyone adding a word to it.

## Area 11 — tests: the core tree, rows, schema, JSON and keys suites

## packages/core/src/tree.test.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/tree.test.ts:401` — Nothing in this file asserts that `removeNode` leaves its input tree unchanged, so invariant 4 ("tree operations are immutable, because undo holds references to prior documents") is undefended for the one operation that deletes. Evidence: the only three `removeNode` call sites that hold a pre-op reference are `:87` (fresh `fixture()` inline, no prior assertion), `:401-407` (`after.children?.[1]).toBe(before.children?.[1])` — trivially true if `after === before`), and `:413` (`toBe(before)` for a miss, which a mutating version also satisfies). There is no `expect(after).not.toBe(before)` for removal, unlike `:377` for `updateNode`. Failure scenario: replace `removeNode` (tree.ts:362-387) with `const i = root.children.findIndex(c => c.key === key); if (i >= 0) { root.children.splice(i, 1); return root }` plus in-place recursion — every assertion in `tree.test.ts` still passes (`:87` sees ['root','stack','b','c']; `:405` sees ['b']; `:406` and `:413` are identity-true), and `moveNode` (which composes remove+insert) also stays green, while the designer's undo stack would be silently corrupted.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/tree.test.ts:57` — The `generateKey` describe covers only the type `TextField`, so the key-sanitising branch that exists because of a named past incident is untested. Evidence: `tree.ts:136-137` (`const cleaned = camel.replace(/[^A-Za-z0-9_]/g, ''); const base = keyProblem(cleaned) ? 'field' : cleaned`) with a comment describing a kit typing a component `Date-Picker` or `2FA`; the only tests are `:59` (`'TextField'` → `textField`) and `:64` (numbering), and `generateKey` appears in no other test file (only `factory.ts` calls it; there is no `factory.test.ts`, and `kit-contract.test.tsx` instantiates only identifier-named defs). Failure scenario: delete both lines so `base = camel` — the suite stays green, while `instantiate` on a kit def typed `Date-Picker` mints the key `date-Picker`, which `validateDocument` then reports as `invalid-key`: the palette adds a component the JSON dialog refuses.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/tree.test.ts:494` — Every `duplicateNode` fixture carrying a condition uses a single flat `eq`, so the recursive and list-rewriting branches of `repointBinding` are never executed. Evidence: fixtures at `:511-519`, `:551-558`, `:1372-1379`, `:1406-1409` are all one-level `eq`/`truthy`; `repointBinding` (tree.ts:233-246) additionally rewrites `shape['options']` for `in`/`notIn` and recurses through `shape['conditions']` and `shape['condition']`, and `repointBinding` is exported for the designer's rename with no direct test anywhere in the repo. Failure scenario: delete the `conditions`/`condition` recursion at tree.ts:240-246 — a `Step` whose `renderWhen` is `{op:'and', conditions:[{op:'eq', left:{kind:'context',path:'step'}, right:{kind:'literal',value:'step1'}}]}` duplicates with the original's key still inside it (two steps render at once, the copy unreachable), and no test fails.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/tree.test.ts:106` — `moveNode` is never exercised with the source and the target in the same parent, which is the commonest designer gesture and the one with an index off-by-one. Evidence: the moves tested are `'c'→'stack'` (`:108`), `'stack'→'a'` (illegal, `:114`), `'root'→'stack'` (`:119`), unknown keys (`:129-130`), and in the structural-sharing block `'b'→'a'`, `'left'→'a'`, `'missing'→'root'`, `'root'→'left'` (`:425-432`) — none has `findParent(root, key)?.key === newParentKey`. Failure scenario: `moveNode` composes `insertNode(removeNode(root, key), …, index)` (tree.ts:423), so moving a node *down* within its own parent lands one slot short of the requested index; no test in this slice would report it.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/tree.test.ts:68` — The describe is named "mutations are immutable" but only one of its six tests checks immutability (`:69-74`, `insertNode`); the other five assert result content and structural sharing, so the name reads as a guarantee the block does not deliver for `removeNode`, `updateNode` or `moveNode`.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/tree.test.ts:81` — Only the over-range clamp is tested; the lower clamp `Math.max(0, …)` at tree.ts:356 has no test, so `insertNode(root, 'stack', node, -3)` is unpinned.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/tree.test.ts:7` — `flatten` and `walk` are exported from `tree.ts` but never imported here; `flatten`'s `parentKey`/`index`/`depth` contract is pinned only in `packages/designer/src/keyboard.test.ts`, i.e. outside the package that owns it.

## packages/core/src/rows.test.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/rows.test.ts:155` — This table asserts, as correct behaviour, that a repeater whose submitted value is not a list has *every rule inside it* skipped with nothing reported — the silent-permissive server-side outcome the project states it is organised against. Evidence: `rowsOf` returns `[]` for a non-array (rows.ts:117); `validateForm` then runs `rows.forEach` zero times and returns (validation.ts:508, 527-535), having only run the repeater's *own* rules against the raw value, where `lengthOf('nope') === 4` (validation.ts:269-271) so even a `minLength` on the list passes; the test pins `expect(errors).toEqual({ email: DEFAULT_MESSAGES.required })` at `:175`. Failure scenario: a client posts `{"email":"a@b.co","addresses":"nope"}` to a server calling `validateForm`; every `required` field inside the repeater is accepted as satisfied and the submit goes through. Correct behaviour is the one the cap branch already models three lines away — `errors[addressOf(path, node.key)] = <message>` when the value is *present and not a list* (validation.ts:522-525) — distinguishing that from an absent value, which legitimately means no rows.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/rows.test.ts:596` — The nested-repeater fixture re-introduces Trap 11: both repeaters set `key` and `dataKey` to the same string, so the nested-address tests cannot see the distinction they exist to prove. Evidence: `key: 'orders', dataKey: 'orders'` (`:603-607`) and `key: 'lines', dataKey: 'lines'` (`:618-619`); the inner fields `reference` and `sku` carry no `dataKey` at all. The frames are built from `dataKeyOf(node)` (validation.ts:531, rows.ts:66) and the leaf from `node.key`. Failure scenario: change `pushRow(path, { key: dataKeyOf(node), … })` to `{ key: node.key, … }` — the assertions at `:640-644`, `:648-656` and `:660-662` are byte-identical, i.e. this describe adds nesting coverage but zero coverage of the defect the sibling describe at `:513` was written for. (The same collapse holds for the `addresses` fixture at `:104-118`.)
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/rows.test.ts:58` — The `decodeRow` rejection list never exercises the digit bound, which is the one part of the grammar carrying a stated invariant. Evidence: the longest index in the list is `addresses[1]`; `SEGMENT` bounds the index at fifteen digits (`(0|[1-9][0-9]{0,14})`, rows.ts:90) specifically so a decoded coordinate re-encodes to itself. Failure scenario: relax the quantifier to `[0-9]*` — no test fails, while `addresses[1000000000000000000000]` decodes to index `1e21` and `encodeRow` gives back `addresses[1e+21]`, a coordinate that no longer round-trips.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/rows.test.ts:368` — The unplaceable-address list covers the missing-row and not-a-list branches but not the row-is-not-an-object branch. Evidence: `'addresses[9].postcode'` hits `row === undefined` and `'email[0].postcode'` hits `!Array.isArray(list)` (rows.ts:499, 502); no case supplies a list whose element is a scalar or an array, so `typeof row !== 'object' || Array.isArray(row)` (rows.ts:502) is never the reason for the refusal.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/rows.test.ts:341` — The `data` fixtures at `:341`, `:538` and `:635` are describe-scope constants shared by every test in their block rather than factories; they survive only because `applyChange`'s immutability is separately asserted (`:363-365`), so a future in-place write would surface as `it()`-order dependence rather than as a failing immutability test.

## packages/core/src/schema.test.ts

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.test.ts:115` — No fixture in this file puts two format rules on one field, so the branch that reports the format collapse is never executed. Evidence: `schema.ts:111-117` filters `email`/`url`/`uuid` and pushes `formats.slice(0, -1)` as lossy; every fixture here (`:118-121`, `:206`) carries exactly one format rule, and the only other caller of `unexportableRules` (`tree.test.ts:1614`) uses one `email` rule plus a `minLength`. Failure scenario: delete `schema.ts:111-117` — the suite stays green while a field carrying both `email` and `uuid` exports only `format: 'uuid'` and the dialog reports the export as lossless, which is the single thing `unexportableRules` exists to prevent.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/schema.test.ts:168` — `titleOf`'s guard against a malformed `props` has no test; every fixture reaches it through the `field()` helper, which always sets `props`. Evidence: `schema.ts:204` (`if (!props || typeof props !== 'object' || Array.isArray(props)) return undefined`), whose comment records that an ungated document threw here inside the JSON dialog; `ComponentNode.props` is required (types.ts:152), so only a cast fixture can reach the branch, and none exists in this file. Failure scenario: delete the guard — no test fails, while `toJsonSchema` on a pasted document with `"props": null` throws `TypeError` in the one place someone is deliberately looking at a broken document.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/schema.test.ts:272` — The "root is itself a field" test covers only the non-repeater half of `schema.ts:302` (`if (root.schema && !isRowScope(root))`); a root that *is* a row scope silently falls to the `else` and flattens its row fields to the top level, untested.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/schema.test.ts:312` — The shared-`dataKey` test uses two fields with no rules, so the `!required.includes(name)` guard at `schema.ts:277` is never the thing preventing a duplicate; two required components sharing one data key would export `required: ['email','email']` without it and no test would notice.

## packages/core/src/json.test.ts

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/json.test.ts:114` — The `safeHref` describe cannot reach the scheme-allowlist fallback, so half of a security function's decision path is untested. Evidence: `json.ts:131-144` takes the `URL.parse` branch whenever `URL.parse` exists, which it does on Node 22 (the vitest environment for this package), so lines 143-144 run only for a scheme-bearing string the WHATWG parser rejects; every hostile fixture here (`javascript:`, `vbscript:`, `data:`, `file:`) parses successfully and is refused by line 132. ## Area 12 — tests: the core validation, condition, context, CSS and visibility suites

## packages/core/src/validation.test.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/validation.test.ts:830` — the `__proto__` fix is pinned for `validateForm` and never for `validateAcross`, where the identical bug is still live. Evidence: `validateForm` builds its result with `Object.create(null)` (validation.ts:488) precisely so `errors['__proto__'] = message` lands as an own key, and this test asserts it; `validateAcross` merges into a plain literal with `merged[key] ??= message` (validation.ts:705, 709). Verified with `node -e`: `Object.entries` of the null-prototype result yields `['__proto__','required']`, and `({})['__proto__'] ??= v` reads `Object.prototype`, which is not nullish, so nothing is assigned — merged own keys `[]`. Failure scenario: a multi-step form with a component keyed `toString`, `valueOf` or `hasOwnProperty` fails `required` on step one; the server calls `validateAcross`, gets `{}` back, and accepts the submit. No test in the suite goes red. See the CRITICAL under `packages/core/src/validation.ts`.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.test.ts:689` — "keeps the first message when two steps report the same field" never produces a collision, so the merge rule it names is untested. Evidence: the fixture's two components are keyed `nickname` and `nickname2` (lines 664, 675) and `FormErrors` is keyed by component key, not data key (validation.ts:496), so the two scopes contribute two distinct keys and the assertions at 690-691 hold trivially. Failure scenario: mutate `merged[key] ??= message` to `merged[key] = message` (last wins) at validation.ts:709 — the whole suite stays green. A fixture that would see it needs one component key visible under two scopes with different messages (e.g. a single node whose `required` rule carries a context-guarded `message`).
- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/validation.test.ts:53` — the block that the spec names as the enforcement of invariant 5 covers 6 of the 9 non-required rules and exactly one shape of "empty". Evidence: the `rules` array holds `email, url, uuid, minLength, min, pattern` (no `maxLength`, `max`, `custom`), and `it.each` feeds only `''` (line 63); `docs/spec/08-validation.md` states "`validation.test.ts` holds this under `every non-required rule passes on an empty value`, which runs the whole catalogue rather than one case", and defines empty as "`undefined`, `null`, a string that is blank once trimmed, or an empty array". No test in this file applies a non-required rule to `undefined` or `null` (`[]` appears only for `minLength`, line 726). Failure scenario: narrow validation.ts:383 to `if (value === '') return undefined` — `max` on an unanswered field now takes the `held === undefined` branch (validation.ts:395-396) and reports "Too large." on a blank optional field, and `email`/`url`/`uuid`/`pattern` all report on an absent key; every test here still passes, because the only fields fed to those rules through `validateField` also carry `required`, which fires first (lines 180, 188).
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/validation.test.ts:194` — the repeater row cap in `validateForm` is untested anywhere in the repository. Evidence: `rg 'MAX_ROWS|TOO_MANY_ROWS'` over the whole repo returns only validation.ts:92/95/522/523 and the re-export at index.ts:113-114 — no test file. Failure scenario: delete validation.ts:522-525 and a submitted body of `{"rows":[…1e6 items…]}` drives a million rule evaluations per nested level with nothing red; flip `>` to `<` and every repeater reports `TOO_MANY_ROWS` instead, which `rows.test.ts` would catch but the cap's own direction still would not.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/validation.test.ts:157` — no test ever inspects the third argument the engine hands the host's evaluator, so the context is free to be dropped on the way in. Evidence: every evaluator in this file is `(expr) => expr === 'true'` (136, 157) or `() => false` (279); none reads `context`. `guardPasses` calls `options.evaluate(when.expression, data, options.context)` (validation.ts:322) and the `custom` case does the same (validation.ts:416). Failure scenario: drop the third argument at validation.ts:322 — a `computed` guard reading `context.user.role` now evaluates against `undefined`, returns falsy, and the guarded `required` rule silently stops applying on the server; the suite stays green.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/validation.test.ts:167` — the guard-kind block exercises `static`, `computed` and (later, at 967) `condition` plus the unreadable default, but never `i18n`. Evidence: no occurrence of `kind: 'i18n'` in this file; `guardPasses` returns `true` for it at validation.ts:331-333, and that is the permissive direction the surrounding comment argues for. Failure scenario: change validation.ts:332 to `return false` and any rule carrying an `i18n` `when` stops applying, with no test red.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/validation.test.ts:290` — `isRequired` is exported from core and never called by any test. Evidence: `rg 'isRequired\('` finds only kit-mui/renderers.tsx:105,284,542 and kit-html/renderers.tsx:70; `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:363,383` drives it only with `validations: [{key:'required'}]` and `[]`. Failure scenario: drop `&& !rule.when` at validation.ts:448 and a conditionally-required field is announced to a screen reader as always mandatory — the case the function's own doc says it exists to avoid — with nothing red anywhere.

## packages/core/src/context.test.ts

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/context.test.ts:472` — the block for "the boot-time questions, on a document nobody checked" never nests a condition, so the depth bound these walks carry is unexercised. - **[LOW]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/context.test.ts:480` — the `it.each` asserts only `not.toThrow()` and covers three of the four boot-time exports, while its docstring says "The three exported functions". Evidence: `evaluatorKeys` is absent from lines 486-488 and gets one shallow fixture of its own at 457-464; `contextKeys`/`missingContext`/`contextValues` get no value assertion on a malformed document, only the shared control at 491.

## packages/core/src/condition.test.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/condition.test.ts:492` — "a condition nested beyond any reasonable depth / answers rather than throwing" nests only `not`, which is the one recursion in `evaluate` that propagates `depth`. Evidence: condition.ts:287 recurses as `evaluate(condition.condition, data, context, row, depth + 1)`, but the `and`/`or` case at condition.ts:280 calls `evaluate(inner, data, context, row)` with the depth argument omitted, so it defaults to `0` at every level and `if (depth > MAX_DEPTH)` (condition.ts:193) can never fire down an `and`/`or` chain. **(Verified against source: the `not` arm passes `depth + 1`, the `and`/`or` `.map` does not.)** Failure scenario: swap `deep()` at line 486 to build `{op:'and', conditions:[inner]}` instead of `{op:'not', …}` and the same 50 000 levels recurse unbounded — `RangeError` out of `evaluateCondition` → out of `isVisible` → out of `validateForm`, i.e. the unhandled 500 on submit and blank form this describe block says it exists to rule out.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/condition.test.ts:508` — "does not throw comparing deeply nested values either" nests objects, which is the one recursion in `same` that propagates `depth`. Evidence: condition.ts:43 recurses as `same(a[key], b[key], depth + 1)` for objects, while the array branch at condition.ts:32 calls `same(item, b[i])` with no depth, so the bound never applies to nested arrays. **(Verified against source.)** Failure scenario: a document comparing two `ref`s, `{op:'eq', left: ref('a'), right: ref('b')}`, against a submitted body where both `a` and `b` are equally deep nested arrays — `same` recurses once per level with no guard and throws. UNCERTAIN on the exact depth a request body can reach through `JSON.parse` before it overflows first; the document-literal path (two deep array literals) is not in doubt.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/condition.test.ts:122` — the numeric ordering fix is not defended: every ordering assertion uses finite integers that a subtraction would answer identically. Evidence: condition.ts:145-148 compares rather than subtracts, with a comment recording the regression ("`Infinity - Infinity` is `NaN`, so every comparison between two infinities answered false and `gte` on a pair of equal values hid the component") and a `Number.isNaN` guard; the suite's operands are `30`, `18`, `0`, `9`. No test anywhere in this file uses `Infinity` or `NaN`. Failure scenario: revert condition.ts:147 to `return a - b` — all of `gte 30/18`, `lt 30/18`, `lte 30/30`, `gt 30/30` still answer correctly and the file passes, while a host-supplied `context.limit = Infinity` compared `gte` against another infinity yields `NaN >= 0` → false → the component is hidden → its subtree drops out of `validateForm`.

## packages/core/src/css.test.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/css.test.ts:140` — the refusal path is tested for the `any` layer only; no test ever puts refusable text in a `mobile`, `tablet` or `desktop` entry. Evidence: line 141 is the sole fixture carrying `} body { display: none`, and it sits under `any`; every device-carrying fixture in the file holds `'padding: 4px'`, `'padding: 6px'` or `'color: blue'` (73, 91, 123, 134, 189). The device path calls `safeDeclarations` at a separate site, css.ts:209. Failure scenario: change css.ts:209 to `const declarations = css[name]?.trim()` — the whole file stays green while `css: { mobile: 'color:red}html{display:none' }` writes a page-wide rule inside the media query, and `mobile: 'x</style><script>…'` leaves the `<style>` element entirely. That is the exact injection css.ts:71 exists to stop, unguarded on three of four layers.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/css.test.ts:145` — `escapeKey`'s backslash case is never exercised. Evidence: the escape class is `/["\\<]|[\u0000-\u001f\u007f]/g` (css.ts:163); the keys and scopes used across this file are `card`, `a"] , body [x="`, `" b`, `a</style><script>x()</script>`, `a\n{}`, `inner`, `outer` — `"`, `<` and the control range are covered, `\` is not. Failure scenario: drop `\\` from the class and a node keyed `a\` emits `[data-fs-key="a\"]{…}`, where `\"` is an escaped quote, so the attribute string runs on past the closing quote and swallows the following rule's selector and declarations — one node's styling lands under another's, which is the failure the comment at css.ts:56-68 describes.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/css.test.ts:302` — "is not confused by an escaped quote inside a string" exercises the in-quote escape branch (css.ts:299) and not the out-of-quote one. Evidence: css.ts:308 is a separate `if (ch === '\\') i += 1` reached only when `quote === undefined`, added per its comment because "an escaped separator split a declaration that CSS reads as a single one"; the fixture `'content: "a\\"; b"; padding: 8px'` never leaves the quoted region. Failure scenario: delete css.ts:308 and `parseDeclarations('content: a\\; b')` splits one declaration into two, which `setDeclaration` then writes back — the silent-rewrite hazard the function was reshaped to avoid.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/css.test.ts:342` — `safeStyleValue` is pinned to refuse every fetching function (`url`, `image-set`, `element`, `cross-fade`) as exfiltration, and nothing states that `safeDeclarations` deliberately allows the same `url()` through the Style tab (css.ts:71 omits `FETCHING`); line 274 even asserts a `url(data:…)` survives `parseDeclarations`. The asymmetry is intentional per css.ts:113-119 but is asserted in neither direction as a policy.

## packages/core/src/visibility.test.ts

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/core/src/visibility.test.ts:23` — no test asserts that the host's evaluator receives `options.context`. Evidence: the evaluators here are `(expression) => expression === 'yes'` (24), `(_expression, data) => data['other'] === 'yes'` (121) and `() => true` (315) — the expression and the data are pinned, the third parameter is never read; `runs` passes it at visibility.ts:41. - **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/visibility.test.ts:81` — "validates nothing at all when the root it was given is itself hidden" blesses the fail-open as correct, and correctly says so, but the pairing that makes it safe is not in this file: the assertion that a host can detect the missing context lives in `context.test.ts:156-159`. Read alone, this test documents `validateForm(step, {}) === {}` as intended with no reference to the boot-time check that is the only thing standing between it and an accepted submit.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `packages/core/src/visibility.test.ts:267` — "never answers undefined" adds nothing over line 264: `toBe(true)` at 264 already excludes `undefined`, so the `typeof … === 'boolean'` assertion cannot fail unless 264 does first.

## packages/core/src/language.test.ts

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `packages/core/src/language.test.ts:27` — the only `findLanguage` fixture is a one-element list whose single entry has `dialect: ''`, so `languageTag(l)` and `l.code` are the same string and the distinction the module exists to defend is invisible (Trap 11). Evidence: `findLanguage` filters on `languageTag(language) === tag` (language.ts:24); with `[{code:'en', dialect:''}]`, the mutation `languages.find((l) => l.code === tag)` answers `'en'` → found and `'en-'` → undefined, so both assertions at 28-29 still pass. Failure scenario: that mutation is exactly the confusion language.ts:1-14 was written to end — the renderer reading `translations['en-US']` while the designer wrote `translations['en']`. A fixture matching what `createDocument` produces (`{code:'en', dialect:'US'}`, plus a second entry such as `en-GB`, looked up by `'en-US'`) would see it; nothing else in the repo calls `findLanguage` in a test.

Failure scenario: `:where(.fs-shell) input { padding: 5px 8px }` — the literal rule that broke MUI's notched outline — passes both `it`s at lines 189 and 200. Related: `selectors()` splits the prelude on every comma (line 27), so `:is(.fs-a, .fs-b) input` becomes two fragments, neither of which either check can classify. The shape is **not** in `designer.css` today: the only `:where` is `.fs-shell:where(:not([data-fs-theme='light']))` at line 49, a single compound with the class first, which both checks handle correctly.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `packages/designer/src/designer.css.test.ts:22-33` — `selectors()` cannot parse CSS nesting: for `.fs-shell { color: red; & .fs-x input { … } }` the captured head is `color: red;\n& .fs-x input`, which contains `.fs-` (so `escapesTheDesigner` is false) and whose ancestor compounds are `color:`, `red;`, `&`, `.fs-x` (so `reachesIntoTheForm` is false) — a reach into the form goes unseen. `designer.css` uses no nesting today, so this is a hole waiting for the first nested rule rather than a live defect.

## Area 15 — tests: the cross-cutting guard suites

## test/kit-contract.test.tsx

- **[CRITICAL]** **[E5]** `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:752-784` — the test enforcing the spec's "one obligation" (`02-kit-contract.md:34`, "every renderer spreads `nodeProps`") cannot tell a working renderer from one that throws, so a renderer can be deleted outright and the rule stays green. **(Verified: `NodeBoundary`'s fallback at `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:286-296` emits `role="alert"`, `data-fs-key`, `data-fs-type` and `data-fs-scope`; the render at `:766-774` passes no `onError`, so the boundary is silent and a caught error does not propagate out of `render()`.)** `packages/renderer/src/renderer.test.tsx:915-930` is committed proof of exactly this: it renders a throwing kit and then asserts `querySelector('[data-fs-key="boom"]')` has `data-fs-type === 'Boom'` — the same two assertions this contract rule makes. Failure scenario: replace the body of any non-container, non-data-bound renderer (`Heading`, `Text`, `Divider`, `Image`, `Link`, `Progress`, `Breadcrumbs`, `Alert`, `Button`) with `throw new Error()` — nothing in the per-kit block goes red, because `renders children inside every container` only covers containers and `shows an error message` only covers `dataBound`.
- **[CRITICAL]** **[E4]** `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:786-815` — same defect in the companion rule: `data-fs-scope` is read off the element, and the boundary fallback sets it (`packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:290`), so "carries the scope of the form it was rendered in" also passes for a renderer that never runs. Failure scenario: a kit that picks attributes out by name *and* throws is indistinguishable here from one that spreads correctly. The correct pattern — an `onError` spy plus `expect(role).not.toBe('alert')` on the target — already exists in this same file at `:577-593`.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:849-865` — "renders a data-bound component without crashing on an absent value" asserts `expect(() => render(...)).not.toThrow()`, which the error boundary makes unfailable. Evidence: `NodeBoundary` (`packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:268-297`) wraps every node at `:459`, so a renderer that dereferences an absent value is caught, not rethrown. Failure scenario: delete the null-guard in any data-bound renderer → this test stays green (the sibling at `:867` catches it only because the fallback text does not contain the error string, i.e. by accident of a different rule).
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:499-513` — `'$name gives an unrecognised Alert severity a role anyway'` never asserts a role; the only expectation is `expect(element.className).not.toContain('eror')`. Evidence: the assertion at `:512` is the whole body, and `Alert` is not `dataBound`, so the `role="alert"` rule at `:898-920` never reaches it. Failure scenario: a kit that keeps `severity === 'error'` as its only gate for `role="alert"` — the exact defect the comment at `:500-504` describes ("drew the banner and announced nothing") — passes this test.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:490`, `:602`, `:663`, `:680` — four bare `return`s turn a missing component or a shrunken option list into a pass, for the controls that exist to stop the primary tests being satisfied trivially. Evidence: `:490` `if (declared.length < 2) return` disables "still tells its own variants apart" whenever `Text.variant` declares fewer than two options, and `supplies options for every select prop` (`:738`) only requires one; `:602/:663/:680` disable all three `Select` controls if a kit drops `Select`. Failure scenario: narrow `Text.variant` to a single option → the narrowing test at `:463` and its control both pass while every variant collapses to the default. This file explicitly rejects the same pattern for `RadioGroup` at `:1005-1018` ("this used to return quietly … which is the one thing a contract suite must never let a kit do").
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:410-421`, `:568-573` — both `it.each` tables are built by filtering a hard-coded type list against what the kits declare, and an empty table registers zero tests silently. - **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `test/docs.test.ts:387-397` — the file states that a spec understating what is built is the drift that matters, and the guards do not cover the live instance of it: `docs/spec/02-kit-contract.md:130` says "no test checks that a label points at its control, that `aria-invalid` is set, or that a focus indicator survives", while `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:317-351` runs an `aria-invalid` check against every kit (and `:353-391` runs `aria-required`, which the rule table at `02-kit-contract.md:85-105` also omits). Failure scenario: a kit author reads the spec, believes `aria-invalid` is on trust, and is surprised by a red build — or, worse, deletes the check as unspecified.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `test/docs.test.ts:287-333` — the accessibility document's contrast table is checked only for the *existence* of the files it names (`:321-332`), never for the figures it quotes, and one row is already wrong. **(Verified independently: `--fsh-warning` `#b26a00` on `--fsh-surface` `#ffffff` computes to 4.24:1 with the repo's own formula, and that pair *is* asserted at `test/contrast.test.ts:225`, against a documented "lowest pair" of 4.83:1 at `docs/accessibility.md:35`.)** Failure scenario: any token change that stays above the floor silently invalidates all seven figures in `docs/accessibility.md:31-44`, which is the one table a reader uses to judge the claim.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `test/docs.test.ts:79-106,335-345` — `.claude/skills/write-tests/SKILL.md` is in the link-checking list at `:344` but in neither count list, and it carries a stale present-tense figure: `SKILL.md:239` says "Run `pnpm e2e` too. 65 of these checks…" while `README.md:71`, `CLAUDE.md:63` and `docs/ROADMAP.md:59` all say 68. **(Verified: `grep -c "^\s*test("` across the eight e2e spec files totals exactly 68.)** `browserTotals()` at `:80` would not match that phrasing even if the file were listed. Failure scenario: the document `CLAUDE.md` tells every contributor to read before writing a test contradicts the three documents the guard does police, and nothing reports it.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `test/docs.test.ts:400-421` — `sourceFiles()` enumerates `packages/*/src` with `readdirSync` and then `readFileSync`s each entry, while `test/eslint.config.test.ts:110` writes `__guardrail_fixture_*__.ts`/`.tsx` into `packages/core/src` and `packages/designer/src` and removes them in a `finally` (`:124-126`). Vitest runs test files in parallel by default (`vitest.config.ts` sets no `fileParallelism: false`). Failure scenario: a fixture is enumerated by `readdirSync` and deleted before `read(path)` runs → `ENOENT` throws out of `mentions()` and every one of the nine `DISPROOF` cases fails, on a run where nothing changed.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `test/docs.test.ts:239,268,301` — three counts are derived by slicing to the end of a file and matching text: `spec.slice(spec.indexOf('| Rule'))` runs past the table so any later pipe-prefixed line inflates `rows`; `matchAll(/^\s+it(?:\.each)?\(/gm)` counts source text, so a commented-out `it(` counts and converting the nine axe states at `test/a11y.test.tsx:154-264` into a single `it.each` would collapse the count to 1 and demand the doc say "one states".

## test/contrast.test.ts

- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `test/contrast.test.ts:152-166` — "the designer own controls have a visible boundary" asserts only `--fs-control-border`, while several real designer controls draw their only boundary with the unasserted `--fs-border` at 1.39:1 light / 1.41:1 dark — below the 3:1 the test's own comment cites for WCAG 1.4.11. Evidence: `packages/designer/src/designer.css:486` `.fs-dialog__editor` (the JSON `<textarea>`, `border: 1px solid var(--fs-border)` on `--fs-bg-well`), `:519` `.fs-dialog__actions button, .fs-dialog__file`, `:651` `.fs-languages__add button`, `:1235` `.fs-color__swatch`, `:1256` `.fs-icon-picker__preview`; computing `ratio('#d7dbe0','#ffffff')` with `test/contrast.ts` gives 1.39, and `ratio('#3a3f46','#24272c')` gives 1.41. `--fs-border` is the only one of the designer palette's 12 colour tokens with no assertion anywhere in the file. Failure scenario: the guard was written against the token that was fixed, not against the controls, so the same defect the comment at `:153-161` describes ("at 1.39:1 it was not saying it") is still shipping on five control classes and the suite is green.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `test/contrast.test.ts:103-252` + `test/contrast.ts:84` — the suite can only see colours declared as custom properties (`if (trimmed.startsWith('--')) tokens[...]`), so a hex literal written beside a rule is invisible to every assertion, and nothing forbids one. Evidence: the comment at `:217-222` records that exact incident ("the success and warning stripes were hex literals sitting beside their rules … outside this suite, which reads tokens"), and no test asserts that either stylesheet is free of hex outside its token blocks. Failure scenario: add `border-color: #e8eaed` to `.fs-dialog__actions button` — a failing 1.2:1 boundary that no assertion in this file can reach.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `test/contrast.ts:57-59` — every threshold is compared against a value already rounded to two places (`Math.round(contrast(a,b)*100)/100`), so a true ratio of 4.4951:1 rounds to 4.5 and clears `toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(AA_TEXT)`. The maths itself is correct against WCAG 2.x: `contrast.ts:38-44` implements the sRGB linearisation with the `0.03928` / `12.92` branch, the `((c+0.055)/1.055)^2.4` curve and the `0.2126/0.7152/0.0722` coefficients, `:48-53` applies the `+0.05` offset to both terms, and `:279-313` pins published reference values and the branch boundary so a drifted formula cannot satisfy them.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `test/contrast.test.ts:103-109,196-202` — the suite now measures three palettes per stylesheet (light, chosen dark, system dark) while `docs/accessibility.md:13` still describes the check as "both stylesheets, in both themes" and its table at `:31-36` has only two rows per stylesheet; nothing compares the two.

## test/a11y.test.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `test/a11y.test.tsx:171-182,184-191,217-234` — four of the nine states change the UI and then assert nothing about the state they claim to have reached, so if a tab click stopped switching panels the audit would silently re-measure the previous DOM under the new test's name. Evidence: each body is `getByRole('tab', …)` → `user.click` → `expect(await audit(container)).toEqual([])`; `getByRole` throws only if the *tab* is missing, never if the panel failed to render. Failure scenario: a regression that leaves the Style panel unmounted makes "has none on the Style tab" audit the at-rest tree and pass, which is the state the comment at `:227-232` says was the point of adding it.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `test/a11y.test.tsx:128-138` — axe never runs over `@formsmith/kit-mui`. Evidence: `renderDesigner()` hard-codes `htmlKit`/`htmlRendererKit`, and `grep -rl axe packages/ test/ e2e/` finds no other suite that renders a MUI form under axe. `docs/accessibility.md:82-90` puts kit accessibility entirely on the kit, and `02-kit-contract.md:107-111` records that `kit-mui` had failed both written obligations for as long as the file existed. Failure scenario: any ARIA regression in the MUI renderers — the kit most consumers will use — is caught only by the hand-written rules in `test/kit-contract.test.tsx`, which is the coverage the file header at `:12-13` says axe exists to supplement.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `test/a11y.test.tsx:140-152,256-263` — "has none with the JSON dialog open" audits a dialog that is not modal. Evidence: the `beforeAll` polyfill defines `showModal()` as `this.open = true`, so there is no top layer, no inert background and no `::backdrop`; `docs/accessibility.md:73-75` claims focus trapping, Escape, the inert background and the backdrop all come from the browser, which is precisely what jsdom does not provide. `write-tests/SKILL.md:122-124` names `inert` and "a `<dialog>`'s Escape handling" as things that belong in `e2e/`. Failure scenario: a regression that renders the dialog non-modal (or leaves the background focusable) is invisible here and there is no corresponding `e2e/` audit.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `test/a11y.test.tsx:34` — `NEEDS_LAYOUT` restates the three disabled rule ids that `docs/accessibility.md:107-108` also lists, with nothing comparing them; `test/docs.test.ts:290-319` checks only the *number of states*, so disabling a fourth rule here would go unmentioned in the document that exists to say what is and is not checked.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `test/a11y.test.tsx:112-126,155-157` — `audit()` returns only `results.violations` and nothing asserts the audited subtree is non-empty or that axe applied any rule, so a `Designer` that rendered an empty container would pass all nine states.

## test/kit-css-injection.test.tsx

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `test/kit-css-injection.test.tsx:153-159` — the MUI half of "refuses a value that fetches" currently asserts against an empty stylesheet. Evidence: `safeStyleValue` (`packages/core/src/css.ts:133-139`) returns `undefined` for both `INJECTION` and `EXFILTRATION`, so `BoxNode`'s `sx` (`packages/kit-mui/src/renderers.tsx:392-398`) is byte-identical in the two cases; emotion caches by content hash and will not re-insert a rule it believes is already on the page — the mechanism `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:188-194` documents in this repo's own words — while `beforeEach` at `:131-135` has already removed the tag the first test inserted. So `emittedRules(EXFILTRATION)` returns `[]`, `[].map(...).join('')` is `''`, and `not.toContain('evil.example')` passes having read nothing. Failure scenario: the assertion cannot distinguish "sanitised" from "not emitted", and unlike its sibling at `:142` it has no `toHaveLength(1)` floor; reordering the three cases or adding a fourth that shares a sanitised `sx` extends the same blindness to the injection assertion at `:140-142`.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `test/kit-css-injection.test.tsx:123-128` — `serverStyleAttribute` takes the first `style="…"` occurring *after* `data-fs-key="target"` in the markup, which depends on `{...nodeProps}` being spread before `style` in the kit's JSX (`packages/kit-html/src/renderers.tsx:418-425`). A kit that ordered them the other way would make this read `''` (or a descendant's attribute), and the two `not.toMatch`/`not.toContain` assertions at `:165-166,174` would pass on it; only "keeps a legitimate colour" at `:170` would object.

- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `test/eslint.config.test.ts:91-101,139` — the single control is `core` importing `node:fs`, and a file ESLint declines to lint yields `[]` via `byPath.get(...) ?? []`, which is indistinguishable from a clean lint for the control case. There is no control proving the restriction does not over-fire where the import is legitimate (`packages/kit-mui` importing `@mui/material`, `packages/renderer` importing `react`), so a config that restricted a library repo-wide would be reported green here and caught only by `pnpm lint` on real source.

## Area 16 — tests: the renderer suite and the Playwright suite

## packages/renderer/src/renderer.test.tsx
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/renderer/src/renderer.test.tsx:678` — `falls back to the key when the bundle for the language is not one` cannot fail, for two independent reasons. (2) The assertion reads `textContent` of the node rendered by `kit`, whose `Field` (lines 28-39) never renders `props` at all — it draws an `<input>` and an error `<span>` — so the string `'not-a-bundle'` could not appear even if `resolveProp` returned it. The index-keyed `<Row key={index}>` and its documented cost (`index.tsx:406-423`: after deleting row 0 the instance that drew row 0 draws row 1, taking browser-owned state — file input contents, IME composition, selection, scroll — with it) has no failing case anywhere.

## e2e/harness.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `e2e/harness.ts:93` — `rootChildren()` and `document()` read `localStorage`, which the playground writes from a **passive** effect (`Designer.tsx:99-101` calls `emit.current?.(api.document)` in a `useEffect`, which reaches `App.tsx:83`'s `persist`), and every caller asserts with a non-retrying `expect(await …)`. Evidence: React schedules passive effects after commit, while Playwright's `click()`/`press()` resolve on event dispatch. Failure scenario: the five specs asserting `toEqual(before)` to show a gesture changed nothing (`canvas.spec.ts:168`, `keyboard.spec.ts:92`, `keyboard.spec.ts:177`, `document.spec.ts:40`, `document.spec.ts:58`) are biased toward passing under this race — the stale read is exactly the old value they expect. The fix shape is `expect.poll(() => designer.rootChildren())`.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `e2e/harness.ts:161` — `stableBox`'s error message, `'Cannot drag something that is not on screen.'`, advertises a guard that does not exist: `boundingBox()` returns `null` only for an element with no box (detached or `display: none`), never for one below the fold. Evidence: the comment on `dragOnto` (lines 176-183) says precisely that an off-screen palette entry "yields a perfectly plausible box". So the one condition the message names is the one condition this branch cannot detect.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `e2e/harness.ts:114` — `openTab` uses an unscoped `page.getByRole('tab', { name })` across both tablists, with Playwright's default substring name matching. The moment a tab name in one pane becomes a substring of one in the other, this resolves to two elements — Trap 4's `getByRole('tablist')` incident with the panes reversed.
- **[NOTE]** **[E4]** `e2e/harness.ts:83` — `node()` is `.first()`, which silently means "row 0" inside a repeater. Any `data-fs-row` assertion made through it is partly guaranteed by the locator rather than by the renderer.
