## e2e/canvas.spec.ts
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `e2e/canvas.spec.ts:80` — `selecting a field does not focus it or type into it` does not check the "type into it" half. Evidence: `.click()` triggers `onMouseDownCapture`'s `preventDefault` (`Canvas.tsx:322-326`), which keeps focus off the input, so `page.keyboard.type('typed')` is delivered to `<body>` and never reaches the canvas at all. The code that implements the claim — `onKeyDownCapture` at `Canvas.tsx:344-360`, written because "tabbing into the canvas and typing reached the kit's own `onChange`… and then the preview shows answers nobody entered" — is exercised by nothing: `grep -rn "keydown"` over `packages/**/*.test.*` and `e2e/` finds only `drag.test.ts`'s Escape dispatches, and no e2e spec ever presses Tab. Failure scenario: delete `onKeyDownCapture` entirely; this test stays green, and an author who tabs into a canvas field seeds preview data while believing they are arranging a layout.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `e2e/canvas.spec.ts:126` — the hand-rolled drag reads `palette.boundingBox()` with nothing scrolled into view, bypassing `dragOnto`'s `scrollIntoViewIfNeeded` (`harness.ts:199`) — the exact Trap 13 incident, in the file the incident is named after. Evidence: `groupKit` preserves declaration order, so `definitions.ts` puts the whole `layout` group (Stack, Grid, Step, Repeater, Box, Card) and the `display` group (Heading, Text, Divider, Image) ahead of `Text field` in a two-column grid inside a scrollable `.fs-panel` (`designer.css:145-152`); `Checkbox`, one group further down, is the entry that already failed every CI run this way. Failure scenario: one more `layout` or `display` entry pushes Text field below 720px, `boundingBox()` returns a plausible rect, `mouse.down()` presses outside the window, the gesture becomes a text selection, and the failure surfaces as a wrong document several steps downstream.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `e2e/canvas.spec.ts:113` — `dragging a component on the canvas moves it` drops on the bottom of the last child, so a `resolveDrop` that ignored the pointer and always returned `index = children.length` satisfies it. The only spec that pins a mid-list index is line 96 (top of `email`, expecting index 2), and it runs at desktop only.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `e2e/canvas.spec.ts:93` — `toHaveCount(0)` on `.MuiPopover-root, .MuiMenu-root` is a negative web-first assertion that resolves the instant it is true, so it can pass before a popover that mounts on a later tick appears. It holds today only because MUI's Select opens from `onMouseDown`.

## e2e/keyboard.spec.ts
- **[HIGH]** **[E5]** `e2e/keyboard.spec.ts:7` — the file header claims "only a browser can check that focus really lands where it should **and that the tab order is what a user meets**", and no spec in `e2e/` ever presses Tab. Evidence: `grep -rn "press('Tab')\|'Shift+Tab'" e2e/` returns nothing. Tab order is one of the three things `CLAUDE.md` says can be checked nowhere else (jsdom has no layout and no real focus ring), so it is checked nowhere at all. Failure scenario: any change that puts the canvas, a pane handle, or the drag layer into a wrong position in the document order — or gives a focusable element a positive `tabindex` — passes every suite.

## e2e/repeater.spec.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `e2e/repeater.spec.ts:49` — no test anywhere with a layout engine ever draws a repeater with more than one row; the file's own comment records that nothing in the designer can add one. Evidence: `geometry.ts:60` (`rowFromEventTarget`), `geometry.ts:77-86` (row-scoped `nodeElement`) and `geometry.ts:177-189` (one candidate per drawn element) were all written for the bug where "the outline, the label and the toolbar all appeared around row 0 whichever row was clicked", and `geometry.test.ts` exercises them in jsdom, where every rectangle is zero. The rectangle half — which row's box a click lands in and which row the frame is drawn around — is checked by nothing.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `e2e/repeater.spec.ts:46` — `designer.node('textField')` is `.first()` (`harness.ts:83`), so `toHaveAttribute('data-fs-row', 'repeater[0]')` is guaranteed by the locator as soon as any row exists; it can never observe a renderer that numbers rows >= 1 wrongly. Same at line 109 and line 121.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `e2e/repeater.spec.ts:87` — `.fs-canvas__data pre` `.first()` is the Data section (`Canvas.tsx:605-608`), which holds `JSON.stringify({})` in a freshly opened preview (`Canvas.tsx:148`), so `not.toContainText('repeater')` holds for any build, including one where the repeater renders nothing at all.

## e2e/style.spec.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `e2e/style.spec.ts:37` — `expect(await styleOf(...)).toBe('24px')` is a plain assertion with no retry, taken immediately after `press('Enter')` and depending on a React commit plus a `<style>` insertion. Evidence: `press()` resolves on event dispatch; Playwright ships `toHaveCSS`, which polls, and it is not used. Same shape at lines 38, 54, 57 and 106.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `e2e/style.spec.ts:77` — unscoped `getByRole('alert')`, with the same latent ambiguity as `authoring.spec.ts:46` (`renderers.tsx:118` gives an errored helper text `role="alert"`).

## e2e/viewport.spec.ts
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `e2e/viewport.spec.ts:82` — the file's only drop-targeting check at mobile width drops on the bottom of the *last* child, so both assertions (`toHaveLength(before.length)` and last-is-`heading`) are satisfied by any `resolveDrop` that ignored the pointer entirely and returned `{ parentKey: root, index: children.length }`. Evidence: `dnd.ts:174-186` computes the slot from child midpoints, and the only spec that pins a mid-list index (`canvas.spec.ts:96`, top of `email` → index 2) runs at desktop. The header's claim — "these check the arithmetic still lands where the user aimed once the canvas is 390px wide" — is therefore unchecked for every position but the end. Failure scenario: a frame-offset bug at 390px that shifts every measured rect by the frame's left margin sends every mid-list drop to the wrong index and this suite stays green.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `e2e/viewport.spec.ts:33` — `surface.width - frameBox.width <= 64` only rules out a constraint tighter than the surface's own 48px of padding (`designer.css:362`), and the test never sets a viewport, so what it can detect depends on the width Playwright's `Desktop Chrome` device happens to give the surface. A `max-width` wider than that surface passes a test named "constrains nothing".

## e2e/hidden.spec.ts
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `e2e/hidden.spec.ts:57` — the hand-rolled drag reads `palette.boundingBox()` with nothing scrolled into view, bypassing `dragOnto` (`harness.ts:199`); identical exposure to `canvas.spec.ts:126` and identical to the Trap 13 incident. The press point is computed from a box that `boundingBox()` will report just as plausibly when the entry is below the fold.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `e2e/hidden.spec.ts:26` — `await expect(designer.node('name')).toBeVisible()` is true of every component on the canvas: nothing in the designer draws with `data-fs-hidden`. Evidence: `grep -rn "fs-hidden"` over `packages/` and `apps/` finds it only in the renderer that emits it and in tests that assert it; `designer.css` has no rule for it, and `docs/spec/05-canvas.md:207` records this as open ("The attribute is in the DOM; nothing draws with it yet"). So "says it is hidden" is a claim about the DOM only, and the spec would read identically if an author could never tell the two apart on screen.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `e2e/hidden.spec.ts:40` — the control assertion runs before anything has been hidden, when no element in the document carries the attribute, so on its own it is satisfied by a build that never emits `data-fs-hidden`; only the `not.toHaveCount(0)` at line 46 makes the pair meaningful, and the file comment says so.

## Area 17 — the specification

## docs/spec/README.md
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/README.md:54` — The index's own rule ("A file's status is the weakest of its sections, not an average, and it has to match what the file itself opens with") is violated by `03-components.md`, which is listed and opens as **Built** while carrying a section marked **Status: partial**. Evidence: spec line 54 states the rule and line 36 lists `03-components.md` as `Built`; `docs/spec/03-components.md:3` opens `**Status: built.**` while `docs/spec/03-components.md:273` reads `## Steps` / `**Status: partial.** The core of it is built.` Failure scenario: a reader scanning the index for what is safe to depend on is told the whole component catalogue is verified, and builds a wizard on the Steps section without reading that Next/Back buttons and a progress indicator do not exist.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `docs/spec/README.md:58` — The sentence enumerating which files read Partial omits `01`, which the table two lines earlier marks Partial. Evidence: line 58 says "`05`, `06`, `07`, `08`, `11` and `12` read Partial because each has a named gap in it"; line 34 lists `01-document-model.md` as `Partial`.
- **[LOW]** **[E3]** `docs/spec/README.md:108` — The "Keeping it true" paragraph describes `test/docs.test.ts` as doing four things, and materially understates it while overstating one. Evidence: the paragraph claims the rule table is compared against "as many rows as the contract suite has tests", but `test/docs.test.ts:265-267` deliberately slices only the `describe.each(KITS)` block and stops at the next top-level `describe`, so roughly thirty further tests in that file are not counted; the paragraph also omits the whole "a section that calls itself planned is still planned" guard (`test/docs.test.ts:512-618`) and the component-catalogue/kit cross-check (`:430-488`). Failure scenario: a contributor adding a `**Status: planned**` section does not know a table in `docs.test.ts` must gain an entry, and the build fails for a reason the specification never mentioned.

## docs/spec/00-product.md
No issues found. (Checked: axe runs in nine states — `test/a11y.test.tsx:154` opens `describe('axe audit of the designer'` with nine `it(` cases; `apps/demo-server/src` declares exactly five routes; peer ranges and `engines` are as claimed.)

## docs/spec/01-document-model.md
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/01-document-model.md:52` — The Versioning section states "`version` is the literal `'1'` and nothing reads it", which the file's own preamble and the code both contradict. Evidence: line 52 says nothing reads it, while lines 11-12 of the same file say `FormDocument.version` came off the unread list "when the gate began refusing a document that does not say `'1'`"; `packages/core/src/tree.ts:645` is `if (candidate['version'] !== '1')` followed by a `malformed(...)` push, so `validateDocument` rejects any other value. Failure scenario: a host reads "nothing reads it", ships a document written with `version: 2` or omits the field, and every paste and every `validateDocument` call on the server reports a malformed document for a field the spec said was inert.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/01-document-model.md:132` — The stated refusal set for `safeDeclarations` is incomplete: CSS comment delimiters are refused too. Evidence: line 132 says "`safeDeclarations` refuses anything holding `{`, `}`, `<` or `@`"; `packages/core/src/css.ts:71` is `const FORBIDDEN = /[{}<@]|\/\*|\*\//`, and the comment above it explains that an unbalanced comment swallows other nodes' rules in the concatenated sheet. Failure scenario: an author annotates a style block with `/* brand blue */`, the whole block is silently dropped, and the spec gives no reason why — the same "appears to work" failure this file's own principles name as worst.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/01-document-model.md:105` — "So `FormData` is keyed by data key and `FormErrors` by component key" is only true outside a repeater; inside one an error key is a row address, not a component key. Evidence: `packages/core/src/validation.ts:495` writes `errors[addressOf(path, node.key)] = error`, and `packages/core/src/rows.ts:76-79` prefixes the path, producing `addresses[1].street`. `docs/spec/12-api.md:100-102` documents this; `01` and `08` do not. Failure scenario: a host writes `errors[node.key]` lookups against a form containing a repeater, finds no error for any row field, and ships a form that displays nothing on a rejected submit.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `docs/spec/01-document-model.md:105` — The code's own doc comment for `FormData` contradicts this spec line. Evidence: spec line 105 says `FormData` is keyed by data key; `packages/core/src/types.ts:185` reads `/** Runtime form data, keyed by component key. */`. The spec is the correct one (`packages/core/src/validation.ts:459` reads through `dataKeyOf(node)`). Failure scenario: a contributor reading the type rather than the spec builds a host that keys data by `key` and silently loses every answer on a field carrying a `dataKey`.

## docs/spec/02-kit-contract.md
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/02-kit-contract.md:129` — The file states that no test checks `aria-invalid`, and one does, against every registered kit. Evidence: lines 128-130 read "The third and fourth bullets are executed by the contract test. The other three are not: no test checks that a label points at its control, that `aria-invalid` is set, or that a focus indicator survives." `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:317` is `it.each(KITS)('$name marks a failing control invalid', ...)`, which renders every data-bound component with an error, reads `target.getAttribute('aria-invalid')` at `:344`, and asserts `expect(missing).toEqual([])` at `:349`. Failure scenario: a kit author, told this is a trust-based obligation, defers it — and their first `pnpm test` fails on a rule the contract document explicitly said was not executed. It is also the exact drift class `README.md:20-27` and `ROADMAP.md:66` claim was already swept out of this file.
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/02-kit-contract.md:118` — The five-bullet "Accessibility is the kit's job" list — presented as what "A kit must" do — omits an obligation the contract suite enforces against every kit: marking a required control required. Evidence: the bullets at lines 119-124 name `<label for>`, `aria-invalid`, a persistent `aria-describedby` target, `role="alert"`, and a focus indicator. `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:353` is `it.each(KITS)('$name marks a required control required', ...)`, checking `aria-required="true"` or the `required` attribute at `:365-368`; core exports `isRequired` for exactly this (`packages/core/src/index.ts:118`, `packages/core/src/validation.ts:446`). Failure scenario: a kit author implements all five documented bullets, adds their kit to `KITS` as step 3 of "Writing your own kit" tells them to, and fails a WCAG 3.3.2 rule that appears nowhere in the contract.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E3]** `docs/spec/02-kit-contract.md:82` — "Rules every kit must satisfy … Each is a test in `test/kit-contract.test.tsx`, run against every registered kit" presents the 19-row table as the complete obligation set, but roughly thirteen further `it.each(KITS)` tests also run against every registered kit. Evidence: beyond the per-kit `describe.each(KITS)` block that begins at `test/kit-contract.test.tsx:698`, `it.each(KITS)` cases sit at `:317`, `:353`, `:377`, `:463`, `:478`, `:499`, `:515`, `:525`, `:535`, `:596`, `:662`, `:678` and `:1071`, covering `aria-invalid`, `aria-required`, `Text` variant classes, unrecognised `Alert` severity, an unsafe `Image` src scheme, malformed option lists, and numeric select values. The guard that is supposed to keep the table honest cannot see them: `test/docs.test.ts:265-267` slices from `describe.each(KITS)` to the next top-level `describe` on purpose. Failure scenario: a third-party kit author budgets against nineteen obligations, adds their kit to the list as instructed, and gets a failing suite naming rules that are not written down anywhere.

- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/06-inspector.md:159` — The Style tab's stated refusal set omits CSS comment delimiters, which are refused. No issues found. ## docs/spec/08-validation.md
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/08-validation.md:68` — The definition of "empty" omits an empty object, which the code treats as empty. Evidence: line 68 reads "**Empty** means `undefined`, `null`, a string that is blank once trimmed, or an empty array"; `packages/core/src/json.ts:170` is `if (typeof value === 'object') return Object.keys(value).length === 0`, and the comment above it records that `{"consent": {}}` used to satisfy a required consent before that line existed. Failure scenario: a host reading this spec builds a payload shape where a field holds `{}` as a sentinel, and every non-`required` rule on it silently passes while `required` fails — behaviour the spec's own enumeration says cannot happen.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/08-validation.md:64` — The rule table says `url` fails when "`URL.canParse` rejects it", but the rule also refuses any scheme outside an allowlist. Evidence: the table row names only `URL.canParse`; `packages/core/src/validation.ts:304-306` is `return URL.canParse(value) && safeHref(value) !== undefined`, and `packages/core/src/json.ts:101` allows only `http:`, `https:`, `mailto:`, `tel:`. Failure scenario: a host mirrors the documented rule on its own server with a bare `URL.canParse` check, accepts `javascript:…` in a field the browser rejected, and the two validators disagree in the permissive direction — the exact class of divergence this file's Server parity section is about.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/08-validation.md:80` — "Every failure degrades rather than throws" lists four behaviours and omits three real refusals, two of which make a legitimate value or pattern fail. Evidence: `packages/core/src/validation.ts:227` returns `false` for any value longer than `PATTERN_INPUT_LIMIT` (4096, `:125`); `:228` returns `false` for any pattern with a nested unbounded quantifier, so `(a+)+` never matches anything; `:369` returns `UNREADABLE_RULE` — an *error message* — for a rule key the code does not recognise, which the "Every other rule passes when the value is empty" statement at line 21 does not admit. Failure scenario: an author writes `(\w+)+@example\.com`, every address fails with "Invalid format", and nothing in the spec explains that the engine refused the pattern rather than the value.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/08-validation.md:143` — The API section documents `validateField`, `validateForm`, `applyRule` and `EvalOptions`, and never mentions the row cap that can take a whole repeater out of validation. Evidence: `packages/core/src/validation.ts:522-525` — when a repeater's submitted list exceeds `MAX_ROWS` (5000, `:92`) it records `TOO_MANY_ROWS` against the repeater and `return`s without validating a single row; both constants are public exports (`packages/core/src/index.ts:114-115`). Failure scenario: a server operator sizing a request-body limit has no way to learn from the specification that a body above the cap produces one generic error instead of per-field ones, and treats the resulting 422 as a bug.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/08-validation.md:165` — "`FormErrors` is keyed by component key, and stays that way now `dataKey` exists" is true only outside a repeater. Evidence: `packages/core/src/validation.ts:495` writes `errors[addressOf(path, node.key)]`, producing `addresses[1].street` inside a row; `docs/spec/12-api.md:100-102` states the address form. Failure scenario: as under `01-document-model.md` — a host looks errors up by bare key and displays nothing for any field inside a repeater.

## docs/spec/12-api.md
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/12-api.md:239` — The public API document names `rowScopeProblems` as the boot-time check for row-scope mismatches, and it is not exported from `@formsmith/core`. **(Verified: `packages/core/src/index.ts:67-79` re-exports eleven names from `./rows.js` and `rowScopeProblems` is not among them; the file contains no `export *`.)** Evidence: line 239 reads "`rowScopeProblems` reports the three mismatches at boot rather than letting a condition read nothing and quietly hide a component", sitting in a section whose sibling boot check `missingContext` *is* exported. The function is defined at `packages/core/src/rows.ts:232` and is reachable only indirectly through `validateDocument` (`packages/core/src/tree.ts:1132`), which reports it as a `'row-scope'` `StructuralError`. Failure scenario: a host writes `import { rowScopeProblems } from '@formsmith/core'` next to its `missingContext` assertion, the build fails, and — finding no alternative named in the specification — the host ships with no boot-time check at all, which is precisely the silent-permissive failure the section exists to prevent.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/12-api.md:62` — The `FormRenderer` prop table, presented as the complete surface of the package most consumers install, omits `nonce` and `onError`. Evidence: the table lists ten props; `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:538` declares `nonce?: string` and `:529` declares `onError?: (error: Error, key: string) => void`, both destructured at `:559-563` and used at `:620` and `:459`. The `nonce` omission is the costly one: `:620` renders the per-component stylesheet as `<style {...(nonce === undefined ? {} : { nonce })}>`, and a host serving `style-src 'self'` drops that element with no error. Failure scenario: a host with a CSP nonce policy embeds the renderer, every authored `node.css` rule silently disappears from the page, and the specification gives them no prop to look for.
- **[LOW]** **[E4]** `docs/spec/12-api.md:16` — Undocumented public exports across all three packages. Evidence: `@formsmith/designer` exports `useDesigner`, `DesignerApi` and `RenameResult` (`packages/designer/src/index.ts:17-18`) which no spec file describes, though `MAX_HISTORY` from the same line is documented at `12-api.md:299`; `@formsmith/core` exports `evaluatorKeys` (`packages/core/src/index.ts:61`, defined at `packages/core/src/context.ts:133`) — the stated sibling of the documented `contextKeys`/`missingContext` boot checks — plus `MAX_ROWS`, `TOO_MANY_ROWS`, `UNREADABLE_RULE`, `keyScopesFor` and `decodeAddress`, none of which appear in any contract. Failure scenario: a host that injects an evaluator on the client but not the server has no documented way to discover `evaluatorKeys`, which exists to catch exactly that asymmetry.
- **[LOW]** **[E2]** `docs/spec/12-api.md:66` — The `data` prop is described as "keyed by node", which is the one wording `01-document-model.md` exists to rule out. Evidence: line 66 reads "Current values, keyed by node"; `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:372` computes `const dataKey = dataKeyOf(node)` and `:466` reads the value as `valueOf(row?.values ?? data, dataKey)`. Failure scenario: a host keys its state object by `key` rather than `dataKey`, and every field on which an author set a `dataKey` renders empty and never receives its value back.

Evidence: `aliases.ts:18` (`find: '@formsmith/designer/styles.css'`) and `aliases.ts:26` (`find: '@formsmith/kit-html/styles.css'`) are plain strings, deliberately, because they are the exact specifiers the regex entries must not swallow. The 1500 unit count could not be counted read-only (`it.each` expands at runtime), but `test/count-reporter.ts:112-133` fails the process on a mismatch, so the figure is machine-enforced — subject to the two ways that reporter disables itself, reported under `test/count-reporter.ts`.

## packages/renderer/README.md
- **[HIGH]** **[E4]** `packages/renderer/README.md:22` — The `onChange` handler in the published usage snippet is the exact anti-pattern the source warns against by name, and it silently destroys data. **(Verified: the README line reads `onChange={(key, value) => setData((d) => ({ ...d, [key]: value }))}`.)** `packages/renderer/src/index.tsx:538-549` documents that same prop as `onChange: (address: string, value: JsonValue) => void` and says "The parameter is named `address` rather than `key` because the old name is an invitation to write `{ ...data, [key]: value }`, which then creates a property called `addresses[1].line1` and silently drops every answer in the repeater. Write it with `applyChange` from `@formsmith/core`." Inside a repeater the first argument is `addresses[1].line1`, so the spread creates a literal property of that name and every row edit is lost. Failure scenario: a host copies the README's handler, the form works until someone adds a `Repeater`, and then every keystroke in a row is written to a junk key and never reaches the payload — with no error anywhere.
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E2]** `packages/renderer/README.md:10` — The install line omits a package the snippet imports. Evidence: L10 is `npm install @formsmith/renderer @formsmith/core`; L15 imports `@formsmith/kit-mui`, which is neither a dependency nor a peer dependency of `@formsmith/renderer`. Failure scenario: same as the designer README — the snippet fails on its second import.
- Verified true, no finding: `FormRenderer` and the `document`/`kit`/`data`/`errors` props exist; the `NodeRendererProps` fields destructured at L31 all exist and the kit-level `onChange` really is `(value: JsonValue) => void`, so `onChange(e.target.value)` is right; "computed props resolve to `null`" matches `index.tsx:170` exactly.

## packages/kit-mui/README.md
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/kit-mui/README.md:18` — The component list is wrong by 19 components. **(Verified: `grep -c "^\s*type: '"` over `packages/kit-mui/src/definitions.ts` returns 27; the README line reads "Components: Screen, Stack, Heading, TextField, NumberField, Checkbox, Select, Button.")** The 27 are Alert, Box, Breadcrumbs, Button, ButtonGroup, Card, Checkbox, DatePicker, Divider, FileUpload, Grid, Heading, Image, Link, NumberField, Progress, RadioGroup, RadioItem, Repeater, Screen, Select, Stack, Step, Switch, Text, TextArea, TextField. `docs/spec/03-components.md` is machine-checked against this same list by `test/docs.test.ts:451-469`, so the spec says 27 while the package README says 8. Failure scenario: the npm landing page for the reference kit advertises under a third of what ships — a reader concludes the kit has no Repeater, no RadioGroup and no DatePicker and either files an issue or picks another library. The guard that catches this drift reads `docs/spec/03-components.md` and never opens a `packages/*/README.md`.

## packages/kit-html/README.md
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `packages/kit-html/README.md:22` — "It ships the same eight component types as `@formsmith/kit-mui`" — it ships 27, and so does kit-mui. **(Verified: the same count over `packages/kit-html/src/definitions.ts` returns 27.)** `test/docs.test.ts:467-468` asserts the two kits implement exactly the same set, so the "same as kit-mui" half is right and only the number is wrong. Failure scenario: as above, plus it makes the "a document authored against one kit renders against the other" claim in the same sentence look far weaker than it is.
- Verified true, no finding: `npm install @formsmith/kit-html` needs nothing else — `htmlKit` and `htmlRendererKit` are both exported, and `import '@formsmith/kit-html/styles.css'` resolves through `exports["./styles.css"]` → `./dist/kit-html.css`, which `scripts/copy-assets.mjs:6` writes. `.fsh-screen` is the token scope, and both dark spellings exist. Note that CI never verifies this package's `dist` — see the `CLAUDE.md:66` finding.

## .claude/skills/write-tests/SKILL.md
- **[MEDIUM]** **[E5]** `.claude/skills/write-tests/SKILL.md:192-198` — Trap 12 describes the invariant-3 guard in the present tense as still holed, when it was rewritten. Evidence: L192-198 "`designer.css.test.ts` enforces invariant 3 by testing whether a selector reaches past `.fs-shell` or `.fs-canvas__surface`. … So `.fs-canvas__frame input { padding: 5px 8px }` … contains neither base string, and all four assertions stay green." The guard now works the other way round — `packages/designer/src/designer.css.test.ts:111-124` is a `CHROME_UNDER_CANVAS` allowlist and `:148-162` flags any rightmost compound with no `fs-` class under any `.fs-shell`/`.fs-canvas` scope — and `:218` is an assertion that `.fs-canvas__frame input` **is** flagged, with `:220` covering `.fs-canvas input` at any depth. Failure scenario: an AI contributor loads this skill, believes the repo's headline CSS guard is currently broken, and either re-does the fix or writes tests against a hole that no longer exists. Only the closing **Rule** (L204-206) still applies. (See the separate finding under `designer.css.test.ts` for the `:where()` hole that *is* currently open — a different spelling of the same class.)
- **[LOW]** **[E5]** `.claude/skills/write-tests/SKILL.md:240` — "Run `pnpm e2e` too. **65** of these checks are the only ones that see layout" — there are 68. **(Verified: counted 9+16+13+4+13+4+3+6 = 68 across `e2e/*.spec.ts`.)** That is the figure `README.md:71`, `CLAUDE.md:63` and `docs/ROADMAP.md:59` all carry and `test/docs.test.ts:85-105` enforces; the skill is outside that guard's document list, which is why it drifted. Failure scenario: minor on its own, but this is the "Before you open the PR" checklist an AI contributor follows literally, and the item immediately below it is "Check the counts".
- **[LOW]** **[E5]** `.claude/skills/write-tests/SKILL.md:178-181` — Trap 11's "keep it that way" instruction names a fixture that is not in one of the three files it names. Evidence: "`rows.test.ts`, `renderer.test.tsx` and `repeater.spec.ts` each now carry a repeater named `addressList` holding `addresses`, with a `street` field holding `line1`." `addressList` appears in `packages/core/src/rows.test.ts:528` and `packages/renderer/src/renderer.test.tsx:380` but nowhere in `e2e/`; `e2e/repeater.spec.ts:100-127` uses a repeater keyed `repeater` with `dataKey` renamed to `contacts`. The substance holds — the two names differ, so the branch is observable — only the fixture name is wrong.
- **[NOTE]** **[E2]** `.claude/skills/write-tests/SKILL.md:241-242` — "`pnpm test` and `pnpm e2e` totals appear in `README.md`, `CLAUDE.md` and `docs/ROADMAP.md`, and `test/docs.test.ts` compares them" is half the mechanism. `test/docs.test.ts` compares the documents with each other; the check against what actually ran is `test/count-reporter.ts:94-134`, wired in at `vitest.config.ts:41`. Failure scenario: a contributor updating a count runs `vitest run test/docs.test.ts`, sees green, and misses that the reporter deliberately skips itself on a filtered run.
- Traps 1-10 and 13 spot-check accurate against the current suites.

## Tasks for you

The report is advisory. Nothing was changed. These are the items where the call is yours, not mine.

1. **Decide whether the `validateAcross` fail-open needs a security note.** The packages are at
   `0.0.0` and unpublished, so there is no consumer to notify and nothing to rotate. If that is still
   true, this is an ordinary bug fix. If any of these has been published anywhere, it is a
   server-side validation bypass and wants a release note.
2. **Rule on the `keyProblem` question the fix depends on.** `toString`/`valueOf`/`hasOwnProperty`
   pass the key grammar today. Fixing `validateAcross` with `Object.create(null)` closes the fail-open;
   adding those names to `RESERVED_KEYS` closes the class. They are different decisions with different
   compatibility costs, and the second one breaks any existing document that uses such a key.
3. **Decide the unit `gap` carries**, in the kit contract rather than in either kit. Every fix below
   that is mechanical; the contract change is the one that needs your call, because it decides which
   kit's existing documents reflow.
4. **Decide whether `apps/playground/src/sample.ts:51-52` stays.** It names a competitor and asserts a
   defect in their demo, in a source file that ships in the public bundle. `CLAUDE.md` confines
   competitive material to `docs/prior-art`, and `README.md:125-128` publishes measured audit failures
   for a subject the same repo names. Both are policy calls, not bugs.

Everything else is ordinary remediation. I have not started any of it.

## Reconciliation with the 2026-08-30 first-pass review

I wrote everything above without opening `tmp/formsmith-review-2026-08-30.md`, then compared. The two
reviews are not duplicates and the difference between them is the most useful thing in this document.

**The first pass found 428 findings (3 CRITICAL / 57 HIGH / 168 MEDIUM / 121 LOW / 79 NOTE) over 97
source files and 20 documents. This pass found 542 over all 176 tracked files.** The larger number is
not a contradiction: this pass covered the whole tree including every test suite as its own slice, and
the remediation run added roughly 15,900 lines for it to review.

### The first pass's three CRITICALs are genuinely fixed

- **MUI `sx` CSS injection.** `safeStyleValue` now exists in `core/src/css.ts:133` and
  `test/kit-css-injection.test.tsx` guards it. Fixed. (This pass found one weakness *in that new guard*
  — its MUI half asserts against an empty stylesheet because emotion caches by content hash — but the
  product defect is closed.)
- **`ConditionEditor` discarding `scope: 'row'`.** The operand now carries the scope the choice implies.
  Fixed.
- **`documentProblems` never checking `translations`.** `tree.ts` now references `translations` seven
  times and the gate covers it. Fixed.

Nothing that the first pass rated CRITICAL survived. That is a real result and the remediation deserves
the credit for it.

### All four of this pass's CRITICALs are in code the remediation run introduced

This is the finding that matters most, and it is only visible by comparing the two reviews against the
git history:

| This pass's CRITICAL | Introduced by | What went wrong |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `validateAcross` fail-open | `4a1234f` added `Object.create(null)` to `validateForm` | The fix was applied to one of two twins. `git log -S validateAcross` on this branch returns **nothing** — the sibling was never opened. |
| Unbounded condition walker in `validateDocument` | `3e03293` added `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` | The bound went on `visit` and not on the `condition` walker nested inside it, so the guard exists, is tested, and does not cover the branch a hostile document uses. |
| Two kit-contract rules unfailable | `4bb62c4` added `NodeBoundary` | A correct product fix. Its fallback element emits `data-fs-key`/`data-fs-type`/`data-fs-scope` — which are exactly the three attributes the contract's `nodeProps` rules read, so those rules stopped being able to fail. Nobody re-checked the guards against the new fallback. |

A fourth in the same shape, one rung down: the first pass flagged `declarationsFor` at LOW for locating a
block by substring. Item 26 rewrote it *and* added a guard-of-the-guard test — and that new test never
calls `declarationsFor`. The fix introduced the defect the fix was for.

**The pattern is one thing, stated four ways: a fix that lands on one side of a symmetry, and a guard
that is not re-checked against the code the fix changed.** That is a process finding, not a code finding,
and it is the reason a second pass on a remediated branch is worth running at all.

### Themes the first pass raised that are still open

Several are open *because they were deliberately triaged* rather than missed — the run's own
`docs/build-out/found.md` hands them over by name, and I re-derived them independently:

- The `gap` unit divergence between the kits (this pass raises it against the contract, not the kits).
- `maxSize` on `FileUpload` refusing files invisibly, in both kits.
- `Button.variant` carrying two different vocabularies.
- The `e2e/harness.ts` localStorage read racing the designer's passive-effect write.
- Repeater rows being unreachable from the designer, so the row geometry has no browser-level test.

Two the first pass raised at LOW and which the remediation left, where I would now argue the severity up:
`designer.css:855`'s `revert` (a no-op, so the inheritance breach it claims to fix is open) and the
duplicate `.fs-tree__row` blocks, which the file's own comment says were eliminated.

### Things this pass found that the first pass did not, unrelated to the remediation

`forms.json.tmp` missing from `.gitignore`; `cspell.json` being 40 lines of configuration that no script
runs; `eslint .` never linting `aliases.ts`; `no-restricted-imports` having no `ImportExpression` visitor;
`rowScopeProblems` being documented as a public export and not exported; the `:where(.fs-shell) input`
hole in the CSS guard; and the four published READMEs that do not work as written. The first pass covered
97 files and these live mostly in the 79 it did not.
